    Let us examine a real case involving a consumer credit bureau to see how this obligation applies in practice. The case is Credit Bureau (Singapore) Pte Ltd [2018]. Credit bureaus produce credit reports detailing how creditworthy an individual is, assigning them a score or a non-scored risk grade. In this case, an individual had a bankruptcy application filed against him, but the creditor eventually withdrew it, meaning the individual was never actually declared bankrupt. However, when the credit bureau issued his credit report, they gave him an "HX" rating. According to their definitions, an HX rating means there is a "past or existing bankruptcy record" filed against the individual. The individual was highly annoyed because he was never bankrupt, and he demanded the rating be amended. The credit bureau refused, and the PDPC ultimately ruled that there was no breach of the Accuracy Obligation. The reasoning was that the HX rating simply indicates a bankruptcy writ or application was filed, which was a historical fact. Therefore, technically speaking, the HX rating was not inaccurate.

    I posed a question to the class: Could you make an argument that this still somehow breaches the Accuracy Obligation? A student suggested that while it is technically correct, it is highly misleading and incomplete. When a lender reads the HX rating, they assume the individual is a financial risk, but if the bureau provided the complete picture—that the claim was withdrawn and possibly lacked merit—the lender might view it differently. Another student brought up the currency of the data, likening it to a childhood cancer diagnosis that is completely resolved but still hangs over the person's record, putting them at an unfair disadvantage. While I agree with these sentiments, the credit bureau's defence is that they are merely providing the bare historical facts. It is ultimately up to the end-user—the lending bank—to decide the weighting of that event and investigate the context. The bureau fulfilled its narrow legal duty of ensuring the data record (that an application was filed) was factually true.

    让我们来看一个涉及消费者信用局的真实案例，看看这项义务在实践中是如何应用的。该案例是新加坡信用局有限公司（Credit Bureau (Singapore) Pte Ltd）[2018] 案。信用局制作信用报告，详细说明个人的信用程度，并为他们分配评分或无分数的风险等级。在这个案件中，有一名个人被提出了破产申请，但债权人最终撤回了申请，这意味着该个人实际上从未被宣布破产。然而，当信用局出具他的信用报告时，他们给了他一个“HX”评级。根据他们的定义，HX评级意味着该个人有“过去或现有的破产记录”被提交。这个人非常恼火，因为他从未破产过，他要求修改这个评级。信用局拒绝了，PDPC最终裁定没有违反准确性义务。理由是，HX评级仅仅表明曾有破产令或申请被提交，这是一个历史事实。因此，从技术上讲，HX评级并不是不准确的。

    我向全班提出了一个问题：你能否提出论点，证明这在某种程度上仍然违反了准确性义务？一位学生认为，虽然它在技术上是正确的，但它具有高度的误导性和不完整性。当贷款人看到HX评级时，他们会认为该个人存在财务风险，但如果信用局提供了全貌——即申请被撤回，且可能缺乏依据——贷款人可能会有不同的看法。另一位学生提到了数据的时效性，把它比作儿童时期诊断出的癌症，虽然已经完全治愈，但仍然挂在这个人的记录上，使他们处于不公平的劣势。虽然我同意这些观点，但信用局的辩护是，他们仅仅是在提供纯粹的历史事实。最终要由最终用户——即贷款银行——来决定该事件的权重并调查其背景。信用局履行了其狭义的法律责任，即确保数据记录（即曾有申请被提交）在事实上是真实的。

  - [20:50:32 - 20:59:25] **Case Study: LLMs in Performance Appraisals**

    Let me finish with one more case study regarding the Accuracy Obligation, focusing on artificial intelligence. Suppose a company called RST Inc integrates a Large Language Model (LLM) into its performance appraisal process. The LLM is instructed to ingest and review internal communications between the employee and their colleagues to produce personality scores. Our employee, Greg, is up for a promotion. The LLM reviews his chat history and gives him an 8 out of 10 for "Aggression." It flags statements Greg made, such as "I can't believe you've gone and done this!" or "You dirty scumbag." However, if any human had read those statements in their proper context, it would have been blatantly obvious that Greg was just joking around with his close teammates. Nevertheless, based on this high aggression score, Greg's boss, Kenny, denies him the promotion. Is this system compliant with the Accuracy Obligation?

    A student immediately pointed out that because a promotion is a high-stakes decision that significantly impacts the individual's life, the standard for what constitutes a "reasonable effort" to ensure accuracy is much higher. Simply relying on a zero-context algorithm for such a critical decision falls short of that standard. However, another student raised a counter-argument: the 8/10 score generated by the LLM is technically an "opinion," and as we discussed earlier, opinions are inherently subjective and cannot be objectively "accurate" or "inaccurate." This makes enforcing the Accuracy Obligation quite tricky. A very perceptive student then brought up the concept of "reliability of the data." Because the LLM processed the text entirely devoid of context, the observational data it relied upon was fundamentally unreliable or incomplete. My own legal argument would lean on this idea of completeness. The data used to calculate the aggression score was incomplete because it lacked vital contextual metadata (tone, relationship, history). Because the data was incomplete for such a high-stakes decision, the organisation failed its Accuracy Obligation. A reasonable effort in this scenario would have required a human review of the flagged statements or, at the very least, giving Greg a chance to clarify the context of his messages before denying the promotion.

    让我以最后一个关于准确性义务的案例研究来结束今天的课程，重点关注人工智能。假设一家名为RST Inc的公司将大语言模型（LLM）整合到其绩效评估流程中。该LLM受命提取并审查员工与其同事之间的内部通信，以生成性格评分。我们的员工Greg正在争取升职。LLM审查了他的聊天记录，并在“攻击性”方面给了他8分（满分10分）。它标记了Greg说过的话，比如“我不敢相信你竟然做了这种事！”或者“你这个肮脏的人渣”。然而，如果任何人类在适当的语境下读到这些话，就会非常明显地看出Greg只是在和亲密的队友开玩笑。尽管如此，基于这个高攻击性评分，Greg的老板Kenny拒绝了他的升职。这个系统符合准确性义务吗？

    一位学生立刻指出，因为升职是一个对个人生活有重大影响的高风险决定，所以构成确保准确性的“合理努力”的标准要高得多。对于如此关键的决定，仅仅依赖一个毫无语境的算法是达不到这个标准的。然而，另一位学生提出了反面论点：LLM生成的8/10分在技术上是一种“意见”，正如我们之前讨论的，意见本质上是主观的，不能客观上判断为“准确”或“不准确”。这使得执行准确性义务变得相当棘手。一位非常有洞察力的学生随后提出了“数据的可靠性”这个概念。因为LLM完全脱离了语境来处理文本，它所依赖的观察数据从根本上就是不可靠或不完整的。我个人的法律论点倾向于这种完整性的观点。用于计算攻击性评分的数据是不完整的，因为它缺乏至关重要的语境元数据（语气、关系、历史背景）。由于对于如此高风险的决定，数据是不完整的，该组织未能履行其准确性义务。在这种情况下，合理努力应该要求人工复查被标记的言论，或者至少在拒绝升职之前，给Greg一个机会来澄清他信息的背景。

  - [20:59:25 - 21:05:29] **Final Q&A: GenAI Accuracy and Workplace Privacy**

    We ended the session with some final questions regarding generative AI and privacy. A student asked how accuracy legislation will transform given that LLMs frequently hallucinate or produce inaccurate outputs. While the aggression score in our example was an opinion, I noted that you can absolutely prompt an LLM to generate strong factual statements (e.g., "Greg stole office supplies on this date"). If an organisation uses an LLM to generate and record factual statements, they are definitely bound by the Accuracy Obligation. The organisation must make a critical judgment call on how much they can trust the LLM's output for important decisions. It also raises the philosophical legal question of whether the neural weights inside an LLM actually constitute personal data in themselves.

    Another student brought up a very practical workplace privacy concern: "Can my employer take my normal, everyday chats with colleagues and feed them into an LLM for operations or appraisals without my consent?" The short answer under the PDPA is yes, they can, because there is a specific statutory exception to the consent obligation for the purpose of "managing employees." Employers do not need to seek explicit consent to process employee communications on company platforms. However, they are still strictly bound by the Purpose Limitation Obligation. You cannot take the exception too far. If the company takes deeply private, sensitive conversations—even on a work chat—and feeds them into an LLM for an unannounced appraisal purpose, that processing may go far beyond what a reasonable person would consider appropriate. Therefore, while it may not breach the consent obligation, feeding everything into an LLM without boundaries could very well breach the purpose limitation rules.

    我们在课程的最后回答了一些关于生成式人工智能和隐私的最终提问。一位学生问，鉴于LLM经常产生幻觉或不准确的输出，关于准确性的立法将如何演变。虽然我们例子中的攻击性评分是一种意见，但我指出，你绝对可以提示LLM生成强烈的陈述性事实（例如，“Greg在这一天偷了办公用品”）。如果组织使用LLM来生成和记录事实陈述，他们绝对受到准确性义务的约束。组织必须做出关键的判断：对于重要的决定，他们能在多大程度上信任LLM的输出。这也引发了一个哲学层面的法律问题：LLM内部的神经权重本身是否构成个人数据。

    另一位学生提出了一个非常实际的职场隐私担忧：“我的雇主能在未经我同意的情况下，拿我平时和同事的日常聊天记录，喂给LLM用于运营或绩效评估吗？”在PDPA下的简短回答是：是的，他们可以，因为对于“管理员工”的目的，同意义务有一个特定的法定例外。雇主不需要寻求明确的同意，就可以处理公司平台上的员工通信。然而，他们仍然受到“目的限制义务”（Purpose Limitation Obligation）的严格约束。你不能把这个例外用得太过分。如果公司获取了高度私人化、敏感的对话——即使是在工作聊天中——并将其喂给LLM用于未事先公布的评估目的，这种处理可能远远超出了通情达理的人认为适当的范围。因此，虽然它可能不违反同意义务，但毫无边界地将所有内容输入LLM，很可能会违反目的限制规则。
***

# Seminar 6 Protection obligation and security arrangements(claude)
## Week 6 Readings
- AGKCP at 17.1-17.5.
- GDPPIS (we will not be relying on this for this course, but it is useful background).
## Week 6 Learning Tips
Start every problem with one question: **"Were the arrangements reasonable in the circumstances?"** This week's cases are not a list of forbidden mistakes. They are illustrations of a single standard. Singtel (2020) suffered a breach and was cleared; Spear Security Force had no breach and was found liable. If you remember only one thing, remember that the **outcome does not decide the case; the adequacy of the precautions does.** Students who equate "data leaked" with "obligation breached" lose marks on exactly the cases designed to test this.

每道题都先问一个问题： **"在当时的情况下，这些安排合理吗？"** 本周的案例不是一份禁止事项清单，而是对同一个标准的示范。Singtel（2020）发生了泄露却被认定合规；Spear Security Force 没有泄露却被认定违规。如果只记住一件事，请记住：**决定案件的不是结果，而是预防措施是否充分。** 把"数据泄露了"等同于"违反义务"的同学，恰恰会在专门考这一点的案例上失分。

Do not treat security governance as a compliance checklist. The PDPC repeatedly rejected box-ticking. PCF had a Data Protection Notice but no policy for the work teachers actually did. NUS had an e-learning module that nobody was required to take. The Law Society had a rotation rule that nobody followed. Each time, the document existed but did not **reach the real risk**. When you analyse a scenario, trace the actual flow of data through people's daily tasks, and ask whether the measure would really have stopped *this* failure.

不要把安全治理当作合规清单。PDPC 一再否定走过场的做法。PCF 有数据保护声明，却没有针对老师实际工作的政策；NUS 有电子课程，却没人被要求去上；律师公会有密码轮换规定，却没人遵守。每一次，文件都存在，却没有**触及真正的风险**。分析情景时，要追踪数据在人们日常工作中的实际流向，并问自己：这项措施真的能阻止*这一次*的失误吗？

Learn the intermediary cases as a **set of contrasts**, not as isolated facts. Put Cellar Door, Singtel (2017), Fullerton and AIG side by side, and for each one write down what the controller did and what it failed to do. Then extract the variables that decide the outcome: credibility, red flags, the cost of supervision, and data sensitivity. In an exam, a new scenario will rarely match one case exactly, but it will almost always turn on those same variables. The AI vendor question shows how they transfer: a startup, an opaque model supply chain and limited visibility all push the expectation of oversight up or down.

把数据中介的案例当作 **一组对照** 来学，而不是孤立的事实。把 Cellar Door、Singtel（2017）、Fullerton 和 AIG 并排放在一起，逐一写下控制者做了什么、没做什么。然后提炼出决定结果的变量：信誉、危险信号、监督成本、数据敏感度。考试中的新情景很少与某个案例完全吻合，但几乎总取决于这些变量。AI 供应商那道题就展示了它们如何迁移：初创公司、不透明的模型供应链、有限的可见性，都会推高或拉低对监督的期望。

For the technical measures, build intuition by **thinking like the attacker**. Redraw the RedMart chain step by step and ask, at each step, which control would have broken it: least privilege, key management or MFA. Do the same for Lovebonito and the National Kidney Foundation: where would a second factor have stopped the attacker? This turns memorised rules into reasoning you can apply to a new system diagram. Remember too that a password can satisfy every formal rule and still be weak, as "Ch!zzle@2018" shows, because attackers guess patterns, not rules.

学习技术措施时，要通过**站在攻击者角度思考**来建立直觉。一步步重画 RedMart 的攻击链，在每一步问：哪项控制措施能切断它？最小权限、密钥管理，还是 MFA？对 Lovebonito 和全国肾脏基金会也这样做：第二个认证因素会在哪里挡住攻击者？这样，背下来的规则就变成了可以套用到新系统图上的推理。还要记住，一个密码可以满足所有形式规则却依然很弱，正如 "Ch!zzle@2018" 所示，因为攻击者猜的是模式，不是规则。

Pay close attention to the **scope of each rule**. The Lovebonito guidance makes MFA presumptively mandatory only for admin accounts holding sensitive or large volumes of data. Elsewhere, MFA depends on a broader risk assessment, which is why the Newmind discussion stayed an open question. Supervision likewise applies only to high-risk activities. Over-applying a strong rule is as much an error as ignoring it. When you write an answer, state the rule, state its boundaries, and then argue which side of the boundary your facts fall on.

要密切注意**每条规则的适用范围**。Lovebonito 的指引只对持有敏感数据或大量数据的管理员账户推定 MFA 为强制要求。其他情况下，MFA 取决于更宽泛的风险评估，这也是为什么 Newmind 那道题的讨论仍是开放问题。监督同样只适用于高风险活动。过度套用一条严格的规则，和忽视它一样是错误。写答案时，先说明规则，再说明它的边界，然后论证你的事实落在边界的哪一边。

Finally, practise the **incident-response structure** from the Newmind question: contain (disable accounts, reset credentials), diagnose (logs, individual-account traceability), and strengthen (policy enforcement, MFA where justified, training if the cause is internal). Rehearse it on your own invented scenarios, and keep your answer inside data protection analysis, because that is what the exam rewards. Ten minutes a week spent turning one case into a three-line "facts, failing, lesson" card will make these cases feel familiar by the end of the course.

最后，练习 Newmind 一题中的**事件响应结构**：控制（停用账户、重置凭证）、诊断（日志、独立账户的可追溯性）、加固（执行政策、在有理由时采用 MFA、若原因在内部则加强培训）。用你自己编的情景反复演练，并把答案限定在数据保护分析之内，因为考试奖励的正是这一点。每周花十分钟，把一个案例整理成"事实、缺陷、教训"三行卡片，到课程结束时，这些案例就会变得非常熟悉。
## Week 6 Summary
- **The Protection Obligation: Core Rule 保护义务：核心规则**
  - **Rule:** Organisations must make **reasonable security arrangements** to protect personal data in their **possession or control**. The goal is to prevent **unauthorised processing** (access, collection, use, disclosure, copying, modification, disposal) and the **loss of storage media**. In practice, it is the most litigated obligation.
  - **Three types of arrangements:** **Administrative** (policies, training, supervision), **physical** (locked storage, proper disposal), and **technical** (encryption, firewalls, account controls, authentication).
  - **Key test:** The question is whether the organisation took *reasonable* measures, not whether a breach occurred.
  - **No absolute guarantee:** *Singtel (2020)*. A vendor's faulty code change made an API expose the data of recycled-number users. There was a data breach but **no breach of the obligation**. Singtel had data protection clauses with the vendor, monitored the vendor's compliance, and ran properly scoped pre-launch checks.
  - **Breach without a data breach:** *Spear Security Force (2016)*. A condo visitor logbook was left open and unattended, and the guards ignored a resident's warning. This was a **breach** even with no evidence of exposure.
  - **规则：** 组织必须作出**合理的安全安排**，保护其**拥有或控制**的个人数据。目的是防止**未经授权的处理**（访问、收集、使用、披露、复制、修改、处置），以及防止**存储介质遗失**。实践中，这是案例最多的一项义务。
  - **三类安排：** **行政性**（政策、培训、监督）、**物理性**（上锁存放、妥善销毁）、**技术性**（加密、防火墙、账户控制、认证）。
  - **核心判断标准：** 问的是组织是否采取了*合理*措施，而不是有没有发生泄露。
  - **无需绝对保证：** *Singtel（2020）*。供应商的代码修改出错，导致 API 暴露了回收号码用户的数据。发生了数据泄露，但**不违反保护义务**。Singtel 与供应商签有数据保护条款，监督了供应商的合规情况，并做了范围恰当的上线前检查。
  - **没有泄露也可能违规：** *Spear Security Force（2016）*。公寓访客登记簿摊开无人看管，住户提醒后保安也没有处理。即使没有证据显示数据被他人获取，仍**构成违规**。
- **Administrative Measures: Policies, Training, Supervision 行政措施：政策、培训、监督**
  - **Specific security policies:** Policies must target the **actual work activities** of staff. *PAP Community Foundation (2019)*: a teacher posted an attendance list with NRIC numbers to a parents' WhatsApp group. There was no policy on parent communications. A general, external-facing **Data Protection Notice is not enough**.
  - **Written, not verbal:** *Terra Systems (2022)*. Temp agents shared a daily password over Zoom, and a rogue ex-employee used it to insert crude remarks. Verbal reminders not to share the password were **insufficient**; a written policy was needed. The shared password was a separate failing.
  - **Training:** *NUS (2017)*. Student leaders ran a Google Sheet of volunteer data whose sharing setting was changed to "anyone with the link". The e-learning module existed but was **not compulsory**. E-learning is acceptable, and formal classroom training is not required, but training must be **mandatory**.
  - **Supervision is only for high-risk activities:**
    - *iApps (2021)*: a code fix was deployed straight to production, exposing user data. There was no second-level check. Relying on staff diligence alone is **not a security measure**.
    - *Credit Counselling Singapore (2017)*: a mass email to debt-programme clients used "To" instead of "BCC", and replies made it worse. Email addresses were **sensitive in context** because they revealed indebtedness, so a second check was needed. A supervisor glance, a draft sent for approval, or a mail merge would do.
  - **Supervision rule of thumb:** A supervision step that fails as a careless one-off is usually still "enough". Repeated failures suggest the design is inadequate.
  - **具体安全政策：** 政策必须针对员工的**实际工作活动**。*PAP 社区基金会（2019）*：老师把含身份证号的出席名单发到家长 WhatsApp 群。没有关于与家长沟通的政策。面向外部的一般性**数据保护声明不够**。
  - **书面而非口头：** *Terra Systems（2022）*。临时员工通过 Zoom 分享每日密码，一名前员工利用它插入粗俗备注。口头提醒不要分享密码**不足够**，需要书面政策。共享密码本身是另一个缺陷。
  - **培训：** *NUS（2017）*。学生领袖管理的志愿者数据 Google 表格，共享设置被改为"任何拿到链接的人"。电子课程存在但**并非必修**。电子学习可以接受，也不要求正式课堂培训，但培训必须是**必修的**。
  - **监督仅针对高风险活动：**
    - *iApps（2021）*：代码修复被直接部署到生产环境，暴露了用户数据，且没有二级检查。只依靠员工尽责**不算安全措施**。
    - *新加坡信贷咨询中心（2017）*：群发给债务计划客户的邮件用了"收件人"而非"密送"，回复全部又扩大了泄露。邮箱地址**在此情境下属于敏感信息**，因为它暴露了负债状况，所以需要二次检查。主管看一眼、以草稿提交审批、或使用邮件合并都可以。
  - **监督的经验法则：** 设置了监督但偶发失误，通常仍算"已尽责"；反复出错则说明监督设计本身不足。
- **Managing Data Intermediaries 管理数据中介**
  - **Both parties are bound:** The protection obligation applies to the **controller** and the **intermediary** alike. The controller holds **primary** responsibility: it sets the measures, writes them into the contract, and follows up with checks. The intermediary holds **direct** responsibility: it executes the agreed measures.
  - **Both in breach:** *The Cellar Door (2016)*. GIW hosted the website and customer database, and the data appeared on Pastebin. Neither party performed its role.
  - **Controller compliant, intermediary in breach:** *Singtel (2017)*. Tech Mahindra ran a database script missing its **WHERE clause**, affecting up to 2.78 million ONEPASS accounts. Singtel had a contract, required training, imposed a dev-environment-first SOP, ran on-site reviews and did its own penetration testing, so it was **not in breach**.
  - **Oversight required:** *Fullerton Healthcare (2023)*. Agape used prison inmates and downloaded the whole database to an online drive, and the data was later sold on the dark web. Fullerton's due diligence and contract were **not enough**. Knowing about the inmates, it should simply have **asked how access was given**.
  - **Oversight not required:** *AIG (2019)*. Toppan inserted the wrong reply envelopes. Given Toppan's **credibility and reputation**, and because enveloping was a minor task, supervising it would be **micromanagement**.
  - **Distinguishing factors (Fullerton vs AIG):**
    - the intermediary's credibility;
    - whether there were known red flags (the inmates);
    - the **cost of supervision** (one question versus 24/7 watching);
    - the **sensitivity of the data** (health and financial versus motor insurance).
  - **Difficulties of supervision:**
    - the expertise paradox: you hire them for expertise you lack;
    - the cost of dedicating staff;
    - lack of visibility, especially at the software or AI level;
    - large or foreign providers that won't negotiate terms;
    - sub-processors (generally, you answer for your own intermediary).
  - **AI vendor application (VX / Quotta):**
    - Write policies on **which data the agent may pull** (minimisation) and which queries are allowed.
    - Put a **human in the loop** to check AI responses before they go out, and train staff on all of this.
    - Confirm the intermediary relationship and contract on transfer security, retention and deletion.
    - Ask **"where exactly is the AI?"** and flow obligations down to any third-party model provider. Expectations are higher for a startup, but visibility into a US vendor is limited.
  - **双方均受约束：** 保护义务同时适用于**控制者**和**中介**。控制者负**首要**责任：制定措施、写入合同、并跟进检查。中介负**直接**责任：执行所约定的措施。
  - **双方均违规：** *The Cellar Door（2016）*。GIW 托管网站和客户数据库，数据后来出现在 Pastebin 上。双方都没有履行各自职责。
  - **控制者合规、中介违规：** *Singtel（2017）*。Tech Mahindra 执行的数据库脚本漏写 **WHERE 子句**，影响多达 278 万个 ONEPASS 账户。Singtel 有合同、要求培训、规定先在开发环境操作的 SOP、做现场审查并自行渗透测试，因此**不违规**。
  - **需要监督：** *Fullerton Healthcare（2023）*。Agape 雇用囚犯，并把整个数据库下载到在线云盘，数据后来在暗网出售。Fullerton 做了尽职调查、签了合同，但**不够**。既然知道有囚犯参与，它本应简单**问一句访问是如何提供的**。
  - **无需监督：** *AIG（2019）*。Toppan 放错了回邮信封。鉴于 Toppan 的**信誉与声誉**，且装信封只是次要环节，去监督就成了**微观管理**。
  - **区分因素（Fullerton 与 AIG）：**
    - 中介的信誉；
    - 是否有已知的危险信号（囚犯）；
    - **监督成本**（问一个问题，还是全天候盯守）；
    - **数据敏感度**（健康与财务信息，还是汽车保险）。
  - **监督的困难：**
    - 专业悖论：你聘请对方正是因为自己缺乏这种专业能力；
    - 派专人监督的成本；
    - 缺乏可见性，尤其在软件或 AI 层面；
    - 大型或海外服务商不愿谈判条款；
    - 次级处理方（一般而言，你只对自己的中介负责）。
  - **AI 供应商应用（VX / Quotta）：**
    - 制定政策，规定**智能体可提取哪些数据**（数据最小化）以及允许哪些查询。
    - 设置**人在回路**，在 AI 回复发出前进行检查，并就上述内容培训员工。
    - 确认中介关系，并在合同中约定传输安全、数据保留与删除。
    - 追问**"AI 到底在哪里？"**，把义务向下传递给第三方模型提供商。初创公司意味着更高的监督期望，但对美国供应商的可见性有限。
- **Core Technical Measures and Account Management 核心技术措施与账户管理**
  - **Core three:** **Firewalls, security reviews (penetration testing), and patching**. *Genki Sushi (2019)*: a ransomware attack hit a payroll database. Genki had no firewall at first, and later one that was misconfigured. It did no periodic penetration testing and did not patch regularly.
  - **Remove unneeded accounts:** *Ascentis (2023)*. A departed Kyanon subcontractor's account was kept and shared by others, and an attacker later used it to extract Starbucks rewards data. Organisations must **review accounts regularly** and remove stale ones.
  - **No shared accounts:** *Orchard Turn (2017)*. One admin account was shared by four users, and an attacker used the EDM server to send phishing emails to ION loyalty members. Shared accounts destroy **accountability and traceability**.
  - **Least privilege:** *RedMart (2022)*. The attack chain was: compromised GitHub account → Chef key in the repository → AWS environment → Hubot key → cloud database → exfiltration. Too many accounts could reach the keys.
  - **核心三项：** **防火墙、安全审查（渗透测试）、打补丁**。*元气寿司（2019）*：薪资数据库遭勒索软件攻击。起初没有防火墙，后来装了但配置错误；没有定期渗透测试，也没有定期打补丁。
  - **删除不需要的账户：** *Ascentis（2023）*。离职的 Kyanon 分包员工账户被保留并由他人共用，攻击者后来借此窃取星巴克会员数据。组织必须**定期审查账户**并删除闲置账户。
  - **禁止共享账户：** *Orchard Turn（2017）*。四人共用一个管理员账户，攻击者利用 EDM 服务器向 ION 会员发送钓鱼邮件。共享账户会破坏**问责和可追溯性**。
  - **最小权限：** *RedMart（2022）*。攻击链为：被盗的 GitHub 账户 → 仓库中的 Chef 密钥 → AWS 环境 → Hubot 密钥 → 云数据库 → 数据外泄。能接触这些密钥的账户过多。
- **Secure Authentication: Passwords and MFA 安全认证：密码与多因素认证**
  - **Definitions:** **Authentication** means checking that a person is who they claim to be. **Secure authentication** is authentication that cannot easily be cheated.
  - **Three factor types:**
    - something you **know**: password, PIN, security answer (an NRIC number is a poor factor because others know it);
    - something you **have**: card, phone, OTP device, staff pass;
    - something you **are**: fingerprint, face, iris.
  - **Edge cases:** A CAPTCHA proves you are human, not *which* human, so it is only loose authentication. An API key is arguably something you know.
  - **Password management policy** has two parts:
    - **Strength:** mixed character types; at least 12 characters; no personal or organisation words, dictionary words or common phrases; avoid predictable patterns (capitalised first letter, number at the end, symbol swaps).
    - **Hygiene:** a unique password per account; no sharing or writing down; no login on unsecured Wi-Fi; no auto-login; change it if you suspect compromise. Periodic change is debated, because people make tiny predictable edits, but the PDPC still recommends it.
  - **Weak-password cases:**
    - *Chizzle (2020)*: "Ch!zzle@2018" was compliant in form but brute-forceable; the database was wiped and held for ransom.
    - *Law Society (2023)*: "Welcome2020lawsoc" was weak and had been used for more than 3 months despite the 3-month rotation rule. Having a policy is not enough; you must **enforce** it.
  - **MFA expected:** *National Kidney Foundation (2021)*. An employee was phished, and his webmail held health, financial and marital data. Remote access plus sensitive data meant **2FA should have been considered**; it would have stopped the attacker.
  - **MFA as a baseline:** *Lovebonito (2022)*. "ilovebonito88" was guessable, and the checkout page was skimmed via the Magento account. Built-in 2FA was not enabled, but the PDPC did not count this against them this time. Its guidance going forward:
    - (1) **Admin accounts** holding sensitive data or **large volumes** of data must use MFA; not using it is presumptively a breach unless the organisation shows it acted reasonably or MFA was disproportionate.
    - (2) **Remote access** by privileged accounts raises the expectation further.
    - (3) Not switching on **out-of-the-box MFA** is an **aggravating factor**.
  - **Incident application (Newmind / POLLY):**
    - Diagnose with logs first; collusion is possible and MFA alone won't fix it.
    - Disable the ex-tutor's account and force password resets.
    - Ensure **individual tutor accounts** for traceability.
    - Weigh MFA: tutors have remote access and direct contact with minors. In an exam, stay within **data protection analysis**, not operations or PR.
  - **定义：** **认证**是核实一个人是否是其所声称的人。**安全认证**是不易被欺骗的认证。
  - **三类因素：**
    - 你**知道**的：密码、PIN、安全问题答案（身份证号是差的因素，因为别人也知道）；
    - 你**拥有**的：卡、手机、OTP 设备、员工卡；
    - 你**本身**的：指纹、人脸、虹膜。
  - **边缘情况：** 验证码只证明你是人，不证明你是*哪个*人，只是宽泛意义的认证。API 密钥可视为你知道的东西。
  - **密码管理政策**分两部分：
    - **强度：** 混合字符类型；至少 12 位；不用与个人或组织相关的词、字典词或常见短语；避免可预测模式（首字母大写、末尾加数字、符号替换）。
    - **卫生：** 每个账户用不同密码；不分享、不写下；不在不安全 Wi-Fi 登录；不开自动登录；怀疑泄露就改。定期更换有争议，因为人们只会做微小、可预测的修改，但 PDPC 目前仍推荐。
  - **弱密码案例：**
    - *Chizzle（2020）*："Ch!zzle@2018" 形式合规但可暴力破解，数据库被删并遭勒索。
    - *律师公会（2023）*："Welcome2020lawsoc" 是弱密码，且尽管规定三个月轮换，仍使用超过三个月。有政策还不够，必须**执行**。
  - **应考虑 MFA：** *全国肾脏基金会（2021）*。员工被钓鱼，其网页邮箱含健康、财务、婚姻数据。远程访问加敏感数据，意味着**应考虑 2FA**，而 2FA 本可挡住攻击者。
  - **MFA 作为基线：** *Lovebonito（2022）*。"ilovebonito88" 容易猜，攻击者经 Magento 账户篡改了结账页面。自带的 2FA 没有开启，但 PDPC 这次未将其计入违规。今后的指引：
    - (1) 持有敏感数据或**大量**数据的**管理员账户**必须用 MFA；不用即推定违规，除非组织证明其做法合理或 MFA 不成比例。
    - (2) 特权账户**远程访问**时期望更高。
    - (3) **自带 MFA 却不开启**是**加重因素**。
  - **事件应用（Newmind / POLLY）：**
    - 先用日志诊断原因；可能存在串通，单靠 MFA 解决不了。
    - 停用前导师账户，并强制重置密码。
    - 确保**每位导师有独立账户**，以便追溯。
    - 权衡 MFA：导师可远程访问，且直接接触未成年人。考试时只做**数据保护分析**，不涉及运营或公关。
## Week 6 Lecture
  - [18:35:57–18:38:42] **The Protection Obligation and the Three Types of Security Arrangements**

    In practice, this obligation is the most significant of the ten or so obligations we're looking at. So, what is the Protection Obligation? You can see it in one paragraph. It says that you have to make reasonable security arrangements to protect personal data in your possession or under your control. The reason you're doing this is to prevent any unauthorised processing of the personal data, or to prevent the loss of any storage media on which the personal data is stored. So basically, you're expected to take reasonable steps to make sure that no one is able to access your personal data, destroy it, extract it, and so on, to the extent that this is unauthorised. To put it a different way, this is basically your data security obligation.

    The key phrase for us is "reasonable security arrangements". That is what organisations are expected to put in place. In terms of the types of security arrangements, the PDPC has said there are three main categories: administrative, physical, and technical. Administrative arrangements are things like policies and training. Basically, if you're using words, such as training, to get your staff to be compliant, that is administrative. Next, physical security arrangements involve actual physical barriers to protect the personal data, for example a locked storage cabinet, or properly disposing of your personal data. The last category is technical security arrangements, and for this course I think these will be among the more important types. Technical security arrangements are those you implement for your IT systems, things like encryption, firewalls, and so on. So when you're thinking about what security arrangements to put in place, I recommend you consider all three.

    在实践中，这项义务是我们所讨论的大约十项义务中最重要的一项。那么，什么是保护义务？一段话就能看清楚。它规定，你必须作出合理的安全安排，以保护你所拥有或控制的个人数据。这样做的目的是防止对个人数据进行任何未经授权的处理，或防止存储个人数据的任何存储介质遗失。所以基本上，你需要采取合理措施，确保没有人能够访问、销毁或提取你的个人数据等等，只要这些行为是未经授权的。换一种说法，这基本上就是你的数据安全义务。

    对我们来说，关键词是"合理的安全安排"，这正是组织被期望落实的。至于安全安排的类型，PDPC 指出主要有三类：行政性、物理性和技术性。行政性安排是指政策、培训这类东西。基本上，如果你是通过文字、比如通过培训，让员工做到合规，那就属于行政性安排。其次，物理性安全安排涉及用实际的物理屏障来保护个人数据，例如上锁的储物柜，或者妥善销毁个人数据。最后一类是技术性安全安排，我认为对于这门课来说，这会是比较重要的类型之一。技术性安全安排是指你为 IT 系统实施的措施，比如加密、防火墙等等。所以，当你思考应该采取哪些安全安排时，我建议你把这三类都考虑进去。

  - [18:38:42–18:42:01] **No Absolute Guarantee: Singapore Telecommunications (2020)**

    So how does the Protection Obligation actually work? If you look at the cases, the vast majority of cases involving this obligation start off because of some sort of incident. A data breach happens, someone's personal data gets leaked on the internet, and then it gets reported. That's how it normally happens. But what the Protection Obligation says is that you are actually not required to absolutely guarantee the security of your personal data, because that is of course impossible. An absolute security guarantee is not possible to achieve. What this means is that even if an organisation happens to have suffered a data breach, that is not by itself a breach of the Protection Obligation. Just because you got hacked by some sophisticated hacker and got a bit unlucky does not necessarily mean you have breached it. You breach the Protection Obligation only if you fail to take reasonable security arrangements.

    The case illustrating this point is the Singapore Telecommunications case. You probably know Singtel is a telco. What happened was that it launched a mobile app to allow its customers to manage their services. Normally, the app would communicate with the telco's servers through an API, which would only retrieve the information for each customer. So if you're looking at the app using your account, you should only see your own numbers. Singtel engaged an IT vendor who was supposed to execute a certain code change, but did so incorrectly. Because of this incorrect code change, the API started to retrieve both the active and the recycled numbers associated with each customer. So you could see the details not only of your own number, but also of any number you previously had, and the people now associated with those numbers. As a result, the personal data of the current users of recycled numbers was revealed to the customers. Technically this is a data breach, since personal data was exposed. But the PDPC said there was no breach of the Protection Obligation, because Singtel had in fact put in place enough security. For example, they imposed data protection requirements on the vendor, they monitored the vendor's compliance, and they did pre-launch checks, and so on. Those checks just didn't manage to catch this particular issue.

    那么保护义务在实践中是如何运作的？如果你看这些案例，涉及这项义务的绝大多数案例都是由某种事件引发的：发生了数据泄露，某人的个人数据在网上被泄露，然后被举报。通常就是这样发生的。但保护义务规定的是，你其实并不需要绝对保证个人数据的安全，因为这当然是不可能的，绝对的安全保证是无法实现的。这意味着，即使一个组织碰巧遭遇了数据泄露，这本身并不构成违反保护义务。仅仅因为你被某个高明的黑客攻击、运气不太好，并不一定意味着你违反了义务。只有当你未能采取合理的安全安排时，才构成违反保护义务。

    说明这一点的案例是新加坡电信（Singapore Telecommunications）案。你们大概知道 Singtel 是一家电信公司。事情是这样的：它推出了一款手机应用，让客户管理自己的服务。正常情况下，这个应用通过 API 与电信公司的服务器通信，而 API 只会检索每个客户自己的信息。所以如果你用自己的账户查看应用，你应该只能看到自己的号码。Singtel 聘请了一家 IT 供应商执行某项代码修改，但供应商改错了。由于这次错误的代码修改，API 开始检索与每位客户相关联的在用号码和回收号码。于是你不仅能看到自己当前号码的信息，还能看到你以前用过的任何号码，以及现在使用这些号码的人的信息。结果，回收号码现用户的个人数据被暴露给了这些客户。从技术上讲，这是一次数据泄露，因为个人数据被暴露了。但 PDPC 认为并没有违反保护义务，因为 Singtel 确实落实了足够的安全措施。例如，它对供应商施加了数据保护要求，监督了供应商的合规情况，也做了上线前检查等等。只是这些检查没能发现这个特定的问题。

  - [18:42:13–18:43:53] **Breach Without a Data Breach: Spear Security Force (2016)**

    But the flip side is also true. Just because you got lucky and didn't suffer a data breach doesn't mean you haven't breached the Protection Obligation. Again, the key thing is: did you put in place reasonable security arrangements? One case that illustrates this is Spear Security Force, which involves a security company stationed at a condominium. The organisation had the usual security guard post in front of the condominium, and it had the usual visitor logbook. When you visit a condo, you may wonder why they always have this logbook for you to fill in your details. This logbook contained the names, mobile numbers, and so on, of the visitors. One of the residents said that this logbook was basically left unguarded, open on the table at the security post. Even though he flagged this to the organisation, they didn't take action to resolve the problem. So the PDPC said this is obviously a breach of the Protection Obligation, because you need to actually find some way to secure the book so that no one can take it and get the personal data. The PDPC found a breach even though it didn't find any evidence that anyone had actually taken any data from the book. So: no data breach, but still a breach of the Protection Obligation.

    但反过来也成立。仅仅因为你运气好、没有遭遇数据泄露，并不意味着你没有违反保护义务。同样，关键在于：你是否落实了合理的安全安排？说明这一点的一个案例是 Spear Security Force 案，涉及一家驻守在某公寓的保安公司。该组织在公寓门前设有常见的保安亭，也有常见的访客登记簿。你们去公寓拜访别人时，可能会好奇为什么他们总是有这本登记簿让你填写个人信息。这本登记簿记录了访客的姓名、手机号码等。一位住户投诉说，这本登记簿基本上无人看管，摊开放在保安亭的桌子上。尽管他向该组织反映过，但他们并没有采取行动解决问题。所以 PDPC 认为这显然违反了保护义务，因为你需要想办法把登记簿保管好，让别人拿不到它、获取不到其中的个人数据。PDPC 认定存在违规，尽管它没有发现任何证据表明真有人从登记簿里拿走了数据。所以：没有数据泄露，但仍然违反了保护义务。

  - [18:43:53–18:44:29] **Roadmap: General Security Measures**

    So what I'm going to do is take you through some of the more general security measures that most organisations would be expected to have. I'll just focus on those that are more commonly applicable. If you look at the cases, there are a bunch of other, more specific measures, but we won't look at those in this course. Okay, any questions so far before I proceed?

    接下来，我会带大家看一些比较通用的安全措施，也就是大多数组织都应当具备的措施。我只会聚焦那些比较常见、普遍适用的。如果你去看案例，还有很多更具体的措施，但这门课不会涉及那些。好，在我继续之前，到目前为止有什么问题吗？

  - [18:44:39–18:47:45] **Security Policies: PAP Community Foundation (2019)**

    Right, so let's look at the first arrangement, which is security policies. This is an example of an administrative security arrangement. What are you expected to do? You are expected to implement specific security policies to guide your staff on how to handle personal data in their work activities. The key word here is "specific". Meaning, you actually think about what your staff are doing, and build in security policies to address any risks that might emerge.

    For example, there's the case of the PAP Community Foundation, or PCF. PCF operates a number of preschools and childcare centres in Singapore. One of these schools organised a school trip for the kids. A few days before the trip, the teacher organising it decided to send a photograph of the attendance list to the WhatsApp group chat with the parents, I guess to share who was going on the trip. But this list contained the personal data of the fifteen students, including the contact numbers and NRIC numbers of the five parents who were also coming on the trip. A parent then complained to the PDPC that this was an unauthorised disclosure of personal data.

    The PDPC said this was a breach of the Protection Obligation. Why? Because when the PDPC looked at PCF's policies, there was no specific policy dealing with the handling of personal data in the context of parental communication. There was nothing to guide teachers on how to communicate with parents in a data-protection-compliant way. The PDPC said this was a necessary policy because, as you would expect, PCF's staff communicate with parents quite regularly, so this is something the organisation should have considered. During the investigation, PCF responded by saying, look, we have this Data Protection Notice. But the PDPC said that kind of notice, which is usually an external-facing notice for public consumption, is not enough, because all it tells you is how the organisation generally handles personal data. It doesn't deal with the specific situation of parental communication. So this highlights the need for specific security policies.

    好，我们来看第一项安排，也就是安全政策。这是行政性安全安排的一个例子。你需要做什么？你需要实施具体的安全政策，指导员工在工作中如何处理个人数据。这里的关键词是"具体"。也就是说，你要真正思考员工在做什么，并制定相应的安全政策来应对可能出现的风险。

    举个例子，PAP 社区基金会（PCF）案。PCF 在新加坡经营多所幼儿园和托儿中心。其中一所学校为孩子们组织了一次校外游。出行前几天，负责组织的老师决定把出席名单拍照发到与家长的 WhatsApp 群里，我想是为了告诉大家谁会参加。但这份名单包含了十五名学生的个人数据，其中还有五位同行家长的联系电话和身份证（NRIC）号码。随后有家长向 PDPC 投诉，称这是未经授权披露个人数据。

    PDPC 认定这违反了保护义务。为什么？因为 PDPC 审查 PCF 的政策时发现，没有任何具体政策涉及在与家长沟通时如何处理个人数据，没有任何东西指导老师如何以符合数据保护要求的方式与家长沟通。PDPC 认为这是一项必要的政策，因为正如你所预料的，PCF 的员工与家长沟通相当频繁，所以这是该组织本应考虑到的。在调查过程中，PCF 回应说，我们有数据保护声明啊。但 PDPC 说，这种声明通常是面向外部、供公众阅读的，并不足够，因为它只告诉你组织大体上如何处理个人数据，并没有处理与家长沟通这一具体情境。所以这个案例凸显了制定具体安全政策的必要性。

  - [18:47:59–18:50:59] **Written Policies, Not Verbal Reminders: Terra Systems (2022)**

    So what is the standard when it comes to security policies? The answer is that, in general, you need to have it in writing. Informal practices or verbal reminders are not going to be good enough in most cases. The reason is that even if you do give these verbal reminders, it's hard for you to establish that you actually have this security arrangement, and it's also hard for your staff to figure out what exactly the policy is when they check.

    An interesting case that highlights this point is Terra Systems. Terra Systems provides IT services, and it was awarded a government contract to provide call centre services to check whether people were adhering to their Stay-Home Notices. This was during the COVID lockdown period, when people were given Stay-Home Notices and required to stay at home for, I think, fourteen days. It's a form of quarantine. So there was a need for a call centre to call people to check whether they were actually at home. To run this call centre, Terra Systems created an online portal containing the personal data of everyone serving a Stay-Home Notice, with the information provided by the immigration authority, ICA. For the employees to work, they were given access to this portal, and most of the staff were temp staff. These temp staff were given limited access, specifically through a common daily password, which was shared during a daily morning Zoom meeting. So every morning there's a meeting with all these temp staff, and you share the password for the day.

    What happened was that there was a rogue ex-employee who continued to be able to obtain these common daily passwords, maybe because some insider told him what the password was, and he then inserted crude comments into the remarks section of a few cases. The PDPC said there was a breach of the Protection Obligation, and pointed out one particular failing: the agents were told not to share the password, but only verbally. There was no written policy in place, and the PDPC said this was not enough.

    Okay, any questions regarding specific policies?

    那么，安全政策的标准是什么？答案是，一般来说，你需要有书面政策。在大多数情况下，非正式做法或口头提醒是不够的。原因在于，即使你确实给了口头提醒，你也很难证明你确实落实了这项安全安排；员工在需要查阅时，也很难弄清楚政策到底是什么。

    一个很有意思的案例是 Terra Systems 案。Terra Systems 提供 IT 服务，它获得了一份政府合同，提供呼叫中心服务，核查人们是否遵守居家通知（Stay-Home Notice）。那是新冠封锁期间，人们收到居家通知，被要求在家待大约十四天，我记得是这样，算是一种隔离。所以需要一个呼叫中心打电话给这些人，核查他们是否真的在家。为了运营这个呼叫中心，Terra Systems 建立了一个在线门户，里面有所有正在执行居家通知的人员的个人数据，信息由移民与关卡局（ICA）提供。员工为了工作获得了该门户的访问权限，而大多数员工是临时工。这些临时工获得的是有限访问权限，具体来说是通过一个公共的每日密码，这个密码在每天早上的 Zoom 会议上分享。所以每天早上开会，把当天的密码告诉所有临时工。

    结果发生的事情是，有一名心怀不轨的前员工仍然能够拿到这些公共每日密码，可能是因为有内部人员告诉了他密码，然后他在几个案例的备注栏里插入了粗俗的评论。PDPC 认定违反了保护义务，并指出了一个具体的缺陷：员工被告知不得分享密码，但只是口头告知，没有任何书面政策，PDPC 认为这是不够的。

    好，关于具体政策有什么问题吗？

  - [18:51:14–18:51:52] **Student Comment: Shared Passwords**

    A student comments that, generally, it's good IT practice not to have any account sharing or shared passwords. One reason is that if there's a data breach, it becomes very difficult to trace. In this case, it was found out who was involved, but otherwise it could have been any of those staff, and it would be really difficult to track them down.

    Yes, exactly. Actually, this case involved quite a number of different failings, one of which is, as you mentioned, the fact that there was a shared password. We'll discuss this a bit later on when we talk about account management, which is another form of security practice. Any other questions?

    一位同学评论说，一般来说，良好的 IT 做法是不要共享账户或密码。原因之一是，如果发生数据泄露，会非常难以追查。在这个案例中，最后查出了是谁干的，但否则的话，可能是这些员工中的任何一个，要追查他们会非常困难。

    是的，完全正确。实际上，这个案例涉及相当多不同的缺陷，其中之一就是你提到的，存在共享密码。我们稍后讲账户管理时会讨论这一点，那是另一种安全措施。还有其他问题吗？

  - [18:52:03–18:55:08] **Staff Training: National University of Singapore (2017)**

    Okay, the next security arrangement, which actually follows from the first one, is staff training. Staff training is what you need to do after you have the security policy. You write out a policy, but you need to get your staff to follow it, so you need to train them on those policies.

    The usual case used to illustrate this point is actually the NUS case. We have a residential college called the College of Alice and Peter Tan, or CAPT. Every year they organise a freshman orientation camp to welcome the new students. NUS designated student leaders to organise this camp, and having been picked, the leaders went out to recruit a bunch of student volunteers. As usual, the student leaders created an online form using Google Forms and asked all the volunteers to fill in their details to sign up to help organise the camp. These details were all stored in a Google Spreadsheet, and the intention was that only the student leaders should have access to it. To do this, the student leaders selected the "share with specific people" setting, which allows the spreadsheet to be shared only with specific Google accounts. But somehow, some unknown party managed to change this setting to "share using a link", which means anyone with the URL could access the spreadsheet. This of course is a potential leakage.

    The PDPC investigated and said there was a breach of the Protection Obligation. One of the key failings by NUS was that it didn't really have any data protection training for the student leaders. NUS could point to a particular e-learning programme, but it did not make it compulsory. So naturally, none of the student leaders did the e-learning, even though the student leaders here were actually handling the personal data. The PDPC said that in this case, training is a necessary security measure. That being said, the PDPC also said that you don't actually need formal classroom training. E-learning is fine, but you have to make it compulsory.

    好，下一项安全安排其实是从第一项延伸出来的，就是员工培训。员工培训是你在制定安全政策之后需要做的事。你写好了政策，但你需要让员工遵守，所以你需要就这些政策培训他们。

    用来说明这一点的常见案例其实就是 NUS 案。我们有一所住宿学院叫陈振传学院（College of Alice and Peter Tan，CAPT）。他们每年都会举办新生迎新营欢迎新生。NUS 指定了学生领袖来组织迎新营，被选中之后，这些领袖去招募了一批学生志愿者。和往常一样，学生领袖用 Google Forms 建了一份在线表格，让所有志愿者填写个人信息报名参与组织迎新营。这些信息全部存在一份 Google 电子表格里，本意是只有学生领袖才能访问。为此，学生领袖选择了"与特定人员共享"的设置，这样表格只能与特定的 Google 账户共享。但不知怎么的，某个不明人士把这个设置改成了"通过链接共享"，这意味着任何拿到网址的人都能访问这份表格。这当然就是潜在的泄露。

    PDPC 进行了调查，认定违反了保护义务。NUS 的关键缺陷之一是，它实际上没有为学生领袖提供任何数据保护培训。NUS 可以拿出某个电子学习课程，但并没有把它设为必修。所以很自然地，没有一个学生领袖去上这门电子课程，尽管这些学生领袖实际上在处理个人数据。PDPC 认为，在这种情况下，培训是一项必要的安全措施。话虽如此，PDPC 也说，你其实不需要正式的课堂培训。电子学习是可以的，但你必须把它设为必修。

  - [18:55:08–18:56:27] **Q&A: Penalties and How Cases Reach the PDPC**

    A student asks what the penalty or punishment is for something like this.

    The penalty for this, I'd have to think. I don't remember offhand. For this case, I'm going to guess it was either a warning or a fairly low penalty. This is one of the earlier cases, where the PDPC intended to issue only light penalties, because people were just starting up their data protection compliance.

    Another student asks whether this means someone actually complained to the PDPC.

    Most cases involve some sort of complaint. Usually the PDPC receives a complaint and then investigates. Unless it's some major data breach that becomes more or less a public matter, like the SingHealth incident, these smaller cases tend to reach the PDPC through some sort of complaint. So if you can avoid getting a complaint, then I guess you'll be safe for the moment.

    Okay, so that's security policies and staff training.

    一位同学问，这种情况的处罚是什么。

    这个案子的处罚，我得想一想，一时记不起来。我猜这个案子要么是警告，要么是比较轻的罚款。这是较早期的案例之一，那时 PDPC 有意只给予较轻的处罚，因为大家才刚开始做数据保护合规。

    另一位同学问，这是否意味着有人真的向 PDPC 投诉了。

    大多数案例都涉及某种投诉。通常 PDPC 收到投诉后才会展开调查。除非是那种几乎成为公共事件的重大数据泄露，比如新保集团（SingHealth）事件，否则这些较小的案件往往是通过某种投诉到达 PDPC 的。所以如果你能避免被投诉，那我想你暂时是安全的。

    好，这就是安全政策和员工培训。

  - [18:56:27–18:59:51] **Staff Supervision for High-Risk Activities: iApps (2021)**

    The last major administrative measure is supervision. Unlike the other two, supervision is not always required. It's generally only required when the staff are engaging in so-called high-risk activities, meaning activities where there is a high risk that some unauthorised processing of personal data might happen.

    The case illustrating this point is the iApps case. The organisation is an IT company that does mobile apps, as the name iApps suggests. One of its customers was Sport Singapore, which I think is the government statutory board, which engaged the organisation to develop the system underlying the ActiveSG app. That's an app people in Singapore use to book facilities and other sports activities. The organisation developed a code fix, maybe to fix some kind of bug. Such code fixes were meant to be deployed first in the enterprise environment, meaning not live yet, so that they can be tested before release to the public. But here, the engineer erroneously deployed it straight into production. The code went live immediately, and because the code was defective, some users were able to see the personal data of other individuals.

    The PDPC said this was also a breach of the Protection Obligation. One of the problems the PDPC highlighted was that when the organisation deployed code, there was no second-level check or supervisory mechanism to make sure the deployment was done properly. What the PDPC is saying is that relying on employees to perform their jobs properly is not, by itself, a security measure. In a situation where there's clearly high risk, meaning that if the thing is done wrongly, personal data will be exposed or improperly processed, and code deployment is one of those situations, then you're expected to do a bit more. One example of doing a bit more is imposing some sort of supervision over the process. In this case, for example, before deploying the code fix, a senior person could have been asked to review it.

    最后一项主要的行政措施是监督。与前两项不同，监督并不总是必需的。一般只有当员工从事所谓的高风险活动时才需要，也就是那些很有可能发生未经授权处理个人数据的活动。

    说明这一点的案例是 iApps 案。该组织是一家做手机应用的 IT 公司，名字 iApps 就说明了这点。它的客户之一是新加坡体育理事会（Sport Singapore），我想那是政府法定机构，委托该组织开发 ActiveSG 应用的底层系统。这是新加坡人用来预订场地和其他体育活动的应用。该组织开发了一个代码修复，可能是为了修某种 bug。这类代码修复本应先部署到企业环境，也就是还没上线的环境，以便在向公众发布前进行测试。但这次，工程师错误地把它直接部署到了生产环境。代码立即上线，由于代码有缺陷，一些用户能够看到其他人的个人数据。

    PDPC 认定这也违反了保护义务。PDPC 指出的问题之一是，该组织在部署代码时，没有任何二级检查或监督机制来确保部署正确完成。PDPC 的意思是，仅仅依赖员工把工作做好，本身并不是一项安全措施。在明显高风险的情况下，也就是一旦做错就会导致个人数据被暴露或被不当处理的情况，代码部署就是其中之一，你就需要多做一些。多做一些的一个例子就是对流程施加某种监督。比如在这个案例中，在部署代码修复之前，可以要求一位资深人员先审查一下。

  - [19:00:04–19:03:03] **Supervision for Sensitive Mass Emails: Credit Counselling Singapore (2017)**

    Another example is Credit Counselling Singapore, or CCS. CCS is a Singapore charity that specialises in helping what we call debt-distressed clients, meaning people facing debt problems. One of the things this charity did was a debt management programme, or DMP. Under this programme, they would help clients work out some kind of feasible payment arrangement with their creditors, since these clients otherwise can't pay. As part of the DMP, CCS would seek status updates from clients to make sure they were keeping up with the payment terms.

    One time, one of CCS's admin staff sent an email to all these DMP clients asking for the usual updates. But this time, she incorrectly placed their email addresses in the wrong field. Usually, if you want to hide the addresses, you put them in the BCC field. But this staff member put them into the "To" field, which means everyone can see everyone else's email addresses. To make things worse, two of the recipients then hit "reply all" when replying to CCS, which disclosed even more of their personal data to everyone else.

    The PDPC said this was a breach because, again, there was an absence of any form of supervision or second-level check. When you're sending mass emails involving sensitive personal data, the PDPC says this is a high-risk activity, because chances are, at some point, you will make a mistake and disclose sensitive information. So CCS was expected to have some kind of check, rather than just allowing the admin staff to send this particular email. But the PDPC does clarify that you don't have to supervise every single case where you send a message. What is called for is supervision if the personal data happens to be sensitive. In this case, the email addresses are sensitive because, in this particular context, they tell everyone else that you are in some kind of debt trouble, which is of course a fairly sensitive piece of information.

    另一个例子是新加坡信贷咨询中心（Credit Counselling Singapore，CCS）。CCS 是新加坡的一家慈善机构，专门帮助我们所说的债务困境客户，也就是面临债务问题的人。这家慈善机构做的事情之一是债务管理计划（DMP）。在这个计划下，他们会帮客户与债权人商定某种可行的还款安排，因为这些客户否则无力偿还。作为 DMP 的一部分，CCS 会向客户索取进度更新，确保他们按照还款条件执行。

    有一次，CCS 的一名行政人员给所有 DMP 客户发邮件，索取例行更新。但这次她把收件人邮箱地址放错了栏位。通常如果你想隐藏地址，你会把它们放在密送（BCC）栏。但这位员工把地址放进了"收件人（To）"栏，这意味着每个人都能看到其他所有人的邮箱地址。更糟糕的是，有两位收件人在回复 CCS 时点了"全部回复"，这又向所有人披露了更多他们的个人数据。

    PDPC 认定这构成违规，原因同样是缺乏任何形式的监督或二级检查。PDPC 认为，当你群发涉及敏感个人数据的邮件时，这就是高风险活动，因为很可能在某个时候你会犯错，泄露敏感信息。所以 CCS 本应设立某种检查，而不是任由行政人员直接发送这封邮件。但 PDPC 也澄清，并不是每次发送信息都需要监督。需要监督的是个人数据恰好属于敏感数据的情况。在这个案例中，邮箱地址是敏感的，因为在这个特定情境下，它们会让其他所有人知道你陷入了某种债务困境，这当然是相当敏感的信息。

  - [19:03:15–19:05:13] **Q&A: What Counts as Adequate Supervision?**

    A student asks: would it be enough for the supervisor to just come over, look, give a thumbs up, and that's it? Or does it have to be more extensive than that?

    I suppose there are different ways to implement supervision. I think one example would be what you just described: having someone take a look at the screen before the email gets sent, just to make sure nothing has gone wrong. Another option is that you create the email and send it as a draft to the supervisor, who then sends it. As long as someone else is looking at it, just another pair of eyes on it.

    The student follows up: what if there is a secondary person reviewing, but the email still goes out wrongly?

    Right, so I guess the question is: what happens if person A sends to person B, who is supposed to supervise, but person B fails to do it properly, and the email gets sent out anyway? In this case, I think the PDPC would probably say you've done enough. You can't stop people from being careless. Depending on how you implemented the supervision, the PDPC will probably say that you did enough. But of course, if it happens again and again, then one has to suspect that the way in which you implemented supervision was not sufficient. But if it's a one-off, careless, unlucky thing, normally that will be fine. Does anyone else have any questions?

    一位同学问：主管只要过来看一眼、竖个大拇指，就算可以了吗？还是需要更全面的审查？

    我想实施监督有不同的方式。我认为一个例子就是你刚才描述的：在邮件发送前请别人看一下屏幕，确保没出错。另一种选择是你写好邮件，以草稿形式发给主管，再由主管发送。只要有另一个人看过就行，就是多一双眼睛。

    这位同学追问：如果有第二个人审查了，但邮件还是错误地发出去了呢？

    对，我想问题是：如果 A 把邮件交给负责监督的 B，但 B 没有好好审查，邮件还是发出去了，会怎样？在这种情况下，我认为 PDPC 很可能会说你已经做得足够了。你没办法阻止人粗心。取决于你如何实施监督，PDPC 很可能会认定你已经尽到了责任。但当然，如果这种事一再发生，那就不得不怀疑你实施监督的方式是否不够充分。但如果只是一次性的、粗心的、不走运的事情，通常是没问题的。还有其他人有问题吗？

  - [19:05:24–19:07:04] **Managing Data Intermediaries: Primary vs Direct Responsibility**

    One more administrative measure is managing data intermediaries. You'll recall that in the second seminar we talked about the concept of a data intermediary. There can be a data controller–data intermediary relationship, where the data intermediary processes personal data on behalf of the data controller. For example, if you engage Microsoft for cloud storage, Microsoft is a data intermediary. We also talked about how, in general, the data intermediary is not bound by most of the PDPA's obligations, because it is not the data controller. That's what I mentioned in that seminar. But the thing to understand, as far as the Protection Obligation is concerned, is that both parties are responsible. The Protection Obligation is one of those obligations that hits both the controller and the intermediary.

    Looking at it from the controller's perspective, one of the things the controller has to do is govern its data intermediaries, and that's also an important security measure. Of course, this only applies if you are appointing an intermediary. So how does the PDPC look at this kind of relationship? What it has said in some cases is that the data controller has the so-called "primary" responsibility for security, whereas the data intermediary has the "direct" responsibility.

    还有一项行政措施是管理数据中介（data intermediary）。你们应该记得，在第二次研讨课上我们讲过数据中介的概念。可以存在数据控制者与数据中介的关系，即数据中介代表数据控制者处理个人数据。例如，如果你聘请微软提供云存储，微软就是数据中介。我们也讲过，一般而言，数据中介不受 PDPA 大部分义务的约束，因为它不是数据控制者。这是我在那次研讨课上提到的。但就保护义务而言，你需要理解的是，双方都负有责任。保护义务是同时约束控制者和中介的义务之一。

    从控制者的角度来看，控制者必须做的事情之一就是管理其数据中介，这也是一项重要的安全措施。当然，这只适用于你委任了中介的情况。那么 PDPC 如何看待这种关系？它在一些案例中说过，数据控制者对安全负有所谓的"首要"责任，而数据中介负有"直接"责任。

  - [19:07:32–19:10:23] **Both Parties in Breach: The Cellar Door (2016)**

    The Cellar Door case illustrates this point. The data controller here is The Cellar Door, which sells various food, wine, beverages, and so on. It appointed GIW, an IT service provider, to build its website and host its data. Then in 2014, it was discovered that The Cellar Door's customer data had been leaked and uploaded onto Pastebin, which is a place where hackers tend to upload their materials. Both parties were unable to figure out how the data got leaked. The PDPC investigated and found that both The Cellar Door, as the controller, and GIW, as the intermediary, were in breach.

    So how does the PDPC look at this scenario? First, it concludes that GIW is the intermediary, because it is processing customer data on behalf of The Cellar Door. The processing takes place because GIW was hosting, or storing, The Cellar Door's customer data, and that counts as processing. So now you have this relationship. What is the data controller supposed to do? As the data controller, The Cellar Door has the so-called primary responsibility. What that means is that the controller's responsibility is to put in place security measures that the intermediary is supposed to follow. For example, the controller should make sure that in its contract with the intermediary, security arrangements are imposed. It may also need to follow up with checks to make sure the intermediary is actually following through on whatever security arrangements were agreed. So that's primary responsibility. As for the intermediary, which has the direct responsibility, that's more straightforward: the intermediary needs to carry out whatever security arrangements were agreed. In this case, both organisations failed in their respective responsibilities.

    The Cellar Door 案说明了这一点。这里的数据控制者是 The Cellar Door，销售各种食品、葡萄酒、饮料等。它委任了 IT 服务商 GIW 为其建网站并托管数据。到了 2014 年，人们发现 The Cellar Door 的客户数据被泄露并上传到了 Pastebin，那是黑客常常上传他们材料的地方。双方都没能查明数据是如何泄露的。PDPC 调查后认定，作为控制者的 The Cellar Door 和作为中介的 GIW 都违反了义务。

    那么 PDPC 是如何分析这个情形的？首先，它认定 GIW 是中介，因为它代表 The Cellar Door 处理客户数据。之所以构成处理，是因为 GIW 托管、即存储了 The Cellar Door 的客户数据，这就算处理。于是就有了这种关系。那么数据控制者应该做什么？作为数据控制者，The Cellar Door 负有所谓的首要责任。这意味着，控制者的责任是制定中介需要遵守的安全措施。例如，控制者应确保在与中介的合同中规定安全安排。它可能还需要跟进检查，确保中介确实落实了所约定的安全安排。这就是首要责任。至于负有直接责任的中介，那就更直接了：中介需要执行所约定的任何安全安排。在这个案例中，两个组织都没有履行各自的责任。

  - [19:10:23–19:13:53] **Controller Compliant, Intermediary in Breach: Singapore Telecommunications (2017)**

    From this point, you can already see that it is possible for one party to succeed but the other to fail. There will be some cases where the data intermediary, because of their carelessness, fails to comply with the Protection Obligation, but the controller is found to have done enough. So it's possible for the controller to have fulfilled its responsibility even if the intermediary fails.

    The case that highlights this point is, again, a Singtel case, an earlier one. The context is the Singtel ONEPASS service, which allowed customers to access their accounts with Singtel. Singtel engaged an intermediary called Tech Mahindra to develop and maintain this ONEPASS service. One Singtel customer complained they were having login difficulties. Singtel forwarded this complaint to Tech Mahindra, which realised it needed to update this customer's profile. So it executed a database script to update the profile. But after the script was run, it was found that other ONEPASS accounts had also been modified to show that particular user's personal data. This happened to up to 2.78 million accounts. The reason this somewhat dramatic incident happened was that when Tech Mahindra wrote the script, they forgot to add the WHERE clause, so the change affected every single customer's profile instead of just the one affected customer.

    So, data breach, the PDPC investigates, and interestingly, it found that while the intermediary, Tech Mahindra, was in breach, Singtel, the controller, was not. The reason is that Singtel did actually do what was necessary as data controller. In the contract, it required Tech Mahindra to adhere to its data security policies. It required training. It imposed an SOP requiring database changes to be done in a development environment first. It did on-site security reviews, conducted its own penetration testing, and so on. So it actually did the full suite of things a responsible data controller should do. Tech Mahindra, on the other hand, failed its direct responsibility. To put it simply, they didn't follow instructions, and that was a breach of the Protection Obligation. What this case highlights is that, as the controller, as long as you do what you're required to do, even if the intermediary fails on their side, you will not be held liable.

    从这里你已经可以看出，有可能一方合规而另一方违规。会有一些案例中，数据中介因为自己粗心而没有遵守保护义务，但控制者被认定已经尽到了责任。所以，即使中介失职，控制者也有可能已经履行了自己的责任。

    说明这一点的案例同样是 Singtel 案，一个更早的案子。背景是 Singtel 的 ONEPASS 服务，让客户访问自己在 Singtel 的账户。Singtel 委托一家叫 Tech Mahindra 的中介开发和维护 ONEPASS 服务。一位 Singtel 客户投诉登录有困难。Singtel 把投诉转给 Tech Mahindra，Tech Mahindra 发现需要更新这位客户的资料。于是它执行了一个数据库脚本来更新资料。但脚本运行后，人们发现其他 ONEPASS 账户也被修改成显示这位用户的个人数据。受影响的账户多达 278 万个。之所以发生这么戏剧性的事件，是因为 Tech Mahindra 写脚本时忘了加 WHERE 子句，导致修改影响了每一位客户的资料，而不只是那一位受影响的客户。

    于是发生了数据泄露，PDPC 进行调查，有意思的是，它认定中介 Tech Mahindra 违规，而控制者 Singtel 没有违规。原因是 Singtel 确实做了数据控制者该做的事。它在合同中要求 Tech Mahindra 遵守其数据安全政策，要求进行培训，规定了数据库变更必须先在开发环境中进行的标准作业程序（SOP），进行了现场安全审查，还自行做了渗透测试等等。所以它确实做了一个负责任的数据控制者应做的全套工作。而 Tech Mahindra 则没有履行其直接责任。简单说，他们没有按指示办事，这就违反了保护义务。这个案例说明，作为控制者，只要你做了你需要做的事，即使中介那边出了问题，你也不会被追究责任。

  - [19:14:17–19:18:35] **Reasonable Oversight Required: Fullerton Healthcare Group (2023)**

    So what are data controllers supposed to do with their data intermediaries? One thing, as we mentioned, is the need to impose certain security measures on the intermediary. It is now common practice that when you contract with a data intermediary, you make sure there are security obligations in the contract. So that's common. The more difficult question is: are you really expected to make sure the data intermediary follows through? Here, we have cases that say different things.

    One case, Fullerton Healthcare Group, suggests that you are actually expected to exercise supervision. This case is interesting. Fullerton is an enterprise healthcare service provider. Fullerton engaged Agape, a social enterprise, to provide call centre and appointment booking services. And because Agape is a social enterprise, it engaged inmates from Changi Women's Prison to provide some of these call centre services. In order for Agape to provide these services, Fullerton gave Agape access to customers' personal data. The way it did this was by authorising one Agape computer through which Agape could access Fullerton's customer data. I guess this was to make sure there was only one channel through which the data could flow. But I guess Agape didn't get the memo. What they did was download the whole database and re-upload it onto their own online drive, to allow the inmates to also access the data. Subsequently, Fullerton realised that the data was being sold on the dark web. It investigated by hiring a security consultant, who found the seller of the data, and the seller claimed to have exfiltrated the data from Agape's online drive. So that was the vulnerability.

    The PDPC found a breach of the Protection Obligation on both sides, the controller and the intermediary. Focusing on the controller, Fullerton, the PDPC said that organisations should exercise, quote unquote, "reasonable oversight" over their data intermediaries. Fullerton did do some things. It carried out due diligence on Agape and imposed the usual security measures in the contract. So it did the basic things. But it did not exercise reasonable oversight by monitoring how Agape was handling the personal data. Specifically, the PDPC said that since Fullerton was aware that Agape was engaging prison inmates to carry out the services, it should have asked how exactly Agape was planning to give the inmates access. If it had asked those questions, it would probably have stepped in to stop this download-and-re-upload process. So this was a case where the PDPC said the controller was expected to supervise.

    那么，数据控制者应该如何对待其数据中介？如前所述，其中一件事是需要对中介施加某些安全措施。现在的普遍做法是，与数据中介签合同时，确保合同中有安全义务。这很常见。更难的问题是：你是否真的需要确保数据中介切实执行？在这一点上，不同案例的说法不一样。

    其中一个案例，Fullerton Healthcare Group（富乐顿医疗集团）案，表明你确实需要进行监督。这个案子很有意思。Fullerton 是一家企业医疗服务提供商。Fullerton 聘请了社会企业 Agape 提供呼叫中心和预约服务。由于 Agape 是社会企业，它雇用了樟宜女子监狱的囚犯来提供部分呼叫中心服务。为了让 Agape 提供这些服务，Fullerton 向 Agape 提供了客户个人数据的访问权限。具体方式是授权 Agape 的一台电脑访问 Fullerton 的客户数据。我想这是为了确保数据只通过一个渠道流动。但我猜 Agape 没收到这个通知。他们把整个数据库下载下来，再重新上传到自己的在线云盘，好让囚犯也能访问数据。后来，Fullerton 发现这些数据正在暗网上出售。它聘请了安全顾问进行调查，顾问找到了数据卖家，卖家声称他是从 Agape 的在线云盘中窃取的数据。这就是漏洞所在。

    PDPC 认定控制者和中介双方都违反了保护义务。就控制者 Fullerton 而言，PDPC 说组织应当对其数据中介进行所谓的"合理监督"。Fullerton 确实做了一些事情：它对 Agape 做了尽职调查，并在合同中规定了常规的安全措施。所以基本的事情它做了。但它没有通过监控 Agape 如何处理个人数据来进行合理监督。具体而言，PDPC 认为，既然 Fullerton 知道 Agape 雇用囚犯来提供服务，它就应该询问 Agape 究竟打算如何让囚犯获得访问权限。如果它问了这些问题，它很可能就会介入，阻止这种下载再上传的做法。所以在这个案例中，PDPC 认为控制者有监督的义务。

  - [19:18:43–19:20:41] **No Supervision Required: AIG Asia Pacific Insurance (2019)**

    But you also have other cases where the PDPC says you don't have to supervise. For example, in the AIG case, you have an insurance company that contracted with an intermediary called Toppan to print and mail letters. In one instance, Toppan sent out a batch of renewal letters for motor insurance. These letters contained a form with customer information for customers who wanted to renew their insurance, plus a business reply envelope, so that customers, after filling in the form, could put it into the envelope and send it back. In this instance, one of Toppan's employees, when packing the letters, inserted the wrong business reply envelopes. So if a customer filled in the form and used that envelope, the form would go to another company, Tan Chong Credit.

    The PDPC said there was a breach of the Protection Obligation, but not by AIG; AIG was okay. The PDPC noted that AIG did put in place contractual requirements for Toppan to secure personal data, including when packing these letters. And although AIG didn't actually supervise how the mailing was done, the PDPC said it was not reasonable to expect AIG to supervise that, in particular because of Toppan's credibility and reputation. I'm not sure whether Toppan does in fact have such a reputation, but that was what the PDPC decided.

    但也有其他案例中，PDPC 认为你不需要监督。例如在 AIG 案中，一家保险公司与一家叫 Toppan（凸版）的中介签约，由其打印和邮寄信件。有一次，Toppan 寄出了一批汽车保险续保信。这些信件里有一份包含客户信息的表格，供想要续保的客户填写，另外附有一个商业回邮信封，客户填好表格后可以装进信封寄回。这一次，Toppan 的一名员工在装信时放错了商业回邮信封。所以如果客户填好表格并使用那个信封，表格就会寄到另一家公司陈振中信贷（Tan Chong Credit）。

    PDPC 认定存在违反保护义务的情况，但违规的不是 AIG，AIG 没有问题。PDPC 指出，AIG 确实在合同中要求 Toppan 保护个人数据，包括在装封这些信件时。虽然 AIG 并没有实际监督邮寄过程，但 PDPC 认为期望 AIG 去监督这一点是不合理的，尤其是考虑到 Toppan 的信誉和声誉。我不确定 Toppan 是否真的有这样的声誉，但这就是 PDPC 的决定。

  - [19:20:41–19:24:55] **Discussion: Why Supervise in Fullerton but Not in AIG?**

    So one question we might consider is whether there are any relevant differences between the AIG case and the Fullerton case. What makes it so that in one case you must supervise, but in the other case you don't have to?

    A student suggests two differences: first, the quality of the intermediary; second, whether or not you know they are engaging inmates.

    So one differentiating factor is that in the AIG case you had an intermediary that was more credible. Agape was probably a newer sort of player, and it was doing this interesting arrangement of engaging prison inmates. Any other relevant differences?

    Another student points to the access arrangement: in Fullerton, you'd only need to ask some more questions about how the intermediary was giving access to the data; whereas in AIG, knowing the process would require actually going down to watch, which would take too much effort.

    So another differentiating factor is that in the Fullerton case, supervision was actually quite simple, because Fullerton just had to ask the right question. Whereas supervision in the AIG case was much more onerous. You'd almost have to send someone down, twenty-four seven, to watch them do the work, in which case you might as well do it yourself. I think in cases where supervision would involve an unreasonable amount of effort, you cannot reasonably be expected to do it.

    One more distinguishing factor you can see is that in the Fullerton case the data is fairly sensitive, because it involves at least some health information and financial information. Whereas in the motor insurance case, it's probably not that sensitive.

    A student asks: couldn't AIG have asked Toppan how they prevent accidental swapping of the letters and envelopes? Wouldn't that be a similar question to the one Fullerton should have asked?

    I think asking a question in this case would not change anything, because AIG did impose contractual requirements for Toppan to take care when sending out these letters. I suppose it is possible they could have asked further questions about the specific procedures when packing the letters into the envelopes. But I think the PDPC used the word "micromanagement" in relation to that. It took the view that you could not reasonably expect AIG to micromanage that. Whereas in the Fullerton case, the question to be asked was a fairly broad one: essentially, how are you giving access, and how do you intend to achieve it?

    所以我们可以思考一个问题：AIG 案和 Fullerton 案之间有没有相关的区别？为什么一个案子中你必须监督，而另一个案子中你不必？

    一位同学提出两点区别：第一，中介的素质；第二，你是否知道他们雇用了囚犯。

    所以一个区分因素是，在 AIG 案中，中介更有信誉。Agape 可能是个较新的参与者，而且它采用了雇用囚犯这种很特别的安排。还有其他相关区别吗？

    另一位同学指出访问安排的问题：在 Fullerton 案中，你只需要多问几个关于中介如何提供数据访问的问题；而在 AIG 案中，要了解流程就需要实际去现场看，这会耗费太多精力。

    所以另一个区分因素是，在 Fullerton 案中，监督其实相当简单，Fullerton 只需要问对问题就行。而在 AIG 案中，监督要繁重得多。你几乎需要派人去现场，全天候盯着他们干活，那样的话你还不如自己做。我认为，在监督需要付出不合理程度精力的情况下，不能合理地期望你去做。

    你还能看到另一个区分因素：在 Fullerton 案中，数据相当敏感，因为至少涉及一些健康信息和财务信息。而在汽车保险的案子里，数据可能没那么敏感。

    一位同学问：AIG 难道不能问问 Toppan 他们如何防止信件和信封被意外装错吗？这不就和 Fullerton 本该问的问题类似吗？

    我认为在这个案子里问这个问题不会改变什么，因为 AIG 确实在合同中要求 Toppan 在寄送这些信件时谨慎处理。我想他们确实有可能进一步询问装信封时的具体流程。但我记得 PDPC 在这一点上用了"微观管理（micromanagement）"这个词。它认为不能合理地期望 AIG 去微观管理这种事情。而在 Fullerton 案中，需要问的问题是一个相当宽泛的问题：本质上就是，你们如何提供访问权限，打算怎么实现？

  - [19:25:11–19:26:27] **Q&A: Should Singtel Have Reviewed Tech Mahindra's Script?**

    A student raises a question about the Singtel–Tech Mahindra case, essentially whether Singtel should have been expected to check the database update before it was run.

    I can't recall whether they actually had some kind of supervision in this case. I think there may have been some requirement for code to be reviewed by Singtel before it was deployed. But even if there was no such requirement, I think this case would be a bit tricky for me as to whether I would require Singtel to review the code. Because if you expect Singtel to review every single code change this particular vendor makes, that might require so much effort that it would no longer be worthwhile appointing the vendor. So I'm not sure whether this would be required.

    一位同学就 Singtel 与 Tech Mahindra 一案提问，大意是：是否应当期望 Singtel 在脚本运行前审核这次数据库更新？

    我记不清这个案子中他们实际上是否有某种监督。我想可能有要求代码在部署前由 Singtel 审查。但即使没有这样的要求，对我来说，这个案子是否应要求 Singtel 审查代码，也是有点棘手的。因为如果你期望 Singtel 审查这家供应商的每一次代码修改，那可能需要耗费太多精力，以至于委任这家供应商都不再划算了。所以我不确定这是否会被要求。

  - [19:26:27–19:29:41] **Student Objection: Is a Reputation-Based Test Fair?**

    A student objects that differentiating the level of responsibility based on the credibility and reputation of the company seems a little unfair. Even if a company has a hundred percent track record, mistakes can still happen, so responsibility still exists. It feels discriminatory, because it depends on the company's standing.

    That's a really fair point. The fact that a company has been operating for a number of years with a good track record may just be luck, and it may not reflect its actual data security capabilities. I suppose this is a kind of rule of thumb that can be applied when deciding whether supervision is required. Of course, you can't just rely on credibility, because you also need to put in place your own security arrangements and get the intermediary to agree to them. You can't just say, you handle this, I'm done. In terms of whether supervision is required, think about the big data intermediaries: Microsoft, Google. In those cases, you probably would not even bother to ask whether you should supervise their data processing, because you're fairly confident these entities are so reputable that they have security processes, and probably better processes than yours. So in that sense, reputation can serve as a kind of guide to whether you need to supervise.

    Another student points out that this disadvantages smaller players.

    A further student adds that, reading the actual PDPC decision, it says that because of Toppan's credibility, AIG wasn't really expected to audit such a minor part of the work, since the enveloping process was only a minor part of Toppan's overall service. Given Toppan's credibility, doing so would amount to micromanaging.

    一位同学提出异议：根据公司的信誉和声誉来区分责任程度，似乎有点不公平。即使一家公司有百分之百的良好记录，错误仍然可能发生，所以责任依然存在。这感觉带有歧视性，因为它取决于公司的地位。

    这是一个非常合理的观点。一家公司多年经营、记录良好，可能只是运气好，未必反映其真实的数据安全能力。我想这算是判断是否需要监督时可以使用的一种经验法则。当然，你不能只依赖信誉，因为你还需要制定自己的安全安排，并让中介同意遵守。你不能只说"你来处理吧，我不管了"。至于是否需要监督，想想那些大型数据中介：微软、谷歌。在这些情况下，你可能根本不会去考虑是否要监督他们的数据处理，因为你相当确信这些机构信誉卓著，有自己的安全流程，而且很可能比你的流程更好。所以从这个意义上说，声誉可以作为判断是否需要监督的一种参考。

    另一位同学指出，这对规模较小的参与者不利。

    还有一位同学补充说，他读了 PDPC 的原始裁决，裁决中提到，由于 Toppan 的信誉，AIG 并不需要审计如此次要的一部分工作，因为装信封只是 Toppan 整体服务中很小的一部分。鉴于 Toppan 的信誉，这样做就等于微观管理了。

  - [19:29:41–19:34:43] **Discussion: Difficulties in Supervising a Data Intermediary; Break**

    Let's move on to the next question, which is: what are some difficulties in supervising a data intermediary? One thing you can immediately observe is the problem of doing it too much, especially depending on who the intermediary is.

    A student raises AI: if the intermediary uses deep learning or AI, the data controller does not know exactly how the AI handles things like real-time analytics, even though it has been put in place.

    Another difficulty is the lack of visibility, especially if the processing takes place at the software level. And of course, it can be technically challenging, so the controller has to have the technical competence to actually supervise.

    A student observes that supervision is somewhat paradoxical, even counterproductive: you normally hire an intermediary because they have the expertise to do something you don't have the expertise to do. But if you have to supervise them, then you need to have that expertise yourself.

    It also imposes a cost on you, if you have to dedicate a person to go and supervise the intermediary.

    When you're dealing with foreign and large intermediaries, there will be some difficulties too. Chances are you can't even negotiate with them over what security measures you want. And if you go even further and say, I want oversight whenever you carry out your processing, I think they'll just say no.

    Any other difficulties you've encountered?

    A student points out that it is possible for the intermediary itself to engage further intermediaries.

    I'm not sure I covered what happens in that situation, so I won't include that part in the course. But in general, the idea is that the controller is not responsible for the sub-intermediary; you're responsible for your own intermediary.

    A student offers a further example, the point being that in some arrangements you might get exposed to data you're not supposed to be exposed to, and that for AI, this is something you need to make sure of.

    So I think that goes into the idea of the cost of supervision. Supervision may, in some instances, not be that easy to do. It's not just a matter of sending someone down to look and see what is being done. It may actually involve some configuration of the system.

    Before I go into this question, let's pause here for about ten minutes and come back at maybe seven forty.

    我们进入下一个问题：监督数据中介有哪些困难？你马上就能注意到的一点是监督过度的问题，尤其取决于中介是谁。

    一位同学提到 AI：如果中介使用深度学习或 AI，数据控制者并不确切知道 AI 如何处理诸如实时分析之类的事情，即便系统已经部署上线。

    另一个困难是缺乏可见性，尤其当处理发生在软件层面时。当然，这在技术上也可能很有挑战性，所以控制者必须具备实际进行监督的技术能力。

    一位同学指出，监督有点自相矛盾，甚至适得其反：你通常聘请中介，是因为他们具备你所没有的专业能力。但如果你必须监督他们，那你自己就需要具备这种专业能力。

    如果你必须专门派一个人去监督中介，这也会给你带来成本。

    当你面对海外的大型中介时，也会遇到一些困难。很可能你连和他们谈判想要什么安全措施都做不到。如果你再进一步，说"每次你们处理数据时我都要监督"，我想他们会直接拒绝。

    你们还遇到过其他困难吗？

    一位同学指出，中介本身有可能再委托其他中介。

    我不确定我是否讲过这种情况下会怎样，所以我不会把这部分纳入课程内容。但一般来说，原则是控制者不对次级中介负责；你只对你自己的中介负责。

    一位同学又举了一个例子，要点是在某些安排下，你可能会接触到你本不该接触的数据，而对于 AI 来说，这是需要确保的事情。

    所以我认为这涉及监督成本的问题。在某些情况下，监督可能并不那么容易。它不只是派个人去现场看看他们在做什么，实际上可能涉及对系统的某种配置。

    在进入这个问题之前，我们先在这里休息大约十分钟，大概七点四十回来。

  - [19:43:59–19:46:15] **Question 1: VX, Quotta, and the Agentic AI Component**

    So let's come back. I've included a question on this slide, which I think is a pretty straightforward one. Let's have a look. We have VX, which manufactures and sells various medical devices, both to retailers and directly to consumers. It contracts with Quotta, a US-based startup providing CRM services. Quotta's platform recently incorporated an AI component with agentic functionality, which VX's customer service personnel can use to have the AI component directly access the customer database and construct responses to customer queries. So the question asks: what kinds of policies and training should VX implement in relation to the use of the AI component, and how should VX manage Quotta as a data intermediary? The questions here are more specific, and I think there are a few points you can highlight. Let's take a couple of minutes to think about how you'd respond.

    好，我们回来。我在这张幻灯片上放了一道题，我觉得是比较直接的题目。我们来看一下。VX 是一家制造和销售各类医疗器械的公司，既卖给零售商，也直接卖给消费者。它与 Quotta 签约，Quotta 是一家位于美国、提供客户关系管理（CRM）服务的初创公司。Quotta 的平台最近加入了一个具有智能体（agentic）功能的 AI 组件，VX 的客服人员可以让这个 AI 组件直接访问客户数据库，以构建对客户咨询的回复。所以题目问的是：针对 AI 组件的使用，VX 应当实施哪些政策和培训？VX 应当如何把 Quotta 作为数据中介来管理？这里的问题比较具体，我认为有几个要点可以提出来。我们花几分钟想一想你会怎么回答。

  - [19:46:15–19:48:47] **Discussion: Policies, Training, and Human-in-the-Loop for the AI Component**

    Any thoughts? If you were VX, what would you focus on in terms of administrative security measures?

    A student suggests ensuring that all the customer service staff are trained in data protection, so that they won't expose or send out particular data.

    So training, but what policies would you develop and then train them on? As far as this AI model is concerned, I think there are some new policies needed around the use cases, for the staff who actually use the AI or agent platform.

    Another student suggests that since it's a customer database with multiple columns of customer data, you'd want to define what queries the staff can use, maybe a fixed set of queries. Also, when staff query the customer base, perhaps a supervisor verifies the use case: yes, you can use the platform for this.

    I think we can build on that. When the AI identifies an individual's data and starts extracting it, are there policies on what data it's allowed to extract? Maybe narrow it, so it doesn't pull all the data, just the essential data needed to handle that specific query. So at least for this example, you'd have a couple of policies, including what sorts of data you're allowed to use this AI component to pull. And in terms of supervision, I guess supervision comes in the form of having some human in the loop. So when the AI constructs the response to the query, someone has to look at it and make sure it's not disclosing some other customer's personal data, for example. That would be a policy, and you'd need to train the staff on it if they want to use this component.

    有什么想法吗？如果你是 VX，在行政性安全措施方面你会关注什么？

    一位同学提出，要确保所有客服人员都接受数据保护培训，这样他们就不会暴露或发送特定的数据。

    所以是培训，但你会制定什么政策，再就这些政策培训他们？就这个 AI 模型而言，我认为需要围绕使用场景制定一些新政策，针对那些实际使用 AI 或智能体平台的员工。

    另一位同学提出，既然这是一个包含多列客户数据的客户数据库，就要规定员工可以使用哪些查询，也许是一组固定的查询。另外，当员工查询客户库时，也许由一位主管来核实使用场景：是的，你可以把平台用于这个用途。

    我认为我们可以在此基础上展开。当 AI 识别出某个人的数据并开始提取时，有没有政策规定它可以提取哪些数据？也许要缩小范围，让它不要拉取全部数据，只提取处理该具体查询所必需的数据。所以至少在这个例子里，你会有几项政策，包括允许用这个 AI 组件拉取哪些类型的数据。至于监督，我想监督可以体现为某种"人在回路中"（human in the loop）。也就是当 AI 构建好对查询的回复时，必须有人看一下，确保它没有泄露其他客户的个人数据，比如说。这就是一项政策，如果员工要使用这个组件，你需要就此培训他们。

  - [19:48:47–19:52:18] **Discussion: Managing Quotta as a Data Intermediary**

    Any other thoughts on how you'd manage Quotta as a data intermediary?

    A student says there must be a data processing agreement between Quotta and VX, outlining their respective obligations. For example, VX would supervise or meet with Quotta to check on the results and the accessibility of the customer information. That would be the guardrail to help manage the relationship.

    I guess in this case you would need to impose contractual requirements on the data intermediary. One earlier question you'd have to ask is whether Quotta is in fact a data intermediary. I suppose if the AI component belongs to Quotta, which it probably does, then you'll be sending data to Quotta for it to process the personal data and return the response to you. So there probably will be an intermediary relationship. Once you focus your attention on what exactly the processing involves, you can decide what kinds of security measures to put in place. This would include how to make sure the transfer of data to and from the platform is secure, and what the platform does with the data once it receives it. Does it get to keep the data, or should it automatically destroy the data once it's done? Things like that would be part of the security requirements we'd impose on Quotta.

    Another student points out that because there's an AI component, the question is whether the AI is proprietary to Quotta. Since they're a CRM company, they may not be using their own AI; they could actually be using a third party. So whatever contractual obligations VX imposes on Quotta, Quotta should impose similar contractual obligations on the AI component's owner, the third party.

    So I guess here you really should ask, as in the Fullerton case. You should really ask: you're providing an AI service, but where exactly is the AI? Are you running your own AI, or are you just passing it on to some other AI service provider? That would be a relevant question to ask. Probably here the expectation is higher that you have to conduct some kind of supervision because, as mentioned, this is a startup.
