After that data breach, I think Canvas was down for a couple of weeks.

    Just to run through some other well-known data breaches. On this side, you will see a clip of an article describing the SingHealth data breach, which is the biggest local data breach in Singapore, where about 1.5 million patients had their personal data stolen, including medical information.

    When we look overseas, in the second picture here, the numbers tend to get a bit bigger. One of the more well-known data breaches internationally is the Marriott data breach. What happened was that the hotel database was hacked, and it affected 500 million guests.

    On the other side of the screen, you will see a clip about the Ashley Madison database, which is a very famous case involving a site which promoted infidelity. The result of the data breach was that the people using that site had their personal data exposed. So these are some other examples of data breaches.

    第二类数据泄露我们称之为"攻击"型。一个例子，可能你们自己也亲身经历过，就是最近涉及 Canvas 的数据泄露，也就是我们现在正在使用的这个平台。大约在五月份，一个名为 ShinyHunters 的组织对 Canvas 学习平台发动了大规模网络攻击，影响了 NUS 和其他机构。那次数据泄露之后，我记得 Canvas 停了大概几个星期。

    再快速过一下其他几起著名的数据泄露事件。这一边大家可以看到一篇描述新保集团（SingHealth）数据泄露的文章截图，这是新加坡本地最大的数据泄露事件，约 150 万名患者的个人数据被盗，包括医疗信息。

    看看海外，也就是这里的第二张图，数字往往会更大一些。国际上比较著名的一起数据泄露是万豪（Marriott）数据泄露。当时酒店数据库遭到黑客入侵，影响了 5 亿名客人。

    屏幕另一边是关于 Ashley Madison 数据库的截图，这是一个非常有名的案例，涉及一个鼓吹婚外情的网站。数据泄露的结果是，使用该网站的人的个人数据被曝光。这些就是其他一些数据泄露的例子。

  - [18:41:29–18:42:44] **Duty to Assess and the 30-Day Timeline**

    So that's the first part, which is the data breach. Basically, as an organisation, if you find out that you have suffered a data breach, this triggers the first duty, which is the duty to assess. This duty says that if you have reason to believe that a data breach affecting your personal data has occurred, then you are required to conduct an assessment of whether this data breach counts as a notifiable data breach. Because not every data breach must be notified; only some types are. So your first duty is to determine whether the data breach is notifiable.

    The PDPC does say that the assessment must be done in a reasonable and expeditious manner. It doesn't say how many days you have, but in general, the PDPC's view is that you have thirty calendar days from when you first discover the breach to conduct this assessment, which actually is quite a lot.

    这是第一部分，也就是数据泄露本身。基本上，作为一个组织，如果你发现自己发生了数据泄露，就会触发第一项义务，即评估义务。这项义务规定，如果你有理由相信发生了影响你所持个人数据的数据泄露，那么你就必须评估这次泄露是否属于须通知的数据泄露（notifiable data breach）。因为并不是所有数据泄露都必须通知，只有某些类型才需要。所以你的第一项义务就是判断这次数据泄露是否须通知。

    PDPC 确实说过，评估必须以合理且迅速的方式进行。它没有说你有多少天，但总体而言，PDPC 的看法是，从你最初发现泄露之时起，你有三十个日历日来进行评估，这其实相当长了。

  - [18:42:44–18:43:28] **What Is a Notifiable Data Breach**

    The next question, of course, since you are trying to assess whether the data breach is notifiable, is: what counts as a notifiable data breach? There are two types of data breach that are notifiable. The first type is a breach that is likely to result in significant harm to the affected individuals. The second type is where the data breach is likely to be of a significant scale. Either one will suffice: either significant harm or significant scale.

    接下来的问题当然是，既然你要评估数据泄露是否须通知，那什么才算须通知的数据泄露？有两类数据泄露须通知。第一类是可能对受影响个人造成重大损害（significant harm）的泄露。第二类是可能达到重大规模（significant scale）的泄露。满足其中之一即可：要么是重大损害，要么是重大规模。

  - [18:43:28–18:45:53] **Significant Harm and the Deeming Provisions**

    Let's look at the first type, which is significant harm. What counts as significant harm? Again, the law does not give you a fixed definition. The PDPC has mentioned that significant harm can include things like severe physical, psychological, economic or financial harm, or other forms of harm. Which doesn't really take you very far.

    What is more useful here is that the Personal Data Protection Regulations specify certain types of personal data that, if breached, will automatically be deemed to cause significant harm. What this means is, if your data breach involves certain types of personal data, you can automatically assume there will be significant harm.

    So what are these categories? There are two main combinations that will result in significant harm. The first combination is if you have an individual's full name or ID number, plus certain prescribed personal data, which I'll talk about in the next couple of slides. If you have this combination, then it's deemed significant harm. The second combination is where you have the details of the individual's account with the organisation. This is basically your username plus password. If the data breach discloses this, you automatically have to assume significant harm. So these are the combinations.

    That being said, the regulations also say that we do not automatically deem significant harm if the personal data was already publicly available at the point of the breach, or the personal data was disclosed as required or permitted by law.

    Any questions so far?

    我们来看第一类，即重大损害。什么算重大损害？同样，法律没有给出固定定义。PDPC 提到，重大损害可以包括严重的身体、心理、经济或财务损害，或其他形式的损害。这其实帮助不大。

    这里更有用的是，《个人数据保护条例》（Personal Data Protection Regulations）规定了某些类型的个人数据，一旦泄露，将自动被视为造成重大损害。意思是，如果你的数据泄露涉及某些类型的个人数据，你可以自动认定会造成重大损害。

    那么这些类别是什么？主要有两种组合会被视为造成重大损害。第一种组合是：个人的全名或身份证号码，加上某些规定个人数据（prescribed personal data），我会在接下来几张幻灯片里讲。如果有这种组合，就被视为重大损害。第二种组合是：个人在该组织的账户信息，基本上就是用户名加密码。如果数据泄露披露了这些，你就必须自动认定存在重大损害。这就是这些组合。

    话虽如此，条例也规定，如果个人数据在泄露发生时已经是公开可得的，或者个人数据是根据法律要求或允许而披露的，那么就不会自动被视为造成重大损害。

    到目前为止有什么问题吗？

  - [18:45:53–18:47:16] **Examples of Prescribed Personal Data**

    As I mentioned, one situation where the breach will automatically be deemed to cause significant harm is if we have this combination: full name or ID number plus prescribed personal data. So what is prescribed personal data? If you want to see the full list, you should look at the guidelines, but I've just extracted certain examples to show you.

    On this slide, you can see a range of personal data, and here you have mostly financial information: things like your salary, your credit card details, your net worth, your withdrawals from the organisation, and so on.

    On this slide, you can see additional types of prescribed personal data, and here you have data that mostly relates to health: certain sensitive diseases, personal information about any fertility treatment or contraceptive treatment, domestic abuse, child adoption. These are all prescribed personal data.

    正如我刚才提到的，泄露会被自动视为造成重大损害的一种情况，就是出现这种组合：全名或身份证号码加上规定个人数据。那么什么是规定个人数据？如果想看完整清单，你们应该去看指南，但我只摘取了一些例子给大家看。

    在这张幻灯片上，你们可以看到一系列个人数据，这里大多是财务信息：比如你的薪水、信用卡信息、净资产、你从该组织提取的款项等等。

    在这张幻灯片上，你们可以看到其他类型的规定个人数据，这里主要是与健康有关的数据：某些敏感疾病、关于任何生育治疗或避孕治疗的个人信息、家庭暴力、儿童领养。这些都属于规定个人数据。

  - [18:47:16–18:48:43] **Worked Examples: Medical Clinic and Hotel Storage Drive**

    Now, just to show you one example of how this might work. Let's say you are the database administrator of a medical clinic, and you discover that some of your patient records have been accessed by someone who is not an authorised user. What you are supposed to do is to assess the data breach, to see what was affected and what was disclosed. Here, the medical clinic determines that the data breach involves certain medical records, including patients' NRIC numbers as well as diagnosis and treatment of sexually transmitted diseases, which we saw earlier was one of the categories of prescribed personal data. In this case, you should assess the data breach as a notifiable one, and then you proceed.

    Another example, this time involving the loss of a storage medium. Let's say you have a portable storage drive that contains details of hotel guests, including their full names, passport details and payment details. Again, because of the credit card details, this is also deemed to cause significant harm.

    下面举一个例子说明它是怎么运作的。假设你是一家诊所的数据库管理员，你发现部分病人记录被一个未经授权的用户访问了。你应该做的是评估这次数据泄露，看看哪些数据受到影响、哪些被披露。在这里，诊所确定这次泄露涉及某些医疗记录，包括病人的 NRIC 号码，以及性传播疾病的诊断和治疗，我们之前看到这属于规定个人数据的一类。在这种情况下，你应该把这次数据泄露评估为须通知的泄露，然后继续下一步。

    另一个例子，这次涉及存储介质的丢失。假设你有一个便携式存储设备，里面存有酒店客人的信息，包括全名、护照信息和付款信息。同样，由于涉及信用卡信息，这也被视为会造成重大损害。

  - [18:48:43–18:50:30] **Multiple Linked Data Breaches**

    One situation that might arise is what happens if you discover multiple data breaches. The question that arises when you have, let's say, three different data breaches is whether you need to combine all of them when determining whether the breach is likely to cause significant harm. Let's say breach A involves personal data one, two, three, and breach B involves personal data four, five, six. Do you combine them to determine whether they're likely to cause significant harm?

    What the PDPC says is that you should consider them together when they are likely to be linked, meaning by the same perpetrator, under the same circumstances.

    For example, let's say you are the database administrator of an organisation, and you discover some unauthorised access of your customers' full names and contact details. Full name and contact details by themselves are not enough; there's no prescribed personal data involved. So you will conclude at this point that there is no need to notify. However, later on, the same organisation discovers that the same set of personal data was posted online, plus certain confidential financial information. At this point, you should conclude that these two incidents are connected, so you must consider them together.

    可能出现的一种情况是：如果你发现了多起数据泄露怎么办？比如说有三起不同的数据泄露，问题就是在判断泄露是否可能造成重大损害时，你是否需要把它们合并起来考虑。假设泄露 A 涉及个人数据一、二、三，泄露 B 涉及个人数据四、五、六。你是否要把它们合在一起，来判断是否可能造成重大损害？

    PDPC 的说法是，当它们很可能相互关联时，也就是由同一个行为人、在相同情况下造成的，你就应该把它们放在一起考虑。

    举个例子，假设你是某个组织的数据库管理员，你发现客户的全名和联系方式遭到未经授权的访问。仅有全名和联系方式本身是不够的，没有涉及规定个人数据。所以此时你会得出结论：不需要通知。然而，后来同一个组织发现，同一批个人数据被发布到了网上，还附带了某些机密的财务信息。这时候你就应该认定这两起事件是有关联的，所以必须把它们放在一起考虑。

  - [18:50:30–18:53:12] **Significant Scale and Internal Data Breaches**

    So far we've talked about what counts as significant harm. The other situation where you have to notify is where the breach is of a significant scale, and the answer to what is a significant scale is very straightforward. Basically, as long as five hundred or more individuals are involved, that counts as significant.

    There is one exception to the idea of the notifiable data breach, and this exception relates to so-called internal data breaches. An internal data breach is a data breach that is confined entirely within the organisation. For example, let's say you have an HR executive called Sarah who loses a storage device containing personal data. She loses a thumb drive, and she reports it. After a few days, this thumb drive is found by some other employee named Rachel. Sarah then confirms that Rachel had immediately returned the thumb drive to the HR department, and no one else accessed it. What you have in this scenario is a purely internal data breach: someone lost something, someone else found it, all within the same organisation. So in this case, it's not notifiable.

    A student asks: in the period between losing it and finding it again, a lot of stuff can happen, so you often can't be sure. How would you handle that? Let's say it's found, but maybe somebody used it in the meantime or did something with it, since it's just a memory stick.

    In this scenario, the assumption is that when the thumb drive was lost, Rachel was the only person who picked it up. In a situation where you're not really sure, where maybe someone lost it, someone else passed it on, and then some other employee picked it up, under such circumstances you maybe can't assume that it's internal, and then, just in case, you would treat it accordingly.

    Okay. Any other questions?

    到目前为止我们讲了什么算重大损害。另一种必须通知的情况是泄露达到重大规模，而什么是重大规模，答案非常直接：基本上只要涉及五百人或以上，就算重大规模。

    须通知的数据泄露有一个例外，这个例外涉及所谓的内部数据泄露（internal data breach）。内部数据泄露是指完全局限在组织内部的数据泄露。举个例子，假设有一位名叫 Sarah 的人力资源主管，她丢失了一个存有个人数据的存储设备，丢了一个 U 盘，并且上报了。几天后，这个 U 盘被另一位名叫 Rachel 的员工找到。Sarah 随后确认，Rachel 立即把 U 盘交还给了人力资源部门，没有其他人访问过。这种情况就是纯粹的内部数据泄露：有人丢了东西，另一个人捡到了，全都发生在同一个组织内部。所以在这种情况下，它不属于须通知的泄露。

    一位学生问：在丢失到找回的这段时间里，很多事情都可能发生，所以往往没法确定。这种情况该怎么处理？比如说东西找回来了，但也许有人在这期间用过它，或者拿它做了什么，毕竟它只是一个 U 盘。

    在这个情境中，前提假设是 U 盘丢失后，Rachel 是唯一捡到它的人。如果你不太确定，比如有人丢了，另一个人转手了，然后又被别的员工捡到，在这种情况下你可能就不能认定它是内部泄露，那么为了保险起见，你就要相应地处理。

    好，还有其他问题吗？

  - [18:53:12–18:55:35] **Duty to Notify and Timeline Strategy**

    What we've discussed so far is the concept of data breach. If you discover a data breach, then you have a duty to assess. After you assess the data breach, let's say you assess that it is a notifiable data breach, then that takes you to the next step, which is your duty to notify.

    So what is this duty to notify? Essentially, if you assess that the data breach you have just suffered is a notifiable data breach, then as soon as practicable, you must notify the PDPC first. Here you have no more than three calendar days after the assessment to perform this notification. Plus, if it so happens that the data breach is one that is likely to cause significant harm, so it's not just significant scale, then you should also notify the individuals, because the idea is that you also want to allow them to take steps to protect themselves.

    One thing to note, of course, is the timeline. Because the duty to assess is thirty days and the duty to notify is three days, logically, if you want to give yourself a bit more time, you can probably afford to stretch out the assessment a bit more, because once you complete your assessment, you only have three days to notify. So there may be some sense in stretching out that particular timeline if you need more time to take whatever action you think is needed.

    到目前为止我们讨论的是数据泄露的概念。如果你发现了数据泄露，你就有评估义务。评估之后，假设你认定这是须通知的数据泄露，那就进入下一步，也就是你的通知义务。

    那么通知义务是什么？基本上，如果你评估认定刚刚发生的数据泄露是须通知的泄露，那么你必须在切实可行的情况下尽快先通知 PDPC。评估之后你最多有三个日历日来完成这项通知。此外，如果这次数据泄露恰好是可能造成重大损害的那一类，也就是说不只是规模重大，那么你还应该通知相关个人，因为其理念是让他们也能采取措施保护自己。

    当然，需要注意的一点是时间线。因为评估义务是三十天，通知义务是三天，从逻辑上讲，如果你想给自己多留一点时间，你大概可以把评估的时间稍微拉长一些，因为一旦完成评估，你只有三天时间通知。所以，如果你需要更多时间去采取你认为需要的行动，把这段时间拉长一点或许是有道理的。

  - [18:55:35–18:58:34] **Exceptions to Notifying Individuals: Remedial Action and Technological Protection**

    Under what circumstances do you not need to notify? There are two situations. The first one is if you took remedial actions after the data breach, making it unlikely that the data breach will cause significant harm to individuals. If you do this, you don't need to notify the individuals. You still have to notify the PDPC, but you don't have to notify the individuals.

    For example, let's say you're a travel agency, and you send an email containing personal data, but you send it to the wrong vendor. This would be a data breach. The email contains full names, credit card details and passport numbers, so this is a data breach that will be deemed to cause significant harm because of the data involved. But the travel agency's employee then takes immediate action: contacts the receiving vendor, confirms the attachment was not accessed, and confirms that the attachment was deleted. Having done this, the travel agency may assess that it took timely remedial actions to prevent the data breach from causing significant harm, because it immediately called the vendor and had the email deleted. So here, it will not need to notify the individuals. But you can't escape notifying the PDPC, even in this scenario. So that's one exception.

    The second exception is if you took technological protection measures before the data breach, which make the data inaccessible or unintelligible to any unauthorised party. Usually, what this means is you encrypted or password-protected the personal data, so that even if an unauthorised third party picked up the thumb drive or managed to access your database, they still can't access the personal data.

    For example, an HR director misplaces a storage drive which is encrypted. This storage drive contains the full names, medical schemes and so on of employees. Here you can assess that the technological protection exception applies, because the drive was encrypted. What this means is that even if someone picked up the thumb drive, it's unlikely they can access the personal data. So again, you don't have to notify the individuals, but you must still notify the PDPC.

    在什么情况下你不需要通知？有两种情况。第一种是你在数据泄露后采取了补救措施（remedial actions），使得这次泄露不太可能对个人造成重大损害。如果你这样做了，就不需要通知个人。你仍然要通知 PDPC，但不必通知个人。

    举个例子，假设你是一家旅行社，你发送了一封含有个人数据的电子邮件，但发给了错误的供应商。这就构成数据泄露。邮件里包含全名、信用卡信息和护照号码，所以由于涉及的数据，这次泄露会被视为造成重大损害。但是旅行社的员工随即采取了行动：联系收到邮件的供应商，确认附件没有被打开，并确认附件已被删除。这样做之后，旅行社可以评估认为，它已及时采取补救措施，防止了这次泄露造成重大损害，因为它立刻打电话给供应商并让对方删除了邮件。所以在这里，它不需要通知个人。但即使在这种情况下，你也无法免除通知 PDPC 的义务。这是一个例外。

    第二个例外是你在数据泄露发生之前就采取了技术保护措施（technological protection measures），使数据对任何未经授权的一方而言无法访问或无法理解。通常这意味着你对个人数据进行了加密或设置了密码保护，这样即使未经授权的第三方捡到了 U 盘或者设法访问了你的数据库，他们仍然无法访问个人数据。

    例如，一位人力资源总监遗失了一个加密的存储设备，里面存有员工的全名、医疗计划等信息。这时你可以评估认为技术保护例外适用，因为该设备是加密的。也就是说，即使有人捡到了这个 U 盘，他们也不太可能访问到个人数据。所以同样，你不需要通知个人，但仍然必须通知 PDPC。

  - [18:58:34–19:00:27] **Instructions Not to Notify and PDPC Waivers**

    One other situation where you should not notify the individuals is that sometimes, after you notify the PDPC, they will tell you not to notify the individuals yet. Sometimes this is because they want to conduct their own investigation first, or the police want to conduct an investigation, and notifying individuals might tip someone off.

    One other situation where you don't have to notify is if you manage to convince the PDPC to give you a waiver. You don't have to notify individuals if you obtain a waiver from the PDPC, which it may grant under some exceptional circumstances. For example, there are some overriding national interests, or there are some investigations going on that conflict with the requirement to notify the affected individuals.

    There was also a case where the PDPC granted a waiver because the organisation, despite its efforts, was unable to obtain the contact details of certain overseas-based individuals. What happened in this case was that the organisation suffered a data breach. It tried to reach out to the overseas individuals, but for some of them, it simply was not able to find their contact details. So it wrote to the PDPC to ask for a waiver for these specific individuals, because it was impossible for it to notify them.

    另一种你不应当通知个人的情况是：有时在你通知 PDPC 之后，他们会告诉你暂时不要通知个人。这有时是因为他们想先进行自己的调查，或者警方想进行调查，而通知个人可能会打草惊蛇。

    另一种你不必通知的情况是，你设法说服 PDPC 给予你豁免（waiver）。如果你获得了 PDPC 的豁免，就不必通知个人，PDPC 可能会在某些特殊情况下给予豁免。例如，存在某些压倒性的国家利益，或者正在进行的某些调查与通知受影响个人的要求相冲突。

    还有一个案例，PDPC 给予了豁免，原因是该组织尽管做出了努力，仍无法获得某些海外个人的联系方式。这个案例中，组织发生了数据泄露，它尝试联系海外的个人，但其中一些人它根本找不到联系方式。于是它致函 PDPC，请求就这些特定个人给予豁免，因为它不可能通知到他们。

  - [19:00:27–19:02:08] **How to Notify Individuals and What to Include**

    Let's say you are required to notify the affected individuals. How do you go about doing it? The law itself doesn't stipulate the method of communication, so basically you should select an appropriate mode of communication.

    For example, in this case, we have a voluntary welfare organisation that somehow lost its data because documents were sold to a rag-and-bone man. So it needs to notify the individuals. Here, it assesses that because the individuals could be significantly distressed, since the personal data that was lost is sensitive, it decides to notify individuals using personal phone calls rather than just sending an email. That's an example of choosing the appropriate form of communication.

    Then, what should your notification include? This is set out in the PDPA, but I won't require you to know all the full details. Basically, you should state the facts about the data breach, so what happened; how you handled the data breach, so your response to it; plus the contact details of your representative, usually the DPO, if the individual wants to contact you.

    Okay. Any questions so far?

    假设你需要通知受影响的个人，你该怎么做？法律本身没有规定沟通方式，所以基本上你应该选择一种合适的沟通方式。

    举个例子，这里有一个志愿福利组织，它因为文件被卖给了收旧货的人（rag-and-bone man）而丢失了数据。所以它需要通知相关个人。在这里，它评估认为，由于丢失的个人数据很敏感，这些人可能会感到极大困扰，因此它决定通过亲自打电话的方式通知个人，而不仅仅是发一封电子邮件。这就是选择合适沟通形式的一个例子。

    那么，你的通知应该包含什么？这在 PDPA 中有规定，但我不要求你们知道全部细节。基本上，你应该说明数据泄露的事实，也就是发生了什么；你如何处理这次泄露，也就是你的应对措施；再加上你的代表的联系方式，通常是数据保护官（DPO），以便个人想联系你时可以联系。

    好，到目前为止有什么问题吗？

  - [19:02:08–19:04:09] **Q&A: Breach at a Payroll Vendor (Data Intermediary)**

    A student asks about a scenario where an organisation in Singapore uses a payroll vendor that holds its personal data, and a breach happens at the vendor. Who has the duty to assess: the vendor, or the organisation the vendor works for? And say the payroll vendor notifies the organisation; do these rules on the duty to assess and the duty to notify apply to the vendor or to the organisation?

    I think that's a good question, which I will actually address as one of the special cases, so I'll just go through that one now. The payroll vendor in your example would be a data intermediary, because it is processing your HR information, your employee information, on your behalf. What happens if there's a data intermediary involved? The data intermediary is not responsible for fulfilling these obligations. Its only obligation here is that when it discovers a data breach, it must notify the data controller, which is you: a data breach has occurred. And then it is for the data controller to conduct the assessment and to notify. Of course, the data controller will need information from the data intermediary, but it's not the intermediary's job to conduct the assessment. And the 30-day period starts from the day when we, the organisation, find out.

    一位学生问了这样一个情境：新加坡的某个组织使用一家薪资服务供应商，该供应商持有该组织的个人数据，而泄露发生在供应商那里。谁有评估义务：是供应商，还是供应商所服务的组织？再假设薪资供应商通知了该组织，那么评估义务和通知义务的这些规则，适用于供应商还是组织？

    我觉得这是个好问题，我其实会把它作为特殊情况之一来讲，所以现在就讲一下。你例子中的薪资供应商属于数据中介（data intermediary），因为它代表你处理你的人力资源信息、你的员工信息。如果涉及数据中介会怎样？数据中介不负责履行这些义务。它在这里唯一的义务是，当它发现数据泄露时，必须通知数据控制者（data controller），也就是你：发生了数据泄露。然后由数据控制者进行评估并作出通知。当然，数据控制者需要从数据中介那里获取信息，但进行评估不是中介的工作。而三十天的期限是从我们，也就是该组织，得知泄露的那天开始计算的。

  - [19:04:09–19:06:30] **Q&A: Undiscovered Breaches and Internal Breaches Involving Sensitive Data**

    Any other questions?

    A student asks what happens if the organisation never finds out about a breach. If you never find out about the data breach, let's say the data breach just happens and slips under the radar and you don't notice it, then this obligation is not triggered. If it so happens that you simply didn't notice the breach, then the duty to assess does not even arise.

    Another student refers back to what I said just now about internal breaches and asks about the case where there is an internal data breach. Here, if it's a purely internal data breach, then it does not count as a notifiable data breach, in which case there is no duty to notify.

    Any other questions?

    A student follows up: what if it's internal, but the information is sensitive? Sometimes people send sensitive information to HR that they absolutely shouldn't.

    Okay, so let's say, for example, you accidentally send your own HR department some information that you should not have sent them. I think this would also count as internal, because nothing has left the company. So this will not be notifiable, even if the data is sensitive.

    Okay. Any other questions?

    还有其他问题吗？

    一位学生问，如果组织始终没有发现泄露会怎样。如果你从未发现数据泄露，比如说泄露发生了，却悄无声息地没被察觉，你没有注意到，那么这项义务就不会被触发。如果你恰好根本没有注意到泄露，那么评估义务甚至都不会产生。

    另一位学生提到我刚才讲的内部泄露，问发生内部数据泄露的情况。在这里，如果是纯粹的内部数据泄露，那么它不算须通知的数据泄露，这种情况下就没有通知义务。

    还有其他问题吗？

    一位学生追问：如果是内部的，但信息很敏感呢？有时候人们会把绝对不该发的敏感信息发给人力资源部门。

    好，比如说，你不小心把一些不该发的信息发给了你们自己公司的人力资源部门。我认为这也算内部泄露，因为没有任何东西离开公司。所以即使数据很敏感，这也不属于须通知的泄露。

    好，还有其他问题吗？

  - [19:06:30–19:09:21] **Special Case: Breaches Involving More Than One Organisation**

    We talked about the duty to assess and the duty to notify, and I also mentioned one special case, which is what happens if there's an intermediary involved. Another special case is where the data breach involves more than one organisation, a group of organisations that all suffer the same data breach.

    What happens then? The basic answer is that each organisation is responsible for its own assessment and notification, but there are certain shortcuts you can take. What the PDPC says is that each organisation is independently responsible for complying with the data breach notification obligation, but one of them can take the lead, usually the one most closely connected with the breach, in conducting the assessment. Each organisation must then come to its own conclusion on whether the assessment is correct.

    So that's assessment. When it comes to the duty to notify, this is also individual: each organisation has to notify the PDPC. But again, there can be a shortcut, because when it comes to notifying individuals, the PDPC can say, okay, I don't need all of you to send the same email to all the individuals; the main organisation can do it. So that's what happens if there is more than one organisation.

    It's a fairly long example here, but I'll just summarise it. In this scenario, ABC, DEF and GHI come up with a joint membership scheme. Let's say one of the members alerts ABC that there is some sort of data breach. What ABC can do is agree to take the lead in conducting the assessment. In this case, ABC determines the breach is notifiable. Then DEF and GHI look at the assessment and come to their own conclusions, and let's say they agree. Then each of them must notify the PDPC separately, sending its own notification, though they can do it with a common template if they want. And finally, in this case, the PDPC decides that the lead organisation is best placed to notify the members about the data breach. So that's an example involving multiple organisations.

    我们讲了评估义务和通知义务，我也提到了一种特殊情况，即涉及数据中介时会怎样。另一种特殊情况是数据泄露涉及不止一个组织，也就是一组组织都遭受了同一起数据泄露。

    那会怎样？基本答案是，每个组织各自负责自己的评估和通知，但可以采取一些简便做法。PDPC 的说法是，每个组织都各自独立负责遵守数据泄露通知义务，但其中一个组织可以牵头进行评估，通常是与泄露关系最密切的那一个。然后每个组织都必须就评估是否正确得出自己的结论。

    这是评估部分。至于通知义务，也是各自负责的：每个组织都必须通知 PDPC。但同样可以有简便做法，因为在通知个人时，PDPC 可以说，好，我不需要你们每家都给所有个人发同一封邮件，由主要的那个组织来做就行。这就是涉及多个组织时的情况。

    这里的例子比较长，我简单概括一下。在这个情境中，ABC、DEF 和 GHI 共同推出了一个联合会员计划。假设其中一位会员提醒 ABC 发生了某种数据泄露。ABC 可以同意牵头进行评估。在这个例子里，ABC 认定该泄露须通知。然后 DEF 和 GHI 审阅评估结果，得出各自的结论，假设他们都同意。接着他们每家都必须分别通知 PDPC，各自提交自己的通知，不过如果愿意，可以使用一个共同的模板。最后，在这个例子中，PDPC 决定由牵头组织来通知会员关于这次数据泄露的情况最为合适。这就是一个涉及多个组织的例子。

  - [19:09:21–19:10:52] **Q&A: Organisations Across Different Countries**

So in the scenario where you have a data breach that affects a group of organisations, one in Singapore and one somewhere else, and I'm kind of looking ahead to the next topic here: if that foreign organisation has no processing taking place in Singapore, if everything it's doing is, let's say, in Malaysia, then the PDPC will not touch it, because that's for the Malaysian data protection authority. The PDPC mostly looks at whether there is data processing or storage happening here, for instance in a data centre.

    Any other questions?

    这是个有意思的问题，我认为它涉及另一个话题，即数据保护法的域外效力（extraterritorial reach）。在一起数据泄露影响一组组织的情境中，一家在新加坡，一家在别处，这里我有点提前讲下一个话题了：如果那家外国组织在新加坡没有任何数据处理活动，比如它的所有业务都在马来西亚，那么 PDPC 不会去管它，因为那是马来西亚数据保护机构的事。PDPC 主要看的是这里是否有数据处理或存储活动，比如在数据中心里。

    还有其他问题吗？

  - [19:10:52–19:12:58] **How to Report and Comparisons with Other Jurisdictions**

    The next thing is a fairly practical one: how you are supposed to report. The very short answer is to click on this link. Basically, it's a step-by-step process that goes through what we already went through, showing you what you should do in the event of a data breach. So you just go to this webpage and submit your notification.

    Now, as usual, I want to draw some comparisons with other jurisdictions. Other jurisdictions also have similar data breach notification rules, although data breach notification tends to be a more recent innovation, so not every jurisdiction will have it. Increasingly, though, we are seeing more and more jurisdictions add data breach notification rules to their data protection laws.

    Just to give you a couple of examples, we can look at the EU GDPR, Article 33, which says that in the case of a data breach, the controller shall, without undue delay and not later than 72 hours after becoming aware of it, notify the supervisory authority. So you can see here the timeline in the EU is quite different: seventy-two hours from the time of the breach. Similarly, Article 34 says that if the breach is likely to result in a high risk to individuals, then the individuals also need to be notified.

    So that was the data breach notification obligation.

    接下来是一个相当实际的问题：你应该如何报告。最简短的答案就是点击这个链接。基本上，它是一个分步骤的流程，把我们刚才讲过的内容走一遍，告诉你发生数据泄露时应该怎么做。所以你只要打开这个网页，提交你的通知就行。

    现在，和往常一样，我想和其他司法管辖区做一些比较。其他司法管辖区也有类似的数据泄露通知规则，不过数据泄露通知往往是比较新的制度创新，所以并不是每个司法管辖区都有。但越来越多的司法管辖区正在把数据泄露通知规则加入其数据保护法中。

    举几个例子，我们可以看欧盟 GDPR 第 33 条，它规定在发生数据泄露时，控制者应当在不无故拖延的情况下，且不迟于知悉后 72 小时内，通知监管机构（supervisory authority）。所以你们可以看到，欧盟的时间线很不一样：从泄露发生起七十二小时。同样，第 34 条规定，如果泄露可能对个人造成高风险，那么也需要通知个人。

    以上就是数据泄露通知义务。

  - [19:12:58–19:14:37] **General Management of Data Breaches: Preparation (Not Examinable)**

    I also want to touch upon how to handle data breaches more generally, but I'm not going to test this, because technically this is not an obligation. This is more about good practices when managing data breaches. Here you can look at the guidelines on managing data breaches, which are uploaded on Canvas. I'm just summarising what they say.

    What the PDPC recommends are two steps to prepare for data breaches. First, put in place monitoring tools to detect data breaches: for example, you monitor your traffic, or use security cameras to watch places that contain personal data.

    Second, you should have a data breach management plan, but it doesn't need to be very complicated. Essentially, what you need to do in this plan is to explain to your staff what a data breach is, and tell your staff what to do. Then create a method for staff to report a data breach when it occurs; usually this means emailing the DPO. Then prepare a strategy for responding to data breaches, which you'll see on the next slide. And finally, identify a data breach management team whose job is to respond to the data breach.

    我还想讲一下更一般性的数据泄露处理，但这部分我不会考，因为严格来说这不是一项义务，更多是管理数据泄露的良好实践。这里大家可以参考关于管理数据泄露的指南，已经上传到 Canvas 上了。我只是概括一下其中的内容。

    PDPC 建议用两个步骤来为数据泄露做准备。第一，部署监控工具以发现数据泄露：比如监控你的网络流量，或者用监控摄像头看守存放个人数据的地方。

    第二，你应该制定一份数据泄露管理计划，但不需要很复杂。基本上，这份计划要做的是向员工解释什么是数据泄露，并告诉员工该怎么做。然后建立一个让员工在泄露发生时进行报告的渠道，通常就是给数据保护官发邮件。接着准备一套应对数据泄露的策略，大家会在下一张幻灯片看到。最后，确定一个数据泄露管理团队，负责应对数据泄露。

  - [19:14:37–19:15:54] **The C.A.R.E. Approach**

    So then, how do you actually respond to the data breach? The recommended approach is the CARE approach. C, A, R, E stands for contain, assess, report, evaluate.

    The first step, of course, is to contain. When you discover a data breach, the first thing you do is to make sure you contain it, to prevent more data from being compromised, and take steps to prevent harm from occurring. This is the very first step.

    After you've done that, you move on to the assessment, which is where you look at your containment steps to see if they were successful, and you assess the data breach itself to determine whether it is notifiable.

    The next step, which is part of your data breach notification obligation, is to actually report the data breach to the PDPC and sometimes to individuals.

    The last step is evaluate, which is where you look at your response to the data breach and see what you can do in future to prevent similar breaches.

    那么，你实际上该如何应对数据泄露？推荐的方法是 CARE 方法。C、A、R、E 分别代表控制（contain）、评估（assess）、报告（report）、评价（evaluate）。

    第一步当然是控制。当你发现数据泄露时，你首先要做的就是确保控制住泄露，防止更多数据受到危害，并采取措施防止损害发生。这是最首要的一步。

    完成之后，你就进入评估环节，也就是检查你的控制措施是否成功，并评估数据泄露本身，判断它是否须通知。

    下一步，这也是你的数据泄露通知义务的一部分，就是实际向 PDPC 报告数据泄露，有时也要向个人报告。

    最后一步是评价，也就是回顾你对这次数据泄露的应对，看看将来可以做些什么来防止类似的泄露。

    So in this situation, there are a couple of questions. First, what do you need to do to comply with the data breach notification obligation? And second, more generally, how would you manage this data breach? Maybe we can take about five minutes to think about this question, and after that we can discuss.

    在这个案例中，Maggie 使用一个云端平台来存储数据。这个平台允许集成 AI 代理，Maggie 把她所有的文件都存在这个平台上。有一天 Maggie 告诉你："我可能不小心给我的 AI 代理下错了指令，它把我的文件副本发给了志愿者。在我把它停下来之前，我不确定还有谁收到了我的文件。"

    Okay, so I think we can discuss the two questions here. Let's start with the first question: in this scenario, what do you think you need to do to comply with the data breach notification obligation? Anyone want to suggest an answer?

    A student says that first, you need to make an assessment and figure out, for instance, whether there was significant harm or not, or whether the amounts are significant, so that would be the first step.

    Here you would identify first that there has been a data breach. The data has been sent to unauthorised persons, so this triggers the duty to assess. And the duty to assess is about either harm or scale. I think the question doesn't indicate how many people are affected, so you can't really tell what the scale is. How about harm? Do you think this data breach is one that is likely to cause significant harm?

    The student answers that because means-testing information is involved, they believe there could be significant harm, and that the fact that these people are victims might itself cause distress or embarrassment.

    Yes. So there are two ways you can look at this. For harm, you can see whether the data breach is deemed to cause significant harm because it falls within either of these combinations. Probably in this case, we might say it's the individual's name plus a type of prescribed personal data, which I think would probably be the net worth of the individual, if there's some sensitive financial information. So here you can assess that it is likely to be deemed significant harm based on the deeming provision.

    Alternatively, you can simply assess the actual harm, which means that even without looking at these categories, considering the facts in this scenario, you can probably conclude that the breach is likely to cause significant harm to the individuals in the form of distress, embarrassment, loss of reputation, etc. So either way, most likely this is a notifiable data breach.

    一位学生说，首先你需要进行评估，弄清楚比如说是否存在重大损害，或者数量是否重大，这应该是第一步。

    在这里你首先要认定已经发生了数据泄露。数据被发送给了未经授权的人，所以这触发了评估义务。而评估义务看的是损害或者规模。我觉得题目没有说明有多少人受影响，所以你没法真正判断规模。那损害呢？你认为这次数据泄露可能造成重大损害吗？

    是的。所以你可以从两个角度来看。就损害而言，你可以看这次数据泄露是否因为落入其中任一组合而被视为造成重大损害。在这个案例中，我们大概可以说是个人姓名加上某一类规定个人数据，我认为如果涉及一些敏感的财务信息，那大概就是个人的净资产。所以在这里，你可以基于这项"视为"条款（deeming provision）评估认为它很可能被视为造成重大损害。

    或者，你也可以直接评估实际损害，也就是说即使不看这些类别，考虑到本情境中的事实，你大概也可以得出结论：这次泄露可能以困扰、难堪、名誉受损等形式对个人造成重大损害。所以无论哪种方式，这很可能都是一次须通知的数据泄露。

  - [19:43:00–19:44:33] **Mishandling Offences and the Meaning of Authorisation**

    Next, we look at the provisions imposing criminal sanctions on individuals who deliberately mishandle personal data. These are the so-called mishandling offences, and this part will be quite short. There are three relevant offences: unauthorised disclosure, unauthorised use, and unauthorised re-identification.

    All of these offences assume that the individual concerned was not authorised to do the thing that he did. So what counts as authorisation? According to the PDPC, authorisation can come in any form. It can be spoken, it can be written, but it has to come from someone who is empowered to authorise within the organisation.

    There are some situations where authorisation is simply assumed. For example, if you are an employee, then you are authorised to act in accordance with your employer's policies and practices. So if you act within your job scope, it is assumed you have authority. Or if you are a service provider to an organisation, again you are assumed to have authorisation to act in accordance with your service contract. If you do not have this authorisation, then by definition, what you are doing is unlawful.

    接下来，我们看一下对故意不当处理个人数据的个人施加刑事制裁的条款。这就是所谓的不当处理罪（mishandling offences），这部分会比较短。相关的罪行有三项：未经授权披露（unauthorised disclosure）、未经授权使用（unauthorised use），以及未经授权再识别（unauthorised re-identification）。

    这些罪行都以相关个人没有获得授权去做他所做的事情为前提。那么什么算授权？根据 PDPC 的说法，授权可以是任何形式的：可以是口头的，也可以是书面的，但必须来自组织内有权作出授权的人。

    在某些情况下，授权是被直接推定的。例如，如果你是雇员，那么你被授权按照雇主的政策和惯例行事。所以只要你在职责范围内行事，就推定你有授权。又或者你是某个组织的服务提供商，同样推定你有权按照服务合同行事。如果你没有这种授权，那么按定义，你所做的就是不合法的。

  - [19:44:33–19:46:17] **The Three Offences**

    Let's look at the three offences. The first is the so-called unauthorised disclosure offence, which is an offence if the conditions you can see on the slide are met. First, the individual discloses personal data which was in the possession or under the control of the organisation. Number two, this disclosure was not authorised. And number three, the individual knew that it was not authorised, or was reckless as to whether it was authorised or not. So this is a situation where the individual knowingly discloses personal data even though they were not authorised to do so, and in this situation, the PDPA imposes direct individual criminal liability.

    The next offence is the unauthorised use offence. It is very similar to unauthorised disclosure, with the same few conditions, except there's an additional condition, which is that the individual either obtained a gain from their use, caused harm to another individual, or caused some loss to another individual. If the four conditions are met, again, this is an offence.

    Finally, you have the re-identification offence, which basically is when an individual re-identifies anonymised personal data when they were not authorised to do so.

    我们来看这三项罪行。第一项是所谓的未经授权披露罪，满足幻灯片上这些条件即构成犯罪。第一，个人披露了由组织拥有或控制的个人数据。第二，这一披露未经授权。第三，该个人知道披露未经授权，或者对是否获得授权持罔顾（reckless）态度。所以这是个人明知没有获得授权却仍披露个人数据的情形，在这种情况下，PDPA 直接施加个人刑事责任。

    下一项罪行是未经授权使用罪。它与未经授权披露非常相似，条件基本相同，只是多了一个条件：该个人通过使用获取了利益，或者对另一个人造成了损害，或者给另一个人造成了某种损失。如果满足这四个条件，同样构成犯罪。

    最后是再识别罪，基本上就是个人在未经授权的情况下，对已匿名化（anonymised）的个人数据进行再识别。

  - [19:46:17–19:48:13] **Defences, Including the Consent Defence**

    There are a few defences available. Let's say you have been charged under one of these offences; there are some defences you can rely on. One defence is if the personal data was actually already publicly available. Or the individual was permitted by law or some court order to do what they did. Or you had some reasonable belief that you had the legal right to do what you did.

    There are also some defences that are specific to some of the offences. As far as the unauthorised use and unauthorised disclosure offences are concerned, it is also a defence for the individual to show that the data subject had provided consent to the use.

    For example, you have a bank relationship manager who works within a bank and has built up some clients, and they want to jump ship to a different organisation. This relationship manager gets consent from the clients to move their data to the different organisation. Here, this is unauthorised use, because the first bank didn't authorise him to use the personal data when he moves to a different organisation. But this is not an offence, because the clients consented. He may be sued by the first bank for breach of confidence or trade secrets, but he won't be liable under this offence.

    有几种抗辩可以援引。假设你被控犯有其中一项罪行，你可以依赖一些抗辩。一种抗辩是该个人数据实际上已经是公开可得的。或者该个人是在法律或法院命令允许的情况下做了他所做的事。或者你有合理理由相信你有合法权利做你所做的事。

    还有一些抗辩是针对特定罪行的。就未经授权使用和未经授权披露这两项罪行而言，个人如果能证明数据主体已经同意该使用，也构成抗辩。

    举个例子，有一位银行客户经理，在一家银行工作，积累了一些客户，他想跳槽到另一家机构。这位客户经理征得了客户的同意，把他们的数据转移到新的机构。在这里，这属于未经授权使用，因为第一家银行并没有授权他在转到其他机构时使用这些个人数据。但这不构成犯罪，因为客户已经同意了。他可能会被第一家银行以违反保密义务（breach of confidence）或侵犯商业秘密为由起诉，但他不会因这项罪行承担责任。

  - [19:48:13–19:48:42] **Q&A: Form of Consent**

    A student asks, out of curiosity, whether this kind of consent has to be given orally or in writing, noting that we will come to consent later on.

    The PDPA doesn't require consent to be given in any particular form. Of course, as we discussed, if you want to prove that consent was given, you are most likely going to need some form of record. Okay. So that's the first specific defence.

    一位学生出于好奇问，这种同意必须以口头还是书面形式作出，并提到我们之后会讲到同意的问题。

    PDPA 并不要求同意以任何特定形式作出。当然，正如我们讨论过的，如果你想证明对方已经同意，你很可能需要某种形式的记录。好，这就是第一项特定抗辩。

  - [19:48:42–19:49:24] **Defences Specific to Unauthorised Re-identification**

    As far as unauthorised re-identification is concerned, there is a specific set of defences, basically for individuals who are just testing: testing the effectiveness of the anonymisation, or the integrity and confidentiality of the information or the systems holding it. This defence is meant to protect so-called white hat hackers who, in good faith, are testing the organisations' systems. So this is the defence for unauthorised re-identification.

    就未经授权再识别而言，有一套专门的抗辩，基本上是针对只是在做测试的个人：测试匿名化的有效性，或者测试信息或持有该信息的系统的完整性和保密性。这项抗辩旨在保护所谓的白帽黑客（white hat hackers），他们出于善意测试组织的系统。这就是针对未经授权再识别的抗辩。

  - [19:49:24–19:51:11] **Q&A: Penalties and the Knowledge Requirement**

    So that's all I will say regarding these mishandling offences. Any questions? It's quite a short part. Just bear in mind that this is the one situation where the PDPA directly places responsibility on the individual employee or staff member, rather than the organisation.

    A student asks what penalties come with these offences.

    I can't remember offhand. Usually, I think there is imprisonment and/or a fine.

    The student follows up, asking whether something more needs to be shown for an individual to be liable.

    Yes. If you look at the conditions of the offences, they all require that the individual either knew or was reckless as to whether there was authorisation. In such circumstances, generally, the individual is knowingly not acting in accordance with the organisation's best interests. So the earlier example I gave would not be an offence, because there we have a situation where she was just careless. She wasn't knowingly disclosing personal data; she was just careless in what she did.

    关于不当处理罪，我就讲这么多。有什么问题吗？这部分比较短。只要记住，这是 PDPA 直接把责任加在个人雇员或员工身上、而不是加在组织身上的唯一情形。

    一位学生问这些罪行的刑罚是什么。

    我一时记不起来。我想通常是监禁和/或罚款。

    这位学生追问，要让个人承担责任，是否还需要证明更多东西。

    是的。如果你看这些罪行的构成条件，它们都要求该个人知道或罔顾是否获得授权。在这种情况下，一般来说，该个人是明知地没有按照组织的最佳利益行事。所以我之前举的那个例子不会构成犯罪，因为那是一个她只是粗心大意的情形。她并不是明知地披露个人数据，只是在做事时粗心了。

  - [19:51:11–19:52:45] **The Retention Limitation Obligation**

    So that's the mishandling offences. The last bit we are going to look at, the third obligation that is related to security, is the retention limitation obligation.

    What is this obligation? It is an obligation for organisations to stop retaining personal data, or to remove the means by which the personal data can be associated with that particular individual, which means, basically, anonymisation. Organisations must either stop retaining documents containing personal data or anonymise them as soon as it is reasonable to assume that, number one, the purpose for which they collected the personal data is no longer served by the retention, and number two, there is no legal or business purpose for retaining it.

    To put it simply, once the organisation has no purpose for keeping the personal data, it has to stop keeping it. That's the idea behind retention limitation. And this is related to security because the less data you keep, the fewer vulnerabilities you have in terms of what is exposed in a breach.

    这就是不当处理罪。我们要看的最后一部分，也就是与安全相关的第三项义务，是保留限制义务（Retention Limitation Obligation）。

    这项义务是什么？它要求组织停止保留个人数据，或者去除能将个人数据与特定个人联系起来的手段，基本上也就是匿名化。只要可以合理认定：第一，保留个人数据已不再能服务于收集该数据的目的；第二，保留它已没有任何法律或商业目的，那么组织就必须尽快停止保留含有个人数据的文件，或者将其匿名化。

    简单来说，一旦组织没有保留个人数据的目的，就必须停止保留。这就是保留限制背后的理念。它之所以与安全相关，是因为你保留的数据越少，在发生泄露时可能被曝光的内容就越少，漏洞也就越少。

  - [19:52:45–19:54:24] **The Two Limbs and the Necessity Requirement**

    The key thing to bear in mind regarding this obligation is these two limbs. You must stop retaining if your original purpose for collection is no longer served, and it is not necessary for any legal or business purpose for you to retain the personal data.

    The first one, your original purposes, is very straightforward, because you yourself know what the original purposes were for which you collected the personal data. But once they've expired, you cannot rely on the first limb. The second limb is the more general one: is there any other legal or business purpose for you to keep the personal data? This is the one which is a bit more difficult. The PDPC has provided some examples, like to carry out business operations, for some business improvement purpose, or for research purposes.

    The thing you need to understand regarding this limb is that you need to show that retention is necessary, not just useful. It must be necessary for you to serve that purpose.

    关于这项义务，需要记住的关键是这两个要件。如果你最初收集数据的目的已不再能得到满足，并且保留个人数据对于任何法律或商业目的都不再必要，你就必须停止保留。

    第一个要件，即你的原始目的，非常直接，因为你自己知道当初收集个人数据的原始目的是什么。但一旦这些目的已经结束，你就不能依赖第一个要件了。第二个要件更具一般性：你是否还有其他法律或商业目的需要保留这些个人数据？这一项要难一些。PDPC 提供了一些例子，比如为了开展业务运营、为了某种业务改进目的，或者为了研究目的。

    关于这一要件，你需要理解的是，你必须证明保留是必要的，而不仅仅是有用的。它必须是实现该目的所必需的。

  - [19:54:24–19:55:48] **Examples of Valid Purposes and the Limitation Period**

    Some examples provided in the guidelines. The first example is a dance school collecting personal data of instructors and students. Here, the dance school retains the data, with the consent of the tutors and students, for the purpose of maintaining an alumni network. That's a valid purpose for keeping the personal data.

    Another example is a retailer who retains billing information for purposes of accounting. Again, this would be a valid business purpose, so there's no need to delete the personal data.

    Another commonly relied-upon purpose is basically the potential for a contractual lawsuit. Conveniently, there is a time limit for contractual lawsuits, which are civil. So often you will see organisations say that any information that may be useful if they get sued, they will hold on to for six years, because after six years, the Limitation Act applies, and the other party cannot sue anymore under that contract.

    指南中提供了一些例子。第一个例子是一所舞蹈学校收集了导师和学生的个人数据。在这里，舞蹈学校在导师和学生同意的情况下保留这些数据，用于维护校友网络。这是保留个人数据的有效目的。

    另一个例子是零售商为了会计目的保留账单信息。同样，这属于有效的商业目的，所以不需要删除个人数据。

    另一个常被援引的目的基本上是可能发生的合同诉讼。方便的是，合同诉讼作为民事诉讼是有时效限制的。所以你经常会看到组织说，任何在被起诉时可能有用的信息，他们都会保留六年，因为六年之后，《诉讼时效法》（Limitation Act）就会适用，对方就不能再依据该合同起诉了。

  - [19:55:48–19:57:52] **Anonymise Where Possible: Stylez Pte Ltd**

    As I mentioned, if you want to retain personal data for legal or business purposes, you need to prove necessity, and the implication is that if your purpose can be served with anonymised personal data, then you should anonymise it. You cannot just retain the personal data in its original form.

    The case that illustrates this is the Stylez Pte Ltd case. Here we have an organisation that runs a quotation and service comparison portal. It created a database, which was subsequently exposed by a cyberattack. The PDPC investigated whether the organisation was in breach of the retention limitation obligation by storing this database, and the organisation's argument was that it needed to retain the data for business analysis purposes, because it wanted to assess users' requirements and the timing of user inquiries to improve its business processes.

    The organisation's argument sounds plausible, right? It sounds like you do need to retain client data to conduct your market study or whatever. But the PDPC nevertheless said that this was a breach of the retention limitation obligation. The reason is that the business analysis purposes could be served even with anonymised personal data. It was not necessary to keep the personal data in identifiable form.

    正如我提到的，如果你想为了法律或商业目的保留个人数据，你需要证明其必要性，而这意味着，如果你的目的用匿名化的个人数据就能实现，那么你就应该将其匿名化。你不能只是以原始形式保留个人数据。

    说明这一点的案例是 Stylez Pte Ltd 案。这里有一个经营报价和服务比较门户网站的组织。它建立了一个数据库，后来这个数据库在一次网络攻击中被曝光。PDPC 调查了该组织存储这个数据库是否违反了保留限制义务，而该组织的理由是，它需要为了商业分析目的保留这些数据，因为它想评估用户的需求和用户咨询的时间分布，以改进其业务流程。

    这个组织的理由听起来很有道理，对吧？听上去为了开展市场研究之类的工作，你确实需要保留客户数据。但 PDPC 仍然认定这违反了保留限制义务。原因是，商业分析目的即使用匿名化的个人数据也能实现，没有必要以可识别的形式保留个人数据。

  - [19:57:52–19:58:51] **Complying: Retention Policies and Regular Review**

    So how do you go about complying with the retention limitation obligation? I guess the standard practice is to develop a retention policy indicating how long you are going to keep your personal data. This policy should set out explicitly the retention period, how many years. And if you are retaining the personal data indefinitely or for a very long time, then you should set out the reason why.

    In addition, you should also regularly review your personal data stores to determine whether you actually need the personal data, because some data may not be covered by your data retention policy. So you actually need to look at what data you have and determine whether you still need it.

    那么该如何遵守保留限制义务？我想标准做法是制定一份保留政策，说明你打算把个人数据保留多久。这份政策应明确列出保留期限，也就是多少年。如果你无限期或长期保留个人数据，就应该说明保留的理由。

    此外，你还应该定期审查你所存储的个人数据，以确定你是否真的需要这些数据，因为有些数据可能并不在你的数据保留政策覆盖范围之内。所以你实际上需要去看看你有哪些数据，判断你是否仍然需要它们。

  - [19:58:51–20:00:23] **Ways to Cease Retaining Personal Data**

    So let's say you've decided that you should not retain the personal data. How do you go about complying with retention limitation? There are several ways to cease to retain personal data, and you can choose whichever is most convenient. You can return the documents, transfer them to a different person, destroy the documents, or anonymise the personal data. With all of these options, the point is that you're no longer holding on to the personal data.

    The guidelines specify that if you just archive the personal data, meaning you put it somewhere else, or you deposit it with a third party that you control, this still counts as you retaining the personal data. You have to do a bit more to cease to retain it.

    In a situation where it is not clear whether you have ceased to retain the personal data, the PDPC will consider a few factors, like whether you intend to continue to use the personal data, what you would need to do to access it, whether you gave third parties any access, and whether you made a reasonable attempt to dispose of it. These are things which will be considered in a grey-area sort of situation.

    假设你已经决定不应再保留这些个人数据，那该如何遵守保留限制义务？停止保留个人数据有几种方式，你可以选择最方便的一种。你可以归还文件、将其转交给另一个人、销毁文件，或者将个人数据匿名化。所有这些做法的关键在于，你不再持有这些个人数据。

    指南明确规定，如果你只是把个人数据归档，也就是把它放到别的地方，或者存放在你所控制的第三方那里，这仍然算作你在保留个人数据。你必须多做一些才算停止保留。

    如果不清楚你是否已经停止保留个人数据，PDPC 会考虑几个因素，比如你是否打算继续使用这些个人数据、你需要做些什么才能访问它们、你是否让第三方有任何访问权限，以及你是否作出了合理努力去处置这些数据。在这种灰色地带的情况下，会考虑这些因素。

  - [20:00:23–20:01:16] **Comparisons with Other Jurisdictions**

    Again, to draw a comparison with other jurisdictions: retention limitation is something that most jurisdictions also impose. For example, if you look at the EU GDPR, Article 5, it says that personal data shall be kept in a form which permits identification of data subjects for no longer than necessary for the purposes for which the personal data is processed. That's very similar wording to Singapore. The Chinese version says that the retention period of personal information shall be the minimum period necessary for achieving the purposes of processing, except where required by law. So again, very similar.

    同样，我们和其他司法管辖区做个比较：大多数司法管辖区也规定了保留限制。比如欧盟 GDPR 第 5 条规定，个人数据以可识别数据主体的形式保存的时间，不得超过处理该个人数据之目的所必需的时间。这和新加坡的措辞非常相似。中国的版本规定，除法律另有规定外，个人信息的保存期限应当为实现处理目的所必要的最短时间。所以同样非常相似。

  - [20:01:16–20:02:30] **Q&A: Retention Limitation in the United States**

    A student asks a quick question: out of curiosity, do you know how this works in the US?

    In general, if you're talking about the US, it's a bit of a complicated situation, because at the federal level and the state level, there are separate legal regimes. At the federal level, there is no comprehensive data protection law. At the state level, only some of the US states have data protection laws, and the most prominent of all is the Californian one. I don't know offhand whether it imposes retention limitation, but I suspect it does. So the short answer is that if you're working in the US, you need to focus on the state laws rather than the federal level.

    一位学生快速问了一个问题：出于好奇，您知道美国在这方面是怎么规定的吗？

    总的来说，如果你说的是美国，情况有点复杂，因为联邦层面和州层面有各自独立的法律制度。在联邦层面，没有全面的数据保护法。在州层面，只有部分州有数据保护法，其中最突出的是加州的那部。我一时不确定它是否规定了保留限制，但我猜应该有。所以简短的回答是，如果你在美国工作，你需要关注的是州法，而不是联邦层面。

  - [20:02:30–20:05:10] **In-Class Exercise: Emotie and Retention Limitation**

    If there are no objections, I think we can probably end a bit earlier today, but we will end off with this question, which focuses on retention limitation. Let's look at what this scenario says.

    Emotie is a startup providing an AI-powered emotion detection service. Specifically, what Emotie does is help its clients analyse facial images and detect the emotions expressed in those faces. For example, if you have a client running some vending machine service that wants to see the emotions of its customers as they select different items, that's what Emotie tries to help with. So Emotie's clients will send facial images to Emotie, who will then process the images and return scores for various emotions.

    In order to enhance public and client confidence in its services, Emotie commits in all of its contracts to keep facial images strictly confidential and to process them solely to provide emotion detection services. It promises that it will only use the facial data for providing its emotion detection services.

    In this scenario, Emotie stores the following personal data. First, facial images, of which it receives thousands every day. It also, of course, has employee files; here we have a fairly small staff of ten employees. And finally, client inquiries, which are basically questions sent by clients from time to time.

    The question you need to answer here is: how should Emotie comply with the retention limitation obligation in respect of this personal data? Again, just take five minutes to think about how you'd respond to this question, and we'll discuss it after.

    如果大家没有异议，我想今天我们大概可以稍微早一点下课，不过我们最后还要讨论这道题，它聚焦于保留限制。我们来看一下这个情境。

    Emotie 是一家提供 AI 情绪识别服务的初创公司。具体来说，Emotie 帮助客户分析面部图像，识别这些面孔所表达的情绪。比如，如果有一个经营自动售货机服务的客户，想了解顾客在选择不同商品时的情绪，Emotie 就是帮忙做这个的。所以 Emotie 的客户会把面部图像发给 Emotie，Emotie 处理这些图像，然后返回各种情绪的评分。

    为了增强公众和客户对其服务的信心，Emotie 在所有合同中都承诺对面部图像严格保密，并且只为提供情绪识别服务而处理这些图像。它承诺只会把面部数据用于提供情绪识别服务。

    在这个情境中，Emotie 存储了以下个人数据。第一，面部图像，它每天会收到成千上万张。当然，它还有员工档案，这里的员工规模比较小，只有十名员工。最后是客户咨询，基本上就是客户不时发来的问题。

    你们在这里需要回答的问题是：Emotie 应该如何就这些个人数据遵守保留限制义务？同样，花五分钟想一想你会如何回答这个问题，之后我们再讨论。

So here, six years is likely to be the appropriate duration to retain ex-employee personal data.

    一位学生在回答时，先讲了面部图像。他指出，该组织已经明确承诺只将数据用于一个非常具体的目的，而不用于其他任何目的。所以，尽管从技术上讲保留这些图像可能存在一些商业目的，但由于它已经承诺不会将这些个人数据用于任何其他目的，一旦它用这些图像算出情绪评分并提交给客户，就不能再保留这些面部图像了。

    这位学生接着讲员工档案，说保留这些是可以的，因为经营公司需要它们，比如处理工资之类的事情。

    好，我认为对于员工来说，你显然有保留其数据的商业目的。

    这位学生补充说，这也可以延伸到大约六年，以防有人提出索赔。

    是的。所以这里你需要把在职员工和离职员工区分开。对于在职员工，你显然要保留数据，因为你需要用它来管理员工。但在他们离职之后，你大概可以依赖六年的诉讼时效期，以防他们回头提出某种投诉或诉讼，比如说工资没付对，或者受到了不当对待之类的。所以在这里，六年很可能是保留离职员工个人数据的合适期限。

  - [20:13:08–20:14:25] **Exercise Discussion: Client Inquiries and Other Purposes**

    What else?

    A student talks about the client inquiries. They suggest there are basically two different types: one of a trivial nature, just correspondence that doesn't need to be kept, and complaints, which probably need to be kept longer.

    Okay, so for client inquiries, the question becomes a bit more vague. The original purpose is to address the client inquiry, which has already been fulfilled. I suppose the question then is: are there any other legal or business purposes for retaining these inquiries? I guess one legal purpose would again be to potentially defend yourself against legal disputes. Is there any other purpose for retaining them?

    还有什么？

    一位学生谈到客户咨询。他认为基本上可以分成两类：一类是琐碎性质的，只是普通往来通信，不需要保留；另一类是投诉，大概需要保留更长时间。

    好，对于客户咨询，这个问题就变得有点模糊了。原始目的是处理客户咨询，而这个目的已经实现了。那么我想问题就是：是否还有其他法律或商业目的需要保留这些咨询？我想一个法律上的目的同样是可能需要在法律纠纷中为自己辩护。保留它们还有其他目的吗？

  - [20:14:25–20:16:10] **Exercise Discussion: Training, Business Improvement and Anonymisation**

    A student suggests that in this case, the organisation could consider anonymising some of the inquiries.

    If your purpose is just to train your employees in how to respond to client inquiries, then you can't retain the data in identifiable form.

    A student raises the point that these inquiries come in by email, and people don't go back to them.

    Another student offers a further argument: we could argue for business improvement. If the same questions come in again and again, it means that maybe your website is not clear and isn't explaining things in a really understandable way. So you could do that, and then maybe anonymise the inquiries and use them for some time.

    Okay, but it's the same thing as training. This kind of use case probably does not require you to keep the data indefinitely. You can probably extract the relevant information from the inquiry without retaining the personal data.

    一位学生提出，在这种情况下，组织可以考虑将部分咨询匿名化。

    如果你的目的只是培训员工如何回应客户咨询，那么你就不能以可识别的形式保留这些数据。

    一位学生提出，这些咨询是通过电子邮件进来的，而人们不会回头去处理它们。

    另一位学生提出了进一步的论点：我们可以主张这是为了业务改进。如果同样的问题一再出现，那可能意味着你的网站不够清楚，没有以真正易懂的方式解释清楚。所以你可以这样做，然后也许把这些咨询匿名化，再使用一段时间。

    好，但这和培训是一样的道理。这类用途大概不需要你无限期地保留数据。你大概可以从咨询中提取相关信息，而不必保留其中的个人数据。

  - [20:16:10–20:18:28] **Retention Limitation and Email Inboxes**

    But to go back to the email point, this is where I suppose this particular obligation clashes with how most organisations operate. How most organisations operate is that whenever something goes into your email inbox, these days it stays in your inbox indefinitely, unless you run out of space. I think that is how most organisations operate, which, by right, is not something organisations should do. But as far as emails are concerned, you can see how this is going to be very difficult. If you want to strip out specifically those emails with personal data in them, that's going to take a lot more effort.

    So there are, I suppose, two possible ways to answer this question. The first one is to kind of hope we don't get caught. But the second approach relates to email addresses and communications through email, which we discussed in the personal data topic. The PDPC's view seems to be that just because person A has said something in an email in response to person B, this does not make the email personal data. Only where the email actually contains information describing these individuals does it start to count as personal data.

    So that's one possible approach: to say that most emails won't count, unless the email or the attachment contains personal details. Maybe the reason why the PDPC took this position in the first place is that if you just broadly say all emails are personal data, then basically every organisation is required to purge them, and that is not practical.

    不过回到电子邮件这一点，我想这正是这项义务与大多数组织的运作方式发生冲突的地方。大多数组织的做法是，只要东西进了你的邮箱，如今它就会无限期地留在里面，除非你的空间用完了。我认为大多数组织就是这样运作的，而按理说，这并不是组织应该做的。但就电子邮件而言，你可以看出这会非常困难。如果你要专门把那些含有个人数据的邮件挑出来，那会费很大的工夫。

    所以我想，这个问题有两种可能的回答方式。第一种就是多少指望我们不会被抓到。第二种方法则涉及电子邮件地址和通过电子邮件进行的通信，我们在个人数据那个主题里讨论过。PDPC 的看法似乎是，仅仅因为 A 在回复 B 的邮件中说了些什么，并不会使这封邮件成为个人数据。只有当邮件确实包含描述这些个人的信息时，它才开始算作个人数据。

    所以这是一种可能的方法：认为大多数邮件不算个人数据，除非邮件或附件中包含个人信息。也许 PDPC 当初采取这一立场的原因就是，如果你笼统地说所有邮件都是个人数据，那么基本上每个组织都必须清除它们，这是不现实的。

  - [20:18:28–20:19:12] **Facial Images Without Other Identifiers**

    A student raises a further point, giving business model development as an example.

    I don't think that is going to work. I think that once you send someone's facial image, even without any other identifier, that will still count as personal data.

    一位学生提出了进一步的观点，举了业务模式开发作为例子。

    我认为这行不通。我认为一旦你发送了某人的面部图像，即使没有任何其他识别信息，它仍然算作个人数据。

  - [20:19:12–20:21:27] **A Student's Case for Retaining the Data**

    Another student wants to mention a couple of points in support of retaining the data rather than removing it. For client queries, because they're pretty generic, the student thinks that if these are active clients, it definitely makes sense to keep the queries for as long as they remain clients, whether they come by email or on paper.

    For facial images, the student thinks it depends on what sort of service is being provided. Trying to visualise the interface: maybe the client uploads a photo and a score comes back, but clients often also want to refer back to what they've submitted. So if part of the service is to provide a browsing or scrolling history, then for active clients who want to see what they sent previously, it makes sense to keep those facial images.

    For employee files, the student agrees with the six-year point, but also thinks there might be occasions where retention could be really long. Say the company parted on good terms with its employees, over a long period of time, maybe more than six years, maybe ten years. Then one of them applies for a job somewhere else, and the other company comes back to Emotie and asks how that employee was and whether they performed well. The student presumes Emotie would need to store at least some information, something basic like whether this was a good or bad employee. It would be identifiable personal data, but it would make sense for them to keep, maybe not everything but some summary of the employee, because this happens very frequently in the public service, regardless of whether it's five, ten or twenty years later.

    另一位学生想提几点支持保留数据而非删除数据的理由。对于客户咨询，由于内容相当笼统，这位学生认为如果是活跃客户，只要他们还是客户，保留这些咨询肯定是合理的，无论是电子邮件还是纸质的。

    对于面部图像，这位学生认为这取决于所提供的是什么样的服务。他试着设想一下界面：客户也许上传一张照片，然后返回一个评分，但客户往往也想回头查看自己提交过的内容。所以如果服务的一部分是提供浏览或滚动历史记录，那么对于想查看自己之前发送过什么的活跃客户来说，保留这些面部图像是合理的。

    对于员工档案，这位学生同意六年的说法，但也认为在某些情况下保留期可能会非常长。比如说公司和员工好聚好散，时间过去很久，也许超过六年，也许十年。然后其中一人去别处求职，那家公司回头来问 Emotie，这名员工表现怎么样、工作做得好不好。这位学生推测 Emotie 至少需要保存一些信息，一些基本的东西，比如这是个好员工还是差员工。这会是可识别的个人数据，但保留下来是合理的，也许不是全部，而是对该员工的一些概要，因为这种事在公共部门非常常见，不管是五年、十年还是二十年之后。

  - [20:21:27–20:24:47] **Responding to the Case for Retention: Stretching Purposes and References**

    So I think there are three points that were mentioned. The first one is client inquiries: should we not retain them for as long as the client remains a client, forming part of the record? This one I will put a question mark on, because if you've already addressed the client inquiry, it's hard to see what other use you have for it. Presumably, the client side would keep a copy of that inquiry, so maybe you don't need it as part of the record. But it may depend on what your business is doing with it.

    For facial images, there's an interesting train of thought, which is: what does it mean to process the facial images solely to provide emotion detection services? Someone who wants to play with words could well say that providing emotion detection services is not just sending you the score at the moment; it could extend to other things beyond just providing the service. So this potentially allows the startup to stretch the original purpose by reinterpreting it. That's one possibility if you want to do other things with the facial images, although there is a limit as to how far you can stretch it.

    Then, in terms of retaining the employee files for the purposes of recommendations, as the student confirms, I think this one is genuinely uncertain. If you're retaining the employee files for the purpose of providing references, effectively your timeline is as long as the employee's working life, as long as they're looking for jobs, which is quite a long duration. So in that scenario, if you want to cover yourself, you would probably do something like ask the employee for permission to retain the personal data for the purposes of providing references. Or, when developing your data retention policy, indicate that you are retaining the file for this particular purpose, and that's why you retain it for a long period of time. So if you get investigated for whatever reason about your compliance with this obligation, you have something to show to explain why you're keeping the record.

    我想刚才提到了三点。第一点是客户咨询：只要客户仍是客户，我们是否应该把这些咨询作为记录的一部分保留下来？这一点我要打个问号，因为如果你已经处理了客户的咨询，就很难看出你还有什么其他用途。客户那边大概也会保留一份咨询的副本，所以也许你不需要把它作为记录的一部分。但这可能取决于你的业务拿它做什么。

    对于面部图像，有一个有意思的思路，那就是："仅为提供情绪识别服务而处理面部图像"到底是什么意思？想玩文字游戏的人完全可以说，提供情绪识别服务并不只是当下把评分发给你，它可以延伸到提供服务之外的其他事情。所以这有可能让这家初创公司通过重新解读来扩展原始目的。如果你想用面部图像做其他事情，这是一种可能性，尽管扩展的程度是有限度的。

    然后，关于为了推荐（也就是这位学生确认的用途）而保留员工档案，我认为这一点确实不确定。如果你为了提供推荐信或背景查询（references）而保留员工档案，那么你的保留期实际上就和员工的职业生涯一样长，只要他们还在找工作，这是相当长的时间。所以在这种情况下，如果你想保护自己，你大概会采取一些措施，比如请员工允许你为提供推荐的目的保留其个人数据。或者，在制定数据保留政策时，写明你是为了这个特定目的保留档案的，这就是你长期保留的原因。这样，如果你因为某种原因就是否遵守这项义务而受到调查，你就有东西可以拿出来解释你为什么保留这些记录。

  - [20:24:47–20:26:42] **Converting Facial Images into Numerical Data**

    A student asks: what about the facial images being processed and then stored as some kind of internal data?

    If you have converted it into intermediate data, and let's say you cannot reconvert it back into a facial image, I suppose it stops counting as personal data. Although, again, thinking about it, that's basically biometrics, right? Which does count as personal data. Is that what you're referring to, the conversion of the facial image into numerical data?

    The student confirms, explaining that in machine learning we always convert everything to numbers for the models. If you take one picture and a second picture that is very similar, both will have been converted into similar numbers.

    So in that scenario, if it's not biometrics, where it's not used to verify identity, then I don't think it counts as personal data.

    一位学生问：如果面部图像经过处理后，以某种内部数据的形式存储呢？

    如果你已经把它转换成中间数据，并且假设你无法把它还原成面部图像，我想它就不再算作个人数据了。不过再想一想，那基本上就是生物识别数据（biometrics），对吧？而生物识别数据确实算作个人数据。你指的是不是把面部图像转换成数值数据？

    这位学生确认了，并解释说在机器学习中，我们总是把所有东西转换成数字供模型使用。如果你拿一张照片和另一张非常相似的照片，两者都会被转换成相似的数字。

    所以在这种情况下，如果它不是用于验证身份的生物识别数据，那么我认为它不算个人数据。

  - [20:26:42–20:28:19] **Retaining Images to Verify Scores**

    A student asks a question. In this case, the purpose of retention is the score, but as a client, you would obviously need to know whether the score is correct. Is retention needed for audit purposes, for instance if a client complains that the scores look random, or disputes how many images were actually processed?

    Yes, so that would be a situation, as mentioned earlier, where you might try to squeeze that into your original purpose. Emotie might say that when it said it would process your facial images to provide emotion detection services, that also includes showing you that what it's doing is effective, and so on.

    The student follows up: so then it won't be immediate deletion?

    Yes. If you do stretch the purpose this way, then you may not need to delete immediately. But here they will consider what specifically you intend to do with the facial images.

    一位学生提了一个问题。在这个案例中，保留的目的是评分，但作为客户，你显然需要知道评分是否正确。是否需要为了审计目的而保留，比如客户投诉评分看起来很随机，或者对实际处理了多少张图像有争议？

    是的，这就是我之前提到的那种情形，你可能会试图把它塞进你的原始目的里。Emotie 可能会说，当它说会为提供情绪识别服务而处理你的面部图像时，这也包括向你证明它所做的是有效的，等等。

    这位学生追问：那这样就不用立即删除了？

    是的。如果你这样扩展目的，那么你可能不需要立即删除。但在这里，他们会考虑你具体打算用这些面部图像做什么。

  - [20:28:19–20:29:36] **Obtaining Consent from the Individuals**

    A student asks whether Emotie could get consent to retain the images.

    Here there is no direct interaction with the individuals, because what we have are clients, businesses, obtaining these pictures somehow with their cameras and transmitting the data. So it will be difficult for Emotie to separately get consent from the individuals for whatever other purpose it wants. It is possible that if you can contact the individuals and get their consent to use that personal data for some other purpose, then you can maybe retain the facial images for those individuals. But practically, in this situation, you can't really contact any individual. There is no way of reaching out to them.

    一位学生问 Emotie 能否征得同意来保留这些图像。

    在这里，与个人之间没有直接接触，因为我们面对的是客户，也就是企业，它们用自己的摄像头以某种方式获取这些照片，然后传输数据。所以 Emotie 很难为了它想要的任何其他目的另外征得个人的同意。如果你能联系到这些个人，并征得他们同意将个人数据用于其他目的，那么你也许可以为这些人保留面部图像。但实际上，在这种情况下，你根本联系不到任何个人，没有办法接触到他们。

  - [20:29:36–20:31:04] **Using the Data to Improve the Model**

    A student asks whether it would be enough to say that all the data is helping the business.

    No. That would be tempting, but I think what is expected is that you have to actually point out what specifically those purposes are.

    The student follows up: but what if all your data is helping you improve your model, or something like that?

    Ordinarily, I think that sort of purpose would be a valid business purpose for retaining the facial images, except that in this scenario, Emotie has committed itself to only using the images for that one purpose. So those other purposes are no longer available.

    The student asks what would happen if the contract said the images could be used for other purposes.

    If they changed the contract to extend the processing beyond just providing emotion detection services to, say, training the model, then they can keep the data for those purposes.

    一位学生问，是否只要说所有数据都对业务有帮助就够了。

    不行。这种说法很诱人，但我认为要求的是你必须具体指出这些目的到底是什么。

    这位学生追问：但如果你的所有数据都在帮助你改进模型之类的呢？

    通常来说，我认为这类目的会是保留面部图像的有效商业目的，只是在这个情境中，Emotie 已经承诺只将这些图像用于那一个目的。所以那些其他目的就不能再援引了。

    这位学生问，如果合同中写明图像可以用于其他目的会怎样。

    如果他们修改合同，把处理范围从单纯提供情绪识别服务扩展到比如训练模型，那么他们就可以为这些目的保留数据。

  - [20:31:04–20:31:57] **General Strategy for Retention Decisions**

    Okay, so I think we have comprehensively covered this. The general strategy for deciding whether you can retain personal data is to go back to these two steps. You first consider what the original purposes were, and sometimes you can stretch those original purposes to fit what you want to do with the data. If you cannot do that, then you must rely on the second limb: do you have any other legal or business purposes in mind? So that's how you address retention limitation.

    Any questions about this?

    好，我想我们已经比较全面地讨论了这个问题。判断你能否保留个人数据的一般策略，就是回到这两个步骤。你首先考虑原始目的是什么，有时你可以扩展这些原始目的，以契合你想对数据做的事情。如果做不到，那么你就必须依赖第二个要件：你是否有任何其他法律或商业目的？这就是处理保留限制的方法。

    对此有什么问题吗？

  - [20:31:57–20:33:21] **Q&A: Defining Purposes in Contracts with Data Intermediaries**

    A student asks a quick question. Since I framed the case study around processing "solely to provide emotion detection services", is it common in contracts to define this further, or to set out scenarios of what is excluded or included?

    I've seen these based on the guidelines and some of the cases, including some GDPR cases. I think this is a data intermediary situation. Emotie here is doing a very simple task on behalf of its clients, and in those situations, the recommendation is to carefully delineate the purposes for which the intermediary can process the personal data. That is part of the structure of the intermediary–controller contract, because the whole point of the intermediary is that it only processes personal data for the controller's purposes. That means the contract will specify exactly what the purposes are.

    一位学生快速问了一个问题。既然我把这个案例设定为"仅为提供情绪识别服务"而处理，那么在合同中进一步界定这一点，或者列出哪些情形被排除、哪些被包括，这种做法常见吗？

    我是根据指南和一些案例看到这类做法的，包括一些 GDPR 的案例。我认为这是一个数据中介（data intermediary）的情形。这里的 Emotie 是代表其客户做一项非常简单的工作，在这种情况下，建议是仔细界定中介可以为哪些目的处理个人数据。这是中介与控制者之间合同结构的一部分，因为中介的意义就在于它只为控制者的目的处理个人数据。这就意味着合同会具体规定这些目的究竟是什么。

  - [20:33:21–20:33:43] **Closing**

    Okay, so if there are no questions, then that's actually all I have for today. Today we looked at the secondary security obligations. In the next seminar, we will look at extraterritoriality, which means what happens in the cross-border scenario.

    好，如果没有问题的话，今天的内容其实就到这里了。今天我们看了辅助性的安全义务。下一次研讨课，我们将讨论域外效力（extraterritoriality），也就是跨境情形下会发生什么。
