# Seminar 8
## Week 8 Readings
- AGKCP at 19.1-19.11, 21.1-21.15.
- GDPIA.
## Week 8 Summary
- **Transfer Limitation Obligation (TLO): Rule and Rationale 转移限制义务：规则与理据**
  - **Rule:** When transferring personal data out of Singapore, the organisation must ensure it receives a standard of protection **comparable to the PDPA**.
  - **Test:** The organisation must take **appropriate steps** to ensure the overseas recipient is bound by **legally enforceable obligations** (or holds a **specified certification**), unless an **exception** applies.
  - **Rationale:** Closes the loophole of moving data **out of the regulator's reach** to evade the PDPA.
  - **Trigger point:** The TLO bites **the moment data leaves Singapore**, including when using online or cloud services hosted overseas.
  - **Foreign-to-foreign transfers:** Data that never passes through Singapore (e.g., collected and held overseas, then moved to another foreign country) is **not caught** by Singapore's TLO; the exporting country usually has its own similar rule.
  - **规则：** 将个人数据转移出新加坡时，机构必须确保数据获得与 **PDPA 相当** 的保护标准。
  - **检验标准：** 机构必须采取 **适当措施**，确保海外接收方受 **具有法律约束力的义务** 约束（或持有 **指定认证**），除非适用 **例外**。
  - **理据：** 堵住把数据转到 **监管机构鞭长莫及之处** 以规避 PDPA 的漏洞。
  - **触发时点：** 数据 **一离开新加坡** 即触发转移限制义务，包括使用托管在海外的在线或云服务。
  - **外国到外国的转移：** 从未经过新加坡的数据（如在海外收集并存放，再转到另一外国）**不受** 新加坡转移限制义务管辖；数据转出国通常有类似规则。
- **Mechanism 1 — "By Law" 机制一："通过法律"**
  - **Rule:** You may rely on the recipient country's data protection law if it provides comparable protection. The comparison is at the level of whether the foreign law imposes obligations **on par with the PDPA's** (e.g., a consent obligation); minor differences do not matter.
  - **Timing requirement:** The comparative analysis must be done **before** the transfer.
  - **NUInternational Singapore (2021):**
    - Facts: two Singapore subsidiaries sent staff and student data to their UK parent and a Malaysian sister company; both recipients suffered ransomware attacks.
    - Outcome: **breach** of the TLO.
    - Reason: UK law could possibly be comparable, but no analysis was done beforehand; the argument was raised only **after the fact**.
  - **Risks (class discussion):**
    - It is unclear **how deep** the analysis must be: pointing to provisions, or studying their wording.
    - It is an **open question** whether an AI-generated comparison would satisfy the PDPC.
    - There is a "tactical game": the PDPC may be reluctant to declare a foreign law non-comparable, since that would be diplomatically awkward. Relying on this is still a **risk**.
  - **If done properly:** Proper prior analysis means no breach, but the result stays **uncertain** because the PDPC may disagree with your conclusion. This is why most companies avoid this route.
  - **规则：** 若接收国的数据保护法提供相当保护，可依靠该法。比较的层面是外国法是否施加 **与 PDPA 相当** 的义务（如同意义务）；细微差别无关紧要。
  - **时间要求：** 比较分析必须在转移 **之前** 完成。
  - **NUInternational Singapore（2021）：**
    - 事实：两家新加坡子公司把员工和学生数据发给英国母公司及马来西亚姐妹公司，两个接收方都遭勒索软件攻击。
    - 结果：**违反** 转移限制义务。
    - 理由：英国法或许相当，但事先没做分析，只在 **事后** 才提出这一论点。
  - **风险（课堂讨论）：**
    - 分析要 **多深** 并不清楚：是指出条款即可，还是要研究条款措辞。
    - 用 AI 生成的比较能否让 PDPC 满意，仍是 **悬而未决的问题**。
    - 存在"策略博弈"：PDPC 可能不愿宣布某国法律不相当，因为外交上会很尴尬。但依靠这一点仍是 **冒险**。
  - **若做得妥当：** 事先妥善分析即不违规，但结果仍 **不确定**，因为 PDPC 可能不同意你的结论。这正是多数公司回避这条路的原因。
- **Mechanism 2 — "By Contract" 机制二："通过合同"**
  - **Rule:** The transferring organisation contractually binds the overseas recipient to comparable protection. This is the **most popular, simplest and most certain** route.
  - **Minimum content:** Impose obligations comparable to the PDPA's data protection obligations (*Cigna Europe (2019)*). If the recipient is only a **data intermediary**, impose only the **Protection, Retention Limitation and Data Breach Notification** obligations.
  - **Privity requirement:** The transferring organisation must be **a party to the contract**, because a non-party cannot **enforce** it.
  - **Toll Logistics (2022):**
    - Facts: four Singapore subsidiaries sent employee data to their Australian parent, Toll Holdings, under Corporate Services Agreements; Toll Holdings suffered a ransomware attack.
    - Outcome: **breach** of the TLO.
    - Reason: the CSAs contained **no data protection obligations**. A separate contract between Toll Holdings and its HR vendor did contain such obligations, but the subsidiaries were **not party** to it, so they could not rely on it.
  - **规则：** 转出机构通过合同约束海外接收方提供相当保护。这是 **最受欢迎、最简单、最确定** 的途径。
  - **最低内容：** 施加与 PDPA 各项数据保护义务相当的义务（*Cigna Europe（2019）*）。若接收方只是 **数据中介**，只需施加 **保护、保留限制及数据泄露通知** 义务。
  - **合同当事方要求：** 转出机构必须是 **合同当事方**，因为非当事方无法 **执行** 合同。
  - **Toll Logistics（2022）：**
    - 事实：四家新加坡子公司依据企业服务协议（CSAs）把员工数据发给澳大利亚母公司 Toll Holdings；Toll Holdings 遭勒索软件攻击。
    - 结果：**违反** 转移限制义务。
    - 理由：CSAs **没有任何数据保护义务**。Toll Holdings 与其人力资源供应商之间另有一份包含这类义务的合同，但子公司 **不是该合同当事方**，因此不能依靠它。
- **Mechanism 3 — Binding Corporate Rules (BCRs) 机制三：有约束力的公司规则**
  - **Concept:** A set of rules binding companies within **one corporate group**. It is an unusual instrument, seen mainly in data protection law.
  - **Use:** Group members bound by BCRs that impose PDPA-comparable obligations can transfer data to each other. For example, ABC can transfer to its overseas parent when both are bound by the same BCRs.
  - **Limit:** BCRs work only for transfers **within the same corporate group**.
  - **Transfer vs use:** BCRs and the TLO govern the **transfer** only. Whether the group may **use** the data for other business lines is a separate question for the **Consent Obligation**.
  - **概念：** 约束 **同一企业集团** 内各公司的一套规则。这是一种不寻常的工具，主要见于数据保护法。
  - **用途：** 受施加与 PDPA 相当义务的 BCRs 约束的集团成员之间可以互相转移数据。例如，ABC 与其海外母公司受同一套 BCRs 约束时，ABC 可向母公司转移数据。
  - **限制：** BCRs 只适用于 **同一企业集团内部** 的转移。
  - **转移与使用：** BCRs 和转移限制义务只管 **转移**。集团能否把数据 **用于** 其他业务线，是另一个问题，由 **同意义务** 处理。
- **Mechanism 4 — Specified Certifications (CBPR / PRP) 机制四：指定认证（CBPR / PRP）**
  - **How it works:** Businesses apply for certification through private-sector auditors. Certified businesses are **presumed** to meet the required standard, so data can be transferred to a certified overseas business once its certification is verified.
  - **Key distinction:**
    - **PRP** covers recipients acting only as a **data intermediary**.
    - **CBPR** is required when the recipient acts as a **data controller** in its own right.
  - **Examples:**
    - Alpha.com (Singapore) transferring to Air Bravo (Japan) for a joint promotion: Air Bravo is a controller, so CBPR certification suffices.
    - STU transferring to a US analytics firm acting as its intermediary: PRP certification suffices.
  - **Practicality:** Few organisations are certified, though some large ones are (e.g., Apple). Audits **cost money**, so certification mainly makes sense for regular, high-volume recipients of foreign data.
  - **运作方式：** 企业通过私营审计机构申请认证。获认证企业被 **推定** 达到所需标准，因此核实其认证后即可向该海外企业转移数据。
  - **关键区分：**
    - **PRP** 适用于仅以 **数据中介** 身份行事的接收方。
    - 接收方以独立 **数据控制者** 身份行事时，须持有 **CBPR**。
  - **例子：**
    - Alpha.com（新加坡）为联合推广向 Air Bravo（日本）转移数据：Air Bravo 是控制者，CBPR 认证即足够。
    - STU 向作为其中介的美国分析公司转移数据：PRP 认证即足够。
  - **实际情况：** 获认证的机构不多，但有一些大公司（如 Apple）。审计 **需要费用**，所以认证主要适合经常大量接收外国数据的机构。
- **Exceptions to the TLO 转移限制义务的例外**
  - **Consent to the transfer:** The individual consents specifically to the transfer **out of Singapore**, after being told how their data will be protected abroad. This is distinct from ordinary purpose consent.
    - Example: Cedric consents to GHI moving his data to an overseas cloud service.
    - Limit: hard to obtain at scale (thousands of individuals).
  - **Deemed consent by contractual necessity:** The transfer is reasonably necessary to conclude or perform a contract **between the organisation and the individual**.
    - Example: travel agency DEF sends Karen's details to overseas hotels to book her tour.
    - Follow-up Q&A: if the hotel later suffers a breach, the agency is **not responsible**, because the hotel is a separate company and not the agency's data intermediary.
  - **Vital or national interest:** For example, John's family doctor sends his records to an overseas hospital treating him after an accident. The organisation must also ensure the data is not used for any other purpose.
  - **Data in transit:** Data merely passing through Singapore (e.g., via a server here) with no one in Singapore accessing it.
  - **Publicly available data:** Data already publicly available in Singapore.
  - **对转移的同意：** 个人在获告知其数据在境外将如何受保护后，专门同意把数据转 **出新加坡**。这有别于一般的目的同意。
    - 例子：Cedric 同意 GHI 把他的数据迁至海外云服务。
    - 限制：难以大规模取得（成千上万人）。
  - **合同必要性下的视为同意：** 转移对订立或履行 **机构与个人之间** 的合同合理必要。
    - 例子：旅行社 DEF 把 Karen 的资料发给海外酒店，为她的旅行团订房。
    - 追问：若酒店之后发生泄露，旅行社 **无须负责**，因为酒店是独立公司，不是旅行社的数据中介。
  - **切身利益或国家利益：** 例如 John 的家庭医生把他的病历发给事故后为他治疗的海外医院。机构还须确保数据不被用于任何其他目的。
  - **传输中的数据：** 只是途经新加坡（如经过本地服务器）、新加坡无人访问的数据。
  - **公开可得的数据：** 在新加坡已公开可得的数据。
- **Cross-Border Rules Elsewhere (Non-examinable) 其他法域的跨境规则（不考）**
  - **India:** A **blacklist** model; transfers are allowed by default unless the government restricts a notified country.
  - **EU:** A **whitelist** model. Transfers are allowed with an **adequacy decision** (e.g., NZ, Canada), **appropriate safeguards** such as **standard contractual clauses**, or specific derogations such as explicit consent. The whitelist is very small.
  - **印度：** **黑名单** 模式；默认允许转移，除非政府限制向通知所列国家转移。
  - **欧盟：** **白名单** 模式。凭 **充分性认定**（如新西兰、加拿大）、**适当保障措施** 如 **标准合同条款**，或特定豁免如明确同意，才可转移。白名单非常短。
- **Extraterritorial Application of the PDPA PDPA 的域外适用**
  - **Starting point:** "Organisation" covers entities not formed or recognised under Singapore law and entities with no residence or business address in Singapore. So, in principle, foreign entities are covered.
  - **Limit (territoriality principle):** The PDPA applies only to **processing activities taking place in Singapore**.
  - **ST Engineering (2020):**
    - Facts: a Singapore company sent employee data to its US subsidiary, which suffered a ransomware attack.
    - Outcome: the PDPA did **not apply** to the US subsidiary, which processed nothing in Singapore. The PDPC deferred to US authorities and focused on the Singapore company's **transfer**.
  - **Practical reasons for the limit (class discussion):**
    - Overlapping claims by multiple states would create conflicting regimes; organisations reasonably expect local law to govern local processing.
    - Enforcement abroad is impractical: the PDPC cannot investigate in a foreign country or easily enforce there.
    - Exceptions to territoriality exist (e.g., drug consumption), but those are easy to enforce on return; data processing is not.
    - There is no single international data protection law, only general standards (OECD, APEC) that countries apply differently.
  - **All personal data protected:** The PDPA protects personal data **regardless of where the individual is**.
  - **Lovebonito (2022):**
    - Facts: a cyberattack compromised the data of 5,561 customers; the PDPC found a breach of the Protection Obligation and imposed a $29,000 penalty.
    - The organisation sought a reduction because only 4,474 of those affected were in Singapore.
    - Outcome: **no reduction**; the PDPA draws no distinction by the individual's location.
  - **Two rules to remember:** (1) only **processing in Singapore** is covered; (2) **all** personal data processed here is protected.
  - **起点：** "机构"涵盖非依新加坡法律成立或获承认的实体，以及在新加坡无居所或营业地址的实体。所以原则上外国实体也被涵盖。
  - **限制（属地原则）：** PDPA 只适用于 **发生在新加坡的处理活动**。
  - **ST Engineering（2020）：**
    - 事实：一家新加坡公司把员工数据发给其美国子公司，后者遭勒索软件攻击。
    - 结果：PDPA **不适用于** 美国子公司，因为它没有在新加坡进行任何处理。PDPC 交由美国当局处理，并聚焦于新加坡公司的 **转移** 行为。
  - **限制的实际原因（课堂讨论）：**
    - 多国同时主张管辖会造成相互冲突的制度；机构合理预期本地处理由本地法律规管。
    - 境外执法不切实际：PDPC 无法在外国调查，也难以在当地执行。
    - 属地原则存在例外（如吸毒），但那类情况在入境时容易执法；数据处理则不然。
    - 没有统一的国际数据保护法，只有各国以不同方式适用的一般标准（OECD、APEC）。
  - **保护所有个人数据：** 无论个人身在何处，PDPA 都保护其个人数据。
  - **Lovebonito（2022）：**
    - 事实：网络攻击导致 5,561 名客户的数据外泄；PDPC 认定违反保护义务，罚款 29,000 新元。
    - 机构以受影响者中只有 4,474 人在新加坡为由，请求减轻罚款。
    - 结果：**不予减轻**；PDPA 不按个人所在地作区分。
  - **两条要记住的规则：** (1) 只涵盖 **在新加坡进行的处理**；(2) 在此处理的 **所有** 个人数据都受保护。
- **GDPR Extraterritoriality and the SIA Example (Non-examinable) GDPR 的域外效力与新航例子（不考）**
  - **Art 3(1):** The GDPR applies to processing **in the context of the activities of an EU establishment**, even if the processing occurs outside the EU (e.g., in the US).
  - **Art 3(2):** The GDPR applies to controllers or processors with **no EU establishment** that **offer goods or services** to people in the EU or **monitor their behaviour** there.
  - **Art 3(3):** The GDPR applies where EU law applies **by public international law** (e.g., ships, embassies).
  - **SIA privacy policy:** It has jurisdiction-specific notices (e.g., South Korea, California, Australia), special transfer bases for EU/Swiss residents (contractual necessity, standard contractual clauses) and for Chinese residents, and region-specific data subject rights. This shows how cross-border and extraterritorial rules shape a multinational's policy.
  - **第 3(1) 条：** 在 **欧盟机构活动背景下** 进行的处理适用 GDPR，即使处理发生在欧盟境外（如美国）。
  - **第 3(2) 条：** **在欧盟无机构** 的控制者或处理者，若向欧盟境内的人 **提供商品或服务** 或在当地 **监控其行为**，也适用 GDPR。
  - **第 3(3) 条：** 依 **国际公法** 适用欧盟法律的场所（如船舶、大使馆）适用 GDPR。
  - **新航隐私政策：** 设有针对特定法域的通知（如韩国、加州、澳大利亚），为欧盟/瑞士居民（合同必要性、标准合同条款）及中国居民设定特别转移依据，并规定按地区区分的数据主体权利。这显示跨境与域外规则如何塑造跨国公司的政策。
- **In-Class Exercise: Plasco 课堂练习：Plasco**
  - **Scenario:** Plasco (a data intermediary of cosmetic clinics) sends patients' facial images to an Italian AI partner, which forwards poorly modified images to a US provider for manual editing. Plasco's Malaysian and Thai branches receive images from local clinics and send employee records to Singapore for storage.
  - **Who must comply:** Plasco as intermediary is not itself bound by the TLO; the **clinics, as controllers,** are, and will require Plasco to secure compliance.
  - **Exceptions:** None fit.
    - Contractual necessity fails because there is no contract between Plasco and the patients.
    - Consent would have to be obtained through the clinics, e.g., when the photos are taken.
    - Assume no certifications.
  - **Italy:** Arguably rely **by law**, since the Italian partner is bound by the GDPR, which is arguably comparable. A contract is the safer route.
  - **US:** A **direct contract between Plasco and the US provider** is needed. Plasco cannot rely on the Italian partner's contract because of the privity requirement. Every onward transfer in a chain must comply, though long chains are unlikely in practice.
  - **Three image streams:**
    - Singapore-collected images sent abroad: Singapore's TLO applies.
    - Malaysian- and Thai-collected images: **Malaysian and Thai** transfer rules apply.
  - **Employee records stored in Singapore:** Usual PDPA obligations apply to the Singapore processing (consent for collection, protection, retention limitation, etc.). The incoming transfers from Malaysia and Thailand are governed by **their** transfer rules, not Singapore's TLO.
  - **Clinic–Plasco contract:** It suffices to require Plasco to comply with the TLO when transferring data out of Singapore; Plasco then works out the specific steps.
  - **情景：** Plasco（整形诊所的数据中介）把病人面部图像发给意大利 AI 合作伙伴，后者把修改不佳的图像转给美国服务商人工修改。Plasco 的马来西亚和泰国分公司接收当地诊所的图像，并把员工记录发到新加坡存储。
  - **谁须合规：** Plasco 作为中介本身不受转移限制义务约束；**作为控制者的诊所** 受约束，并会要求 Plasco 确保合规。
  - **例外：** 均不适用。
    - 合同必要性不成立，因为 Plasco 与病人之间没有合同。
    - 同意须通过诊所取得，比如在拍照时。
    - 假设没有认证。
  - **意大利：** 可主张 **通过法律**，因为意大利合作伙伴受 GDPR 约束，而 GDPR 可说相当。合同是更稳妥的途径。
  - **美国：** 需要 **Plasco 与美国服务商直接签订合同**。基于合同当事方要求，Plasco 不能依靠意大利合作伙伴的合同。链条中每一次再转移都须合规，但实践中长链条不太可能出现。
  - **三组图像：**
    - 新加坡收集并发往境外的图像：适用新加坡转移限制义务。
    - 马来西亚和泰国收集的图像：适用 **马来西亚和泰国** 的转移规则。
  - **存于新加坡的员工记录：** 新加坡境内的处理适用通常的 PDPA 义务（收集须同意、保护、保留限制等）。从马来西亚和泰国转入的行为受 **当地** 转移规则管辖，而非新加坡转移限制义务。
  - **诊所与 Plasco 的合同：** 只需要求 Plasco 在把数据转出新加坡时遵守转移限制义务即可；具体步骤由 Plasco 安排。
- **Accountability Obligation: Overview and the DPO 问责义务：概述与数据保护官**
  - **Rule:** Organisations must be able to **demonstrate** compliance. This requires two things:
    - Appoint a **DPO**.
    - Develop and implement **data protection policies and practices**. This overlaps with the Protection Obligation but is broader.
  - **DPO designation:** One or more individuals responsible for PDPA compliance.
  - **Typical DPO tasks:** These are not defined in the PDPA, but usually include:
    - Liaising with senior management.
    - Developing policies.
    - Keeping a personal data inventory.
    - Training staff.
    - Conducting assessments.
    - Serving as the **point of contact** for complaints.
  - **Contact details:** The business contact of at least one DPO must be made available, conventionally an email address in the external privacy policy (e.g., NUS's data protection policy).
  - **Choosing the DPO:** The PDPA is silent, so the **reasonableness** standard applies. The DPO should be knowledgeable and **empowered**, and need not be an employee (outsourcing is allowed).
  - **M Stars Movers (2017):** The DPO should come from **senior management** or at least have a **direct line** to it, because the role involves company-wide decisions. This may not be realistic everywhere, and in very small outfits you simply pick the best-placed person.
  - **规则：** 机构必须能够 **证明** 自己合规。这要求两件事：
    - 任命 **数据保护官（DPO）**。
    - 制定并实施 **数据保护政策和做法**。这与保护义务有重叠，但范围更广。
  - **指定 DPO：** 一名或多名负责 PDPA 合规的个人。
  - **DPO 的常见职责：** PDPA 未作界定，但通常包括：
    - 与高级管理层联络。
    - 制定政策。
    - 维护个人数据清单。
    - 培训员工。
    - 进行评估。
    - 担任处理投诉的 **联络人**。
  - **联系方式：** 必须公开至少一名 DPO 的业务联系方式，惯常做法是写在对外隐私政策中（如 NUS 的数据保护政策）。
  - **选择 DPO：** PDPA 未作规定，因此适用 **合理性** 标准。DPO 应具备相关知识并 **获得授权**，且不必是雇员（可外包）。
  - **M Stars Movers（2017）：** DPO 应来自 **高级管理层**，或至少有 **直达** 管理层的沟通渠道，因为该职位涉及全公司层面的决定。这未必处处现实，很小的团队只需挑选最合适的人。
- **Data Protection Policies and Practices 数据保护政策和做法**
  - **Four requirements:**
    - Develop and implement policies and practices to meet PDPA obligations.
    - Set up a **complaints process**.
    - Provide **staff training** and communicate the policies.
    - Make the policies and the complaints process available **on request**.
  - **Habitat for Humanity (2018):** Policies must be **in writing**.
    - Facts: an admin staff member emailed the entire PDF of volunteer acknowledgement letters to all volunteers instead of splitting it.
    - Outcome: **breach** of the Accountability Obligation.
    - Reason: there was no written policy for sending these letters. Verbal instructions were insufficient, because staff need something to **refer to** and oral instructions may be passed down wrongly.
  - **Xbot (2019):** **External policies are not enough.**
    - Facts: Strata.sg disclosed property transaction data. This was not a Consent Obligation breach because the data was public. The organisation had only an external privacy policy.
    - Outcome: **breach** of the Accountability Obligation.
    - Reason: an external policy **informs individuals**, while an internal policy **guides employees**. Internal policies were required even for a two-person outfit (one director, one employee). The standard is strict.
  - **Spize Concepts (2019):** Policies must be **contextualised** to the organisation's activities.
    - Facts: not understanding its vendor Novadine's system, the organisation enabled a feature that let the public view other customers' order data.
    - Outcome: **breach** of the Accountability Obligation.
    - Reason: it needed internal policies specifically addressing Novadine's services as its data intermediary.
  - **四项要求：**
    - 制定并实施履行 PDPA 义务的政策和做法。
    - 建立 **投诉处理流程**。
    - 提供 **员工培训** 并传达政策。
    - 应要求 **公开** 政策和投诉流程。
  - **Habitat for Humanity（2018）：** 政策必须 **书面化**。
    - 事实：一名行政人员把包含所有志愿者感谢信的整份 PDF 发给全体志愿者，而没有拆分。
    - 结果：**违反** 问责义务。
    - 理由：寄发这类信件没有书面政策。口头指示不足，因为员工需要可以 **参照** 的东西，而口头指示可能被错误传达。
  - **Xbot（2019）：** **只有对外政策不够。**
    - 事实：Strata.sg 披露了物业交易数据。由于数据是公开的，这不违反同意义务。该机构只有一份对外隐私政策。
    - 结果：**违反** 问责义务。
    - 理由：对外政策用于 **告知个人**，内部政策用于 **指导员工**。即使是两人机构（一名董事、一名雇员）也须有内部政策。这个标准很严格。
  - **Spize Concepts（2019）：** 政策必须 **结合** 机构的具体活动。
    - 事实：机构不了解其供应商 Novadine 的系统，启用了一项让公众能查看其他顾客订单数据的功能。
    - 结果：**违反** 问责义务。
    - 理由：它需要专门针对 Novadine 作为其数据中介所提供服务的内部政策。
- **In-Class Exercise: Resupro External Notice 课堂练习：Resupro 对外通知**
  - **Scenario:** You are Levin, DPO of Resupro, a 5-person startup that converts job applicants' CVs into hiring companies' formats. Generate an external notice with the PDPC's free **DP Notice Generator**.
  - **Key contents of the generated notice:**
    - Data collected: name, contact details, nationality, employment and education information.
    - Purposes: providing the service, legal compliance, and transmitting to the hiring companies.
    - Timelines: e.g., 10 business days for withdrawal of consent and for access and correction requests.
    - Security measures already in place.
    - Standard clauses on accuracy, retention and transfer limitation.
    - DPO contact details.
  - **Takeaway:** AI can draft such notices too, but the generator is a PDPC-sanctioned tool. An external notice is essentially a public-facing statement.
  - **情景：** 你是 Levin，五人初创公司 Resupro 的 DPO；公司把求职者简历转换成招聘公司的格式。用 PDPC 免费的 **DP Notice Generator** 生成对外通知。
  - **生成通知的要点：**
    - 收集的数据：姓名、联系方式、国籍、工作和教育信息。
    - 目的：提供服务、遵守法律，以及传送给招聘公司。
    - 时限：如撤回同意、查阅和更正请求均为 10 个工作日。
    - 已采取的安全措施。
    - 关于准确性、保留和转移限制的标准条款。
    - DPO 联系方式。
  - **要点：** AI 也能起草这类通知，但生成器是 PDPC 认可的工具。对外通知本质上是面向公众的声明。
- **In-Class Exercise: Internal Policy for Bobby's Testimonials 课堂练习：为 Bobby 的推荐感言任务制定内部政策**
  - **Scenario:** Bobby (marketing) identifies successful applicants, requests video interviews, and edits them for the website. A checkbox captured consent to be contacted; a separate consent form limits use to showcasing success stories.
  - **Method:** Walk through Bobby's task **step by step**, identify the data protection issue at each step, and set out what he must do.
  - **Research stage:** Use non-public data only for applicants who **ticked the checkbox**; otherwise rely on public sources such as company websites.
  - **Contact stage:** Verify the checkbox consent, and state the purpose clearly in the outreach.
  - **Interview stage:**
    - Obtain a **signed consent form before** the interview. Consent to be contacted ≠ consent to be interviewed.
    - Ask only questions **relevant** to the success story; avoid unnecessary or sensitive data.
  - **Post-interview:**
    - Store the footage securely.
    - Use it **only** for showcasing, not other marketing.
    - **Delete the raw footage** once the edited version is published (retention limitation).
  - **Requests:** Have a process for **withdrawal of consent** and **access requests**, likely routed through the DPO, who instructs Bobby.
  - **Escalation:** "When in doubt, tell the DPO." An FAQ for common interviewee questions reduces the DPO's load.
  - **Scope choices:**
    - Organisation-wide matters (access controls, breach reporting) may sit in general employee rules rather than this activity-specific policy; where to put breach reporting is a choice.
    - Matters such as explaining edits to interviewees belong in the consent form or Bobby's practical workflow.
    - Intermediaries would need coverage if engaged.
  - **Lesson:** An internal policy is a **highly practical working document**; an external policy is a general public **brochure**.
  - **情景：** 负责市场营销的 Bobby 找出成功求职者，请求视频访谈，并剪辑后放上网站。勾选框取得了同意被联系；另一份同意书把用途限于展示成功故事。
  - **方法：** **逐步** 梳理 Bobby 的任务，找出每一步的数据保护问题，并写明他必须怎么做。
  - **研究阶段：** 非公开数据只能用于 **勾选了勾选框** 的申请人；否则依靠公司网站等公开来源。
  - **联系阶段：** 核实勾选框同意，并在联络时清楚说明目的。
  - **访谈阶段：**
    - 访谈 **之前** 取得 **签署的同意书**。同意被联系 ≠ 同意接受访谈。
    - 只问与成功故事 **相关** 的问题；避免不必要或敏感的数据。
  - **访谈之后：**
    - 安全存储录像。
    - **只** 用于展示，不作其他营销用途。
    - 剪辑版发布后 **删除原始录像**（保留限制）。
  - **请求：** 设立 **撤回同意** 和 **查阅请求** 的流程，可能经由 DPO 处理，再由 DPO 指示 Bobby。
  - **上报：** "如有疑问，告知 DPO。"针对受访者常见问题准备 FAQ，可减轻 DPO 的负担。
  - **范围选择：**
    - 全机构层面的事项（访问控制、泄露报告）可放在一般员工规则中，而非这份针对具体活动的政策；泄露报告放在哪里是一个选择。
    - 向受访者解释剪辑之类的事项属于同意书或 Bobby 的实际工作流程。
    - 若聘用数据中介，也需要相应规定。
  - **启示：** 内部政策是 **高度实用的工作文件**；对外政策是面向公众的一般性 **宣传册**。
## Week 8 Learning Tips
Anchor the Transfer Limitation Obligation in its purpose: it exists so the PDPA cannot be evaded by moving data offshore. With that in mind, every mechanism reads as a different way of making protection "follow" the data: foreign law, a contract, group rules, or a certification. When you meet a transfer scenario, ask first whether data is actually leaving Singapore. Data that moves between two foreign countries never triggers Singapore's TLO. Data flowing into Singapore from Malaysia or Thailand engages those countries' transfer rules, not ours.

透过转移限制义务的目的来理解它：它的存在是为了防止有人把数据转到境外来规避 PDPA。记住这一点，每种机制都可以理解为让保护"跟着"数据走的不同方式：外国法律、合同、集团规则或认证。遇到转移情景时，先问数据是否真的离开新加坡。在两个外国之间流动的数据从不触发新加坡的转移限制义务。从马来西亚或泰国流入新加坡的数据，适用的是那些国家的转移规则，而不是我们的。

A common pitfall is treating the outcome as the test. In NUInternational, the UK may well have had comparable law, yet the organisations still breached the TLO, because the test requires a prior comparative analysis, not a comparable law in the abstract. In Toll Logistics, protective contractual terms existed somewhere in the group, but the subsidiaries could not enforce them. Make a contrast table for these two cases with three columns: what the organisation relied on, why it failed, and what would have worked. Then add a row for the Plasco US transfer, where the same privity logic requires a direct Plasco–US contract.

一个常见误区是把结果当成检验标准。在 NUInternational 案中，英国法律很可能相当，但这些机构仍然违反了转移限制义务，因为检验标准要求事先做比较分析，而不只是抽象地存在相当的法律。在 Toll Logistics 案中，集团内某处确实有保护性的合同条款，但子公司无法执行它们。为这两个案例做一张对比表，分三栏：机构依靠了什么、为什么失败、怎样做才行得通。然后为 Plasco 的美国转移加一行，同样的合同当事方逻辑要求 Plasco 与美国方直接签约。

Keep the lecturer's qualifications intact rather than flattening them. "By law" is a legitimate route, but it carries real uncertainty: how deep the analysis must go, whether an AI-drafted comparison would be accepted, and whether the PDPC would agree with your conclusion. That uncertainty is exactly why contracts dominate in practice. Likewise, M Stars Movers' advice to appoint the DPO from senior management is a recommendation the lecturer described as possibly unrealistic, not an absolute rule. Exam answers that reproduce these nuances will read as far stronger than ones stating bright-line rules.

保留讲师的限定说法，而不要把它们简化。"通过法律"是正当途径，但存在真实的不确定性：分析要做多深、AI 起草的比较能否被接受、PDPC 是否会同意你的结论。正是这种不确定性使合同在实践中占主导地位。同样，M Stars Movers 案建议从高级管理层中任命 DPO，讲师形容这可能并不现实，它并非绝对规则。考试答案若能体现这些细微之处，会比只写一刀切规则的答案有力得多。

Do not over-apply each rule beyond its scope. The TLO governs transfer, while the Consent Obligation governs whether the recipient may use the data for a new purpose, such as another business line. Keep these two questions separate. Similarly, PRP certification covers only intermediary recipients, the contractual necessity exception requires a contract with the individual (which fails in Plasco), and the PDPA's territorial limit concerns where processing occurs, not where the individual lives (Lovebonito). Write one-line "facts / failing / lesson" cards for ST Engineering and Lovebonito side by side, so the two extraterritoriality rules stick together.

不要把每条规则用到其范围之外。转移限制义务管的是转移，而同意义务管的是接收方能否把数据用于新目的，比如另一条业务线。要把这两个问题分开。同样，PRP 认证只涵盖作为中介的接收方，合同必要性例外要求与个人之间存在合同（在 Plasco 案中不成立），而 PDPA 的属地限制看的是处理发生在哪里，而不是个人住在哪里（Lovebonito）。为 ST Engineering 和 Lovebonito 各写一张"事实 / 失误 / 教训"的单行卡片并排放在一起，让两条域外适用规则一起记牢。

Know what is in scope. The slides mark the Indian and EU transfer regimes, GDPR Article 3, and the SIA privacy policy walkthrough as non-examinable. Understand them as context for why multinationals' policies look the way they do, but spend your revision time on the PDPA mechanisms, the exceptions, and the Singapore cases.

清楚考试范围。幻灯片把印度和欧盟的转移制度、GDPR 第 3 条，以及新航隐私政策的讲解标为不考内容。把它们理解为背景，用来解释跨国公司的政策为何是那个样子，但复习时间要花在 PDPA 的各项机制、例外和新加坡案例上。

For the Accountability Obligation, resist treating it as box-ticking. Habitat for Humanity, Xbot, and Spize Concepts form a ladder of expectations. First, the policy must be written down. Second, it must be internal, guiding staff, not just a public notice, even for a two-person company. Third, it must be tailored to the specific activity and vendors involved. When you face a scenario question, test the organisation against each rung in that order.

对于问责义务，不要把它当成打勾清单。Habitat for Humanity、Xbot 和 Spize Concepts 构成一个逐级递进的期望阶梯。第一，政策必须写下来。第二，它必须是指导员工的内部政策，而不只是公开通知，即使是两人公司也一样。第三，它必须针对所涉的具体活动和供应商量身制定。遇到情景题时，按这个顺序逐级检验机构是否达标。

Finally, rehearse the Bobby exercise as a template for any internal-policy question. Walk the workflow chronologically: research, contact, interview, post-interview, requests, escalation. At each stage, name the obligation engaged (consent, purpose limitation, protection, retention, access and withdrawal) and the concrete instruction it produces. Then decide what belongs in this activity-specific policy and what belongs in organisation-wide rules, such as breach reporting and access controls. Practising this fixed structure will let you produce a complete, well-scoped answer quickly under exam conditions.

最后，把 Bobby 练习当作任何内部政策题的模板来演练。按时间顺序走一遍工作流程：研究、联系、访谈、访谈后、请求、上报。在每个阶段，指出所涉及的义务（同意、目的限制、保护、保留、查阅和撤回），以及它产生的具体指示。然后判断哪些内容属于这份针对具体活动的政策，哪些属于全机构层面的规则，比如泄露报告和访问控制。反复练习这个固定结构，能让你在考试条件下迅速写出完整且范围恰当的答案。
## Week 8 Lecture
- [18:35:00–18:37:25] **The Transfer Limitation Obligation: Nature and Rationale**

  The next obligation deals with transferring personal data outside of Singapore, and this is the so-called Transfer Limitation Obligation. So, what is the nature of this obligation? Whenever you are transferring personal data out of Singapore, you have to make sure that the personal data will receive a similar level of protection, even though it is outside of Singapore.

  The reason for this obligation is that, if there were no such obligation, organisations would simply be able to transfer personal data out of Singapore, where it is out of reach of the regulator, and that would defeat the protection given to the personal data. You cannot have this kind of loophole that allows organisations to evade the PDPA, and that is why there is this obligation.

  So what do organisations need to do to comply with this obligation? The answer is that you have to take appropriate steps to make sure that your recipient in the foreign country is bound by legally enforceable obligations. If this is Singapore, and this is overseas, and you are transferring personal data out of Singapore to some overseas recipient, you have to make sure that this recipient is bound by legally enforceable obligations to protect the personal data as if it were in Singapore.

  接下来这项义务涉及将个人数据转移到新加坡境外，也就是所谓的转移限制义务（Transfer Limitation Obligation）。那么这项义务的性质是什么？每当你把个人数据转移出新加坡时，你必须确保这些个人数据即使身处新加坡境外，也能获得类似水平的保护。

  设立这项义务的原因是：如果没有这项义务，机构就可以直接把个人数据转移出新加坡，转到监管机构鞭长莫及的地方，这样一来对个人数据的保护就被架空了。你不能允许存在这样一个让机构规避PDPA的漏洞，所以才有这项义务。

  那么机构需要做什么才能遵守这项义务？答案是，你必须采取适当措施，确保你在外国的接收方受到具有法律约束力的义务（legally enforceable obligations）的约束。假设这边是新加坡，那边是海外，你把个人数据从新加坡转移给某个海外接收方，你就必须确保这个接收方受到具有法律约束力的义务约束，要像数据仍在新加坡一样保护这些个人数据。

- [18:37:25–18:38:28] **Ways of Imposing Legally Enforceable Obligations: "By Law"**

  So how can you impose these legally enforceable obligations on the overseas recipient? There are several prescribed ways that you are allowed to do this. You could do this by relying on law. You can do this by relying on a contract.

  For example, you might expect that if you're transferring personal data to, say, the EU, the EU probably provides at least a comparable level of protection to Singapore. So that's the idea of legally imposing obligations by law.

  那么如何对海外接收方施加这些具有法律约束力的义务？法律规定了几种允许的方式。你可以依靠法律，也可以依靠合同。

  比如说，如果你把个人数据转移到欧盟，你大概会预期欧盟提供的保护水平至少与新加坡相当。这就是"通过法律"施加义务的思路。

- [18:38:44–18:40:09] **Q&A: How Detailed Must the Comparison Be?**

  A student says they are not familiar with the nuances of the two laws, but presumes there are things here and there that are slightly different, and asks whether you have to go and check everything, or whether it is on a general basis.

  So the question is basically, what is the level? How detailed do you have to be? Because, as you say, data protection laws can differ in minor ways. If the foreign country's data protection laws impose data protection obligations on par with what we have in Singapore, like the consent obligation and so on, that should be sufficient. So that would be the level of detail that you would need to look at.

  一位同学说自己不熟悉两部法律之间的细微差别，但推测它们在一些地方会略有不同，于是问：是需要把所有内容都逐一核查，还是只需从总体上判断？

  这个问题基本上是在问：需要做到什么程度？要多详细？因为正如你所说，各国数据保护法会在细节上有所不同。如果外国的数据保护法施加的数据保护义务与新加坡的相当，比如同意义务（consent obligation）等等，那就应该足够了。所以你需要看的就是这个层面的细节。

- [18:40:19–18:42:55] **Case: NUInternational Singapore — Analysis Must Precede the Transfer**

  One thing to bear in mind if you want to rely on this particular mechanism is that you have to conduct your analysis of whether the foreign data protection law actually provides a comparable level of protection before you transfer the personal data. The case illustrating this point is NUInternational Singapore.

  What happened here is you have two organisations in Singapore which had a UK parent company. These two companies in Singapore transferred personal data to their UK parent company, as well as to a Malaysian sister company. Subsequently, the UK parent company and the Malaysian sister company suffered a ransomware attack that affected this personal data.

  One of the questions the PDPC investigated was whether the organisations in Singapore complied with the Transfer Limitation Obligation when they transferred the personal data from Singapore to the other countries. When they were investigated, one of the arguments these two Singapore organisations made was: our parent company is bound by UK data protection law, and we think UK data protection law is at least comparable to the PDPA. That sounds like a possible argument, but the PDPC did not accept it and said they were still in breach of the Transfer Limitation Obligation.

  The reason is that, even though UK data protection law potentially does provide a comparable level of protection, the organisations didn't actually conduct this comparative analysis before they transferred the personal data. What they were doing is, they got caught, and then after the fact they tried to say, okay, our data protection law in the UK is equivalent. So the general point is that if you want to rely on law, you actually need to conduct this comparative analysis.

  Does anyone have any thoughts about what the risks are when you try to use this method? Let's say you do conduct this analysis.

  如果你想依靠这一机制，有一点要记住：你必须在转移个人数据之前，就对外国数据保护法是否真的提供相当水平的保护进行分析。说明这一点的案例是 NUInternational Singapore。

  案情是这样的：新加坡有两家机构，它们的母公司在英国。这两家新加坡公司把个人数据转移给了英国母公司，以及一家马来西亚的姐妹公司。之后，英国母公司和马来西亚姐妹公司都遭受了勒索软件攻击，波及了这些个人数据。

  PDPC调查的问题之一，是这两家新加坡机构在把个人数据从新加坡转移到其他国家时，是否遵守了转移限制义务。在被调查时，这两家新加坡机构提出的一个论点是：我们的母公司受英国数据保护法约束，而我们认为英国数据保护法至少与PDPA相当。这听起来似乎说得通，但PDPC没有接受这个论点，仍然认定它们违反了转移限制义务。

  理由是，尽管英国数据保护法可能确实提供相当水平的保护，但这两家机构在转移个人数据之前并没有真正进行这种比较分析。它们的做法是，被查到了之后，才事后声称：好吧，我们在英国的数据保护法是对等的。所以一般性的要点是：如果你想依靠法律，你必须真正进行这种比较分析。

  有没有人想过，使用这种方法有什么风险？假设你确实做了这种分析。

- [18:43:09–18:44:56] **Q&A: Online Services, Overseas Servers, and Foreign-to-Foreign Transfers**

  A student raises the situation with digital services today: for example, a company in the US transferring data to its Singapore office, where the server doesn't reside in Singapore but somewhere else. The transferred data may not go to the country where it is supposed to be used, but to a server residing elsewhere, so who is responsible?

  This is a problem or question whenever you are using online services as part of your business. The answer is: the moment the data leaves Singapore, you have to have complied with the Transfer Limitation Obligation.

  The student clarifies the scenario: a Singapore company collects data, but the data resides overseas. The company then opens another office in another country, but doesn't use a server in that country either, and ships the data from there.

  So it is from a foreign country to a foreign country. Then there's no need to comply with the Transfer Limitation Obligation in Singapore, because the data is not leaving Singapore to begin with. But the country from which you are transferring the data will usually have a similar rule.

  一位同学提出了现在数字服务的情况：比如美国的一家公司把数据转给它在新加坡的办公室，但服务器并不在新加坡，而是在别的地方。转移的数据可能并没有去到原本要使用它的国家，而是去了另一个地方的服务器，那么谁来负责？

  只要你在业务中使用在线服务，就会碰到这个问题。答案是：数据一离开新加坡，你就必须已经遵守了转移限制义务。

  这位同学进一步说明情景：一家新加坡公司收集数据，但数据存放在海外。之后公司在另一个国家开设办公室，但也不使用该国的服务器，而是从那里再把数据转出去。

  那就是从一个外国转到另一个外国。这种情况下就不需要遵守新加坡的转移限制义务，因为数据本来就没有离开新加坡。不过，数据转出的那个国家通常也会有类似的规定。

- [18:44:56–18:47:32] **Discussion: Risks of Relying on Foreign Law**

  Another student responds to my earlier question. They say that in this case what hinges is the analysis that the organisation didn't conduct, but the key thing is what would signify a pass for the analysis. It could be a paper exercise saying, okay, we've done the analysis and everything is comparable. That is easy to do, but they don't know how much legal effect it has. So what is key is what sort of work really needs to be done for the analysis before the result is authoritative.

  Yes, so that would be one of the main risks. Whenever you try to rely on this, you would need to conduct an analysis, but it's not clear how deep your analysis needs to be. For example, can you look at the GDPR, point to provisions and say, these provisions cover us? Or do you actually need to look at the provisions, study the wording, and see whether they are appropriate? And of course, if you need to do that kind of analysis, it's going to be a lot more complicated.

  Of course, I suppose you can just ask some AI, like Gemini, to do the analysis for you, and you can try to pass that off as your comparative assessment. But it is an open question whether that will be accepted by the PDPC if you just use AI to churn out the analysis, which I'm sure you can do.

  There is a sort of tactical game you can play, in the sense that you can churn out your analysis and predict that maybe the PDPC won't question it. The reason is that if the PDPC does question it, that leads to an embarrassing, or at least awkward, situation where the PDPC would have to say, actually, this country's data protection law is not comparable to Singapore's, which I'm guessing they are reluctant to do. So you could try to run this, but it's a bit risky. That would be taking a risk.

  另一位同学回应我之前的问题。他说，在这个案例中关键在于机构没有做的那份分析，但真正的要点是：什么样的分析才算"过关"？可能只是一纸文件，写着"我们做了分析，一切都相当"。这很容易做到，但不知道在法律上有多大效力。所以关键是，分析需要做到什么程度，结论才算具有权威性。

  对，这就是主要风险之一。每当你想依靠这种方式，你就需要做分析，但分析要做多深并不清楚。比如，你能不能看看GDPR，指出某些条款说"这些条款覆盖了我们"？还是你需要真正研究条款的措辞，看它们是否恰当？当然，如果需要做那种分析，就会复杂得多。

  当然，我想你也可以直接让某个AI，比如Gemini，帮你做分析，然后试图把它当作你的比较评估交上去。但如果你只是用AI批量生成分析——我相信这完全做得到——PDPC会不会接受，仍是一个悬而未决的问题。

  这里有一种策略性的博弈：你可以生成你的分析，并预判PDPC也许不会质疑它。原因是，如果PDPC真的质疑，就会陷入一种尴尬、至少很微妙的局面：PDPC必须说"其实这个国家的数据保护法与新加坡的不相当"，我猜它们不太愿意这样做。所以你可以试试这样操作，但有点冒险，那是在承担风险。

- [18:47:32–18:49:19] **"By Contract": The Most Common Mechanism**

  So if you don't want to take the risk, what most responsible organisations do these days is rely on the second mechanism, which is by contract. The way this works is that the transferring organisation in Singapore imposes a contract on the overseas recipient, requiring the overseas recipient to protect the personal data to a comparable level.

  This method is the most popular because it is very simple and also quite certain, in the sense that you're not really taking risks when you use it. What level of detail do you need? It's the same as by law. At the minimum, the contract needs to make sure that the transferring organisation is imposing the data protection obligations of the PDPA on the receiver.

  Of course, if it so happens that the receiver is merely a data intermediary, say the receiver is just storing your data, then you will know that a data intermediary only has to comply with a few of the data protection obligations, which are protection, retention limitation, and data breach notification. In which case, you only need to impose those specific obligations on the intermediary.

  Any questions about this?

  所以，如果你不想冒这个险，现在大多数负责任的机构会采用第二种机制，也就是通过合同。做法是：新加坡的转出机构通过合同约束海外接收方，要求其以相当的水平保护个人数据。

  这种方法最受欢迎，因为它非常简单，而且相当确定——使用这种方法时你其实没有承担什么风险。需要多详细？和"通过法律"一样。至少，合同需要确保转出机构把PDPA的数据保护义务施加给接收方。

  当然，如果接收方恰好只是一个数据中介（data intermediary），比如只是替你存储数据，那么你知道数据中介只需遵守少数几项数据保护义务，即保护义务（Protection Obligation）、保留限制义务（Retention Limitation Obligation）和数据泄露通知义务（Data Breach Notification Obligation）。这种情况下，你只需把这几项具体义务施加给该中介即可。

  对此有什么问题吗？

- [18:49:28–18:52:07] **Case: Toll Logistics — You Must Be a Party to the Contract**

  One requirement, if you are relying on a contract, is that you must be a party to the contract. It means you cannot rely on someone else's contract. This is illustrated by the case of Toll Logistics.

  Here, you have four companies in Singapore that are part of a big corporate group. The group is headed by Toll Holdings, which is in Australia. The parent company, Toll Holdings, undertook to do various corporate services for its subsidiaries, like finance and so on, I think, and it did this through a number of corporate services agreements. The four Singapore subsidiaries then transferred personal data to the parent company in Australia.

  Subsequently, the parent company suffered a cyber attack, and the PDPC again investigated whether these four subsidiaries complied with the Transfer Limitation Obligation when they transferred the personal data to Australia. Here, the PDPC found that they breached the Transfer Limitation Obligation. The reason is that when they looked at the contracts between the holding company and the subsidiaries, there were no data protection obligations contained inside those contracts. So the CSAs did not help.

  The PDPC also noted that there was actually a contract between the parent company and some other vendor which did contain certain data protection obligations. But the problem is that the subsidiaries were not party to this contract, and so this contract could not be relied upon. The reason is that if you are not a party to the contract, you cannot enforce the contract.

  So what the PDPC expects is not only that there is a contractual obligation on the overseas recipient, but that you must be able to enforce it, which is why you need to be party to the contract containing all these data protection obligations.

  如果你依靠合同，有一个要求：你必须是合同的一方当事人。也就是说，你不能依靠别人的合同。Toll Logistics 案说明了这一点。

  案中，新加坡有四家公司，同属一个大型企业集团，集团由位于澳大利亚的 Toll Holdings 领导。母公司 Toll Holdings 承诺为其子公司提供各种企业服务，比如财务等等，我记得是这样，并通过一系列企业服务协议（Corporate Services Agreements，CSAs）来实现。这四家新加坡子公司随后把个人数据转移给了澳大利亚的母公司。

  之后，母公司遭受网络攻击，PDPC再次调查这四家子公司在把个人数据转移到澳大利亚时是否遵守了转移限制义务。PDPC认定它们违反了转移限制义务。理由是，在审查控股公司与子公司之间的合同时，这些合同中根本没有任何数据保护义务。所以这些CSAs帮不上忙。

  PDPC还注意到，母公司与另一家供应商之间其实有一份合同，里面确实包含某些数据保护义务。但问题是，这些子公司并不是该合同的当事方，所以不能依靠这份合同。原因是，如果你不是合同当事方，你就无法执行该合同。

  因此PDPC的期望是：不仅要对海外接收方施加合同义务，而且你必须能够执行它，这就是为什么你需要成为包含这些数据保护义务的合同的当事方。

- [18:52:18–18:53:52] **"By Binding Corporate Rules"**

  So that was the by-contract mechanism. The third mechanism is binding corporate rules, which is a bit of a strange creature as far as law is concerned, and we only really see this in data protection law. Binding corporate rules are a set of rules that govern a bunch of companies within one corporate group.

  Often you have multinational companies with one headquarters and a lot of subsidiaries scattered all over the world. What can happen is that the whole group binds itself under the same set of binding corporate rules, which impose data protection obligations on the companies included.

  For example, let's say you have ABC, which wants to transfer personal data to its parent company overseas. The companies in the corporate group bind themselves to binding corporate rules that apply to both ABC and the parent company, and we assume that these binding corporate rules impose the obligations under the PDPA. Here, ABC can then transfer the personal data to its parent company. But this mechanism only works if you are part of the same corporate group.

  以上是"通过合同"的机制。第三种机制是有约束力的公司规则（binding corporate rules），从法律角度看这是个有点奇特的东西，基本上只在数据保护法中才会见到。有约束力的公司规则是一套约束同一企业集团内多家公司的规则。

  跨国公司通常有一个总部和散布全球的众多子公司。整个集团可以共同受同一套有约束力的公司规则约束，这些规则对集团内的公司施加数据保护义务。

  举例来说，假设有一家公司ABC，想把个人数据转移给它在海外的母公司。集团内的公司共同受一套同时适用于ABC和母公司的有约束力的公司规则约束，并且我们假设这些规则施加了PDPA规定的义务。在这种情况下，ABC就可以把个人数据转移给母公司。但这一机制只有在你属于同一企业集团时才适用。

- [18:53:52–18:55:15] **Q&A: Using Transferred Data Across Business Lines**

  A student asks: even if we can transfer the personal data, can the receiving company also use it for its own business? Multinational companies have many different business lines. When they collect a customer's data for one business line, it doesn't mean the customer has agreed to it being used in other businesses. So the data is transferred to the wider group, but that doesn't align with the terms and conditions under which the data was originally collected.

  I think that problem is not addressed by the Transfer Limitation Obligation. That problem is addressed by the consent obligation, which means the question would be: did the individual consent to that purpose? So there are two separate problems. Transferring is one problem, but actually using and disclosing the personal data is a separate problem, governed by the consent obligation.

  一位同学问：即使我们可以转移个人数据，接收的公司能不能把它用于自己的业务？跨国公司有很多不同的业务线。为某一条业务线收集客户数据，并不意味着客户同意这些数据被用于其他业务。所以数据被转到整个集团，但这和最初收集数据时的条款与条件并不一致。

  我认为这个问题不是由转移限制义务来处理的，而是由同意义务（Consent Obligation）处理，也就是说问题在于：个人是否同意了该目的？所以这是两个独立的问题。转移是一个问题，而实际使用和披露个人数据是另一个问题，由同意义务规管。

- [18:55:32–18:56:30] **Q&A: If the Comparative Analysis Is Done, Is There a Breach?**

  Another student asks about the "by law" mechanism: if the organisation did conclude that the UK is comparable, does that mean it is not breaking the rule?

  Yes, that is the big question. Let's say you did your job in conducting the analysis: would you be breaking the rule? Assuming you have done your analysis properly, then you will not be breaking it. But again, most companies don't want to do this, because it's uncertain how much you need to do, and even if you did it properly, it is uncertain whether your analysis was correct, because the PDPC can say, actually, I don't think this is comparable.

  另一位同学就"通过法律"的机制提问：如果机构确实得出英国法律与新加坡相当的结论，那是否意味着它没有违规？

  对，这正是关键问题。假设你认真做了分析，你是否违规？假设你的分析做得妥当，那你就没有违规。但同样，大多数公司不想这样做，因为不确定需要做到什么程度，而且即使做得妥当，也不确定你的分析是否正确，因为PDPC可以说"其实我认为这并不相当"。

- [18:56:49–19:00:05] **Specified Certifications: CBPR and PRP**

  So we talked about the different legally enforceable obligations that can be imposed: by law, by contract, by binding corporate rules. The last one, which is a bit uncommon, is this thing called a specified certification. The idea is that there are certain certification systems called the CBPR and the PRP. This is the logo for CBPR, and this is the logo for PRP.

  Businesses can apply for certification to be compliant under one or both of these certifications, and once you are certified, you are assumed to meet the standard of data protection in Singapore as well as in certain other countries. The implication is that the transferring organisation can transfer personal data to a foreign business that has been certified. Once there is proof that the receiving organisation is appropriately certified, the personal data can be transferred.

  The thing to note is that the two certifications allow different things. If the recipient just has the PRP certification, you can only transfer to that organisation with the PRP if you are doing so on the basis that it is a data intermediary. Whereas if you are transferring the personal data to the organisation in its capacity as a data controller in its own right, then the company needs the CBPR certification.

  To give one example, let's say you have Alpha.com, a travel website in Singapore, and they want to do a joint promotion with a Japanese airline called Air Bravo. Both companies know that certain personal data from Alpha would need to be transferred to Air Bravo. Here, if Air Bravo has the CBPR certification, Alpha can then transfer the personal data over to Air Bravo without any problems.

  You can see which companies have been certified if you go to this web page and look at the certified organisations. Not that many, honestly, but there are some big ones, like Apple. So some organisations decide they will take the extra step to get certified. And then this is just an example under the PRP, for a data intermediary.

  我们已经讲了可以施加的各种具有法律约束力的义务：通过法律、通过合同、通过有约束力的公司规则。最后一种比较少见，叫作指定认证（specified certification）。其思路是，存在某些认证体系，分别叫作CBPR和PRP。这是CBPR的标志，这是PRP的标志。

  企业可以申请认证，符合其中一种或两种认证的要求；一旦获得认证，就被推定为达到了新加坡以及某些其他国家的数据保护标准。这意味着转出机构可以把个人数据转移给已获认证的外国企业。只要有证据表明接收机构获得了适当的认证，个人数据就可以转移。

  需要注意的是，两种认证所允许的事情不同。如果接收方只有PRP认证，那么只有在它以数据中介身份接收数据时，你才能把数据转给它。而如果你是把个人数据转给以独立数据控制者（data controller）身份行事的机构，那么该公司就需要CBPR认证。

  举个例子，假设有一家新加坡旅游网站 Alpha.com，想和一家叫 Air Bravo 的日本航空公司做联合推广。两家公司都知道，Alpha 的某些个人数据需要转移给 Air Bravo。在这种情况下，如果 Air Bravo 拥有CBPR认证，Alpha 就可以毫无问题地把个人数据转过去。

  你可以到这个网页查看已认证机构的名单，看看哪些公司获得了认证。说实话数量不多，但有一些大公司，比如Apple。所以有些机构决定多走一步去获得认证。然后这是PRP下的一个例子，针对数据中介的情况。

- [19:00:05–19:00:54] **Q&A: Cost of Certification**

  A student asks whether the two certifications are very expensive.

  I'm not sure what the price is. The certification is done in partnership with various private-sector auditors. Those auditors will charge a particular price for conducting the audit, and they will certify that you have met the standards under the CBPR or the PRP. So there's a cost, and it may only make sense if you are the sort of company that is regularly going to receive a lot of data from foreign parties.

  一位同学问，这两种认证是否非常昂贵。

  我不确定具体价格。认证是与多家私营审计机构合作进行的。这些审计机构会为审计收取一定费用，并认证你达到了CBPR或PRP的标准。所以是有成本的，可能只有当你是那种经常从外国方接收大量数据的公司时，这样做才划算。

- [19:01:08–19:03:01] **Exceptions to the Transfer Limitation Obligation**

  So legally enforceable obligations are one possibility, and certification is the other. The third option, if you really need to, is that you can rely on certain exceptions. On this slide, you can see what the exceptions are.

  The first exception is if you manage to get consent from the individual to the transfer. But this is different from the consent under the consent obligation, because the consent here is to the transfer of personal data out of Singapore. So you need to specifically get consent for that, and inform the individual about how their personal data will be protected in the foreign country.

  The second exception is where the individual is deemed to have consented to the disclosure of personal data, where the transfer is reasonably necessary for concluding or performing a contract between the organisation and the individual. I'll give an example of this one.

  The next exception is where the transfer is necessary for the vital interests of the individual or in the national interest. The fourth exception deals with data in transit, meaning the data is simply passing through Singapore with no one in Singapore touching it. So if there's a server in Singapore and the data is simply going through it, with no one in Singapore accessing that personal data, then you can just let it pass through.

  The last one is where the personal data is already publicly available in Singapore. The assumption is that since it's already publicly available, there's no point trying to protect the personal data when it's being transferred.

  所以，具有法律约束力的义务是一种途径，认证是另一种。第三种选择，如果确实需要的话，是依靠某些例外。这张幻灯片列出了这些例外。

  第一个例外是你成功取得了个人对转移的同意。但这与同意义务下的同意不同，因为这里的同意是针对把个人数据转移出新加坡这件事。所以你需要专门就此取得同意，并告知个人其个人数据在外国将如何受到保护。

  第二个例外是：当转移对于订立或履行机构与个人之间的合同合理必要时，个人被视为已同意披露其个人数据。我稍后会举一个例子。

  下一个例外是：转移对于个人的切身利益（vital interests）或国家利益是必要的。第四个例外涉及传输中的数据（data in transit），即数据只是途经新加坡，新加坡境内没有任何人接触它。比如新加坡有一台服务器，数据只是从中经过，新加坡没有任何人访问这些个人数据，那就可以直接让它通过。

  最后一个例外是个人数据在新加坡已经公开可得。其假设是，既然数据已经公开，在转移时再去保护它也就没有意义了。

- [19:03:12–19:05:00] **Example: Contractual Necessity, and Q&A on a Hotel's Data Breach**

  I said I'd give an example for the contractual necessity exception. Let's say Karen purchases an overseas tour with some travel agency in Singapore. In order for the travel agency to make hotel reservations in some other country, they need to transfer her personal data to that hotel. Here, you can say that Karen is deemed to have consented to the disclosure of her personal data because it is necessary to perform the contract.

  A student asks: if there is then a breach at the hotel, say the hotel is in Malaysia and it suffers a data breach, is the travel agency responsible?

  No. The travel agency will have satisfied the Transfer Limitation Obligation, in my understanding. Then the question is whether it will be responsible for the hotel's breach, and the answer is no, unless the hotel is acting as the travel agency's data intermediary, which it is not. It is a separate company. So here, the travel agency will not be responsible for whatever happens at the hotel.

  我说过要给合同必要性例外举个例子。假设 Karen 向新加坡某家旅行社购买了一个海外旅行团。为了在另一个国家预订酒店，旅行社需要把她的个人数据转给那家酒店。在这里，可以说 Karen 被视为已同意披露其个人数据，因为这是履行合同所必需的。

  一位同学问：如果之后酒店发生泄露，比如酒店在马来西亚，遭遇了数据泄露，旅行社要负责吗？

  不用。按我的理解，旅行社已经履行了转移限制义务。那么问题在于它是否要为酒店的泄露负责，答案是否定的，除非酒店是作为旅行社的数据中介行事，而事实并非如此，它是一家独立的公司。所以在这里，酒店发生什么，旅行社都不需要负责。

- [19:05:20–19:06:35] **Examples: Consent and Vital Interests**

  So that's the contractual necessity exception. For the consent exception, here's the example. Let's say Cedric is a client of GHI. GHI says: we're going to shift to a cloud-based solution, so can we get your consent to move your data to the cloud? They also tell him, this is how your personal data will be protected in the countries where the data will be handled. Cedric says, yes, I agree to the transfer, and GHI can then transfer the personal data.

  So the consent exception is something you can rely upon, but, as you'd expect, it is not something you can rely upon at scale. You can do it for small numbers, but once you get thousands of people, it's a bit harder to get their consent for the transfer.

  I'm not going to go through this one in detail. This is just an example for vital interests, and essentially the example is: if a doctor in Singapore needs to transfer your personal data to a foreign hospital that is trying to save your life, then obviously the transfer can happen. Okay, so that's the Transfer Limitation Obligation.

  以上是合同必要性例外。关于同意例外，例子是这样的：假设 Cedric 是 GHI 的客户。GHI 说：我们将转用一个基于云的解决方案，能否取得你的同意，把你的数据迁移到云端？他们还告诉他，在处理数据的那些国家里，你的个人数据将如何受到保护。Cedric 说：好，我同意转移。于是 GHI 就可以转移这些个人数据。

  所以同意例外是可以依靠的，但正如你所预料的，它无法大规模地依靠。人数少的时候可以这样做，但一旦涉及成千上万人，要取得他们对转移的同意就困难得多了。

  这个我就不详细讲了。这只是切身利益例外的一个例子，基本意思是：如果新加坡的医生需要把你的个人数据转给一家正在设法挽救你生命的外国医院，那么显然可以转移。好，以上就是转移限制义务。

- [19:06:48–19:08:37] **Cross-Border Transfer Rules in Other Jurisdictions: India and the EU**

  As I mentioned, other foreign jurisdictions also have a similar rule, because they also want to make sure that their personal data will be protected when it is transferred out of their country. But the way this particular obligation is done can vary quite a lot.

  For example, if you look at the Indian version, the Indian data protection law says that the government may, by notification, restrict the transfer of personal data to such country or territory outside India as may be so notified. Meaning that by default you can go ahead, unless the government says you cannot. In other words, this is a blacklist system: if the country is not blacklisted, go ahead.

  The EU's approach is the opposite. What the EU says is that you can transfer out of the EU if there's an adequacy decision by the European Commission, or if you impose appropriate safeguards like standard contractual clauses, which are published by the European Commission, or under some very specific exemptions, like explicit consent.

  The way this works is that the European Commission can whitelist countries as adequate. For example, New Zealand is on the list, and Canada is on the list. So if you are a whitelisted country, personal data can be transferred from the EU to that country. If it is not on the whitelist, which is a very small whitelist, then you need to use other mechanisms, like standard contractual clauses.

  Okay, so that's the discussion on transfer limitation. Any questions before we move on?

  我之前提到，其他外国法域也有类似的规定，因为它们也希望确保个人数据在转出本国时受到保护。但这项义务的具体做法可能差别很大。

  比如印度的版本，印度数据保护法规定，政府可以通过通知，限制将个人数据转移到通知所列的印度境外国家或地区。也就是说，默认情况下你可以转移，除非政府说不行。换句话说，这是一种黑名单制度：只要该国不在黑名单上，就可以转移。

  欧盟的做法正好相反。欧盟规定，如果欧盟委员会作出了充分性认定（adequacy decision），或者你采取了适当的保障措施，比如欧盟委员会发布的标准合同条款（standard contractual clauses），或者符合某些非常特定的豁免情形，比如明确同意，你才可以把数据转出欧盟。

  其运作方式是，欧盟委员会可以把某些国家列入白名单，认定其保护充分。比如新西兰在名单上，加拿大也在名单上。所以如果你是白名单国家，个人数据就可以从欧盟转到该国。如果不在白名单上——这个白名单非常短——那就需要使用其他机制，比如标准合同条款。

  好，关于转移限制的讨论就到这里。继续之前有没有问题？

- [19:08:48–19:12:11] **Extraterritorial Application of the PDPA; Case: ST Engineering**

  We are now going to move on to a very closely related topic, which is extraterritorial application. The Transfer Limitation Obligation deals with your transfer of personal data out of Singapore. This topic deals with the question of to what extent the PDPA applies outside of Singapore. You cannot find this in the guidelines; you would need to look at international law and some of the cases to figure it out.

  In principle, the starting point is that the PDPA applies to entities outside of Singapore, because the definition of organisation in the PDPA includes any entity, whether or not it is formed or recognised under Singapore law, and whether or not it is resident or has a business address in Singapore. So even an entity in another country, in principle, could be covered. So it seems like there's no limit, right? But there is a limit, because the PDPA only applies if the data processing activity is taking place in Singapore. So it applies to any entity, including outside Singapore, but the processing activity must take place in Singapore.

  The case illustrating this is the ST Engineering case. Here, the organisation is a big Singapore engineering company with a network of subsidiaries around the world, including one in the US. The organisation transferred some employee data to its US subsidiary for a number of reasons, and then the US subsidiary suffered a cyber attack.

  The PDPC of course had to investigate whether the transfer complied with the Transfer Limitation Obligation, which they did. But as far as the US subsidiary was concerned, the PDPC said the PDPA does not apply to it, because it was not doing any processing activity in Singapore; all the processing was taking place in the US. So the PDPC said it would simply defer to the US law enforcement authorities' investigation into the US subsidiary. It is basically for the US to police activities taking place within the US.

  This is simply an application of the so-called territoriality principle in international law. We say that a state, like Singapore or the US, may assert jurisdiction over activities taking place within its territory.

  我们现在进入一个密切相关的话题：域外适用（extraterritorial application）。转移限制义务处理的是把个人数据转出新加坡的问题，而这个话题处理的是PDPA在多大程度上适用于新加坡境外。这一点在指南中找不到，需要参考国际法和一些案例才能弄清楚。

  原则上，起点是PDPA适用于新加坡境外的实体，因为PDPA中"机构"的定义包括任何实体，无论其是否依新加坡法律成立或获承认，也无论其是否居于新加坡或在新加坡有营业地址。所以即使是另一个国家的实体，原则上也可能被涵盖。这样看似乎没有限制，对吧？但其实是有限制的，因为PDPA只在数据处理活动发生在新加坡时才适用。所以它适用于任何实体，包括境外实体，但处理活动必须发生在新加坡。

  说明这一点的是 ST Engineering 案。该机构是一家大型新加坡工程公司，在全球拥有子公司网络，其中包括一家美国子公司。该机构出于多种原因把一些员工数据转给了美国子公司，随后美国子公司遭受了网络攻击。

  PDPC当然需要调查这次转移是否遵守了转移限制义务，它们也确实调查了。但就美国子公司而言，PDPC表示PDPA不适用于它，因为它在新加坡没有进行任何处理活动，所有处理都发生在美国。因此PDPC表示将直接交由美国执法机关对美国子公司进行调查。基本上，美国境内发生的活动由美国来监管。

  这只是国际法中所谓属地原则（territoriality principle）的应用。我们说，一个国家，比如新加坡或美国，可以对发生在其领土内的活动主张管辖权。

- [19:12:11–19:16:05] **Discussion: Practical Reasons for the Territorial Limit**

  One question you can think about: are there also practical reasons why the PDPA's scope is confined this way? Why does the PDPA only cover activities based in Singapore?

  A student says it would complicate things. Each country has its own set of laws and jurisdiction, so coming to an agreement between geographies would be a massive task.

  Yes, so that's one problem. Let's say an organisation is doing something in Korea. That organisation correctly assumes that Korean law applies to the processing it is doing within Korea. But it becomes a lot more complicated if other countries also reach out to that company to try to regulate it.

  Another student points to legal enforcement: you can't really go and fine entities in all the countries; that would be very impractical and difficult.

  Yes, so that is the other practical problem. You can try to enforce the PDPA in the US, but you're not going to be able to investigate, because you would have to send people to a foreign country to investigate, which the foreign country will probably not accept. And then you would need to actually enforce against the entity in a foreign forum. So practically, it is quite difficult to have extraterritorial reach, even if you wanted to do it.

  A student asks whether there are cases where the Singapore government says it can police things that happen outside Singapore.

  Yes, there are some exceptions to the territoriality principle, and most notably it relates to drug consumption. When it comes to drug consumption, the Singapore government doesn't really care where you do it; you come into Singapore, you go to jail. So that's one exception to the territoriality principle. Although you can see that in that situation, it is relatively easy to enforce. But for this type of data processing, the enforcement problem is much harder.

  Another student says every country seems to have its own version of the PDPA, and asks whether there is an international law that applies across countries in this area.

  There is not. There are certain international standards, like the ones I mentioned in the first class, the OECD and APEC. They set out general principles, but not every country follows them, and there are some important differences. Okay, so that's why the PDPA only deals with Singapore-based processing.

  大家可以思考一个问题：PDPA的适用范围这样受限，是否也有实际层面的原因？为什么PDPA只涵盖在新加坡进行的活动？

  一位同学说，否则会让事情变得复杂。每个国家都有自己的一套法律和管辖权，要在各地区之间达成一致将是一项巨大的任务。

  对，这是一个问题。假设一家机构在韩国开展业务，它理所当然地认为韩国法律适用于它在韩国境内进行的处理。但如果其他国家也伸手去监管这家公司，事情就会复杂得多。

  另一位同学提到执法问题：你不可能真的去所有国家罚款，那会非常不切实际、非常困难。

  对，这就是另一个实际问题。你可以尝试在美国执行PDPA，但你无法调查，因为你得派人到外国去调查，而对方国家很可能不会接受。然后你还需要在外国的法律体系中对该实体实际执行。所以在实践中，即使想要域外效力，也很难做到。

  一位同学问，是否有新加坡政府声称可以管辖发生在新加坡境外事情的情况。

  有的，属地原则有一些例外，最著名的与吸毒有关。说到吸毒，新加坡政府其实不在乎你在哪里吸，你一入境新加坡，就会坐牢。所以这是属地原则的一个例外。不过可以看到，那种情况下执法相对容易。但对于这类数据处理活动，执法问题要困难得多。

  另一位同学说，似乎每个国家都有自己版本的PDPA，并问在这个领域是否有适用于各国的国际法。

  没有。有一些国际标准，比如我在第一节课提到的OECD和APEC。它们规定了一般性原则，但并非每个国家都遵循，而且存在一些重要差异。好，这就是为什么PDPA只处理在新加坡进行的处理活动。

- [19:16:05–19:18:07] **The PDPA Protects Personal Data of Individuals Outside Singapore**

  That being said, the PDPA does apply to the personal data of individuals who are outside Singapore. It does not discriminate between the personal data of a Singaporean and the personal data of a person from elsewhere.

  The case illustrating this involves an e-commerce retailer that suffered a cyber attack compromising the personal data of 5,501 customers. The PDPC found that it was in breach of the Protection Obligation and fined the company. But in its representations to the PDPC, the organisation tried to argue that it should have a smaller financial penalty because only 444 of the affected individuals were in Singapore; the others were individuals from other countries.

  The PDPC simply rejected this argument and would not reduce the fine on the basis that the affected individuals were in another country, because the PDPA requires organisations to protect all personal data without drawing a distinction between who is in Singapore and who is not. And again, you can see why this makes sense: individuals travel to and from Singapore, so it's quite hard to discriminate between Singaporeans and non-Singaporeans because of how people move around.

  So the two basic rules are: the PDPA applies to processing in Singapore only, but it applies to all personal data, regardless of whether the individual is in Singapore.

  话虽如此，PDPA确实适用于身处新加坡境外的个人的个人数据。它不区分新加坡人的个人数据和来自其他地方的人的个人数据。

  说明这一点的案例涉及一家电商零售商，它遭遇网络攻击，5,501名客户的个人数据被泄露。PDPC认定其违反了保护义务并对公司处以罚款。但在向PDPC提交的陈述中，该机构试图主张应减轻罚款，因为受影响者中只有444人在新加坡，其余都是来自其他国家的个人。

  PDPC直接驳回了这一论点，不会因为受影响的个人身处他国而减轻罚款，因为PDPA要求机构保护所有个人数据，不区分谁在新加坡、谁不在。同样，你可以理解这样做的道理：人们会进出新加坡，由于人员流动，很难区分新加坡人和非新加坡人。

  所以两条基本规则是：PDPA只适用于在新加坡进行的处理，但它适用于所有个人数据，无论个人是否身在新加坡。

- [19:18:07–19:21:26] **The GDPR's Extraterritorial Reach (Article 3)**

  Again, extraterritorial application is where data protection rules can differ quite significantly, and the most notorious jurisdiction when it comes to extraterritorial application is the EU, which does try to reach out to organisations in other countries. So let me explain how this works. On the slide you can see the extraterritorial application rules of the GDPR.

  The first one, Article 3(1), says that the GDPR applies to the processing of personal data in the context of the activities of an establishment of a controller in the EU, regardless of whether the processing takes place in the Union. That's a lot of words, so let me use a diagram to explain it. Let's say you have an establishment, an office or company building, located inside the EU, and this establishment does certain activities. If you have personal data processing taking place in the US, by some other establishment in the US, then as long as the processing is in the context of, or related to, the activities of the EU establishment, the EU will say the GDPR applies to this processing, even though it's taking place in the US. So this is a very broad extension of the GDPR extraterritorially. This was already in the old version, before the GDPR.

  What the GDPR added as new was Article 3(2), which says that the GDPR also applies where the controller or processor is not established in the Union. So this applies to the situation where the data controller or processor has no EU establishment, so it's purely outside, but it is offering goods or services to data subjects in the EU. For example, a US company selling things to a person in Germany would be caught under the GDPR. Or it applies where you are monitoring the behaviour of data subjects in the EU, like tracking the activities of people who are there. All of these will be covered.

  The third one, Article 3(3), is a lighter rule that deals with situations where EU law applies by virtue of public international law. This applies to places like ships, diplomatic missions, embassies and so on.

  同样，域外适用是各地数据保护规则差异相当大的领域，而在域外适用方面最出名的法域是欧盟，它确实会试图把手伸向其他国家的机构。我来解释一下它是怎么运作的。幻灯片上是GDPR的域外适用规则。

  第一条，第3条第1款规定，GDPR适用于在欧盟境内控制者的某一机构（establishment）的活动背景下进行的个人数据处理，无论处理是否发生在欧盟境内。字很多，我用一张图来解释。假设你在欧盟境内有一个机构，比如一间办公室或公司大楼，这个机构开展某些活动。如果有个人数据处理发生在美国，由美国的另一个机构进行，那么只要该处理是在这个欧盟机构活动的背景下进行的、或与之相关，欧盟就会说GDPR适用于该处理，即使它发生在美国。所以这是GDPR非常宽泛的域外延伸。这一点在GDPR之前的旧版本中就已经有了。

  GDPR新增的是第3条第2款，规定即使控制者或处理者未在欧盟设立机构，GDPR也适用。所以它适用于数据控制者或处理者在欧盟没有任何机构、完全在境外的情况，但前提是它向欧盟的数据主体提供商品或服务。比如一家美国公司向德国的某人卖东西，就会被GDPR管辖。或者是你在监控欧盟数据主体的行为，比如追踪身处欧盟的人的活动。这些情况都会被涵盖。

  第三条，第3条第3款，是一条较轻的规则，处理依据国际公法而适用欧盟法律的情形。这适用于船舶、外交使团、大使馆等场所。

  So what is the whole impact of all this extraterritorial application? You can see this quite clearly if you look at a company that operates everywhere. Let me show you an example: the privacy policy of SIA, Singapore Airlines.

  The first thing you can observe is that the front of this policy has a lot of special caveats: for South Korean residents, see this notice; California, South Africa, Australia, the Philippines, and so on. So you see that there are special rules applied for people in different jurisdictions. This is simply the result of SIA needing to comply with the data protection laws of various countries.

  We can also observe Part 5. This is the whole policy, and Part 5 deals with the transfer of information overseas. What it says is: we will transfer your personal data to and from Singapore and other countries. So this explains how they transfer the personal data. And then you can see that this part says, if you are an EU or Swiss resident, when we transfer personal data outside of the EU or Switzerland, we will do this on the basis of contractual necessity or on the basis of standard contractual clauses. So you can see that SIA needs to have special provisions for transfers of personal data of EU residents. And similarly, for Chinese residents, there are also special provisions.

  Here you can see that SIA also needs to deal with specific rights of data subjects depending on where they are from. You have the general data subject rights, which apply across the board, but then you have special rights for different data subjects, whether in the EU, in China, or in Taiwan. So this is what a data protection policy of a multinational company might look like.

  Let's have a look at this question, which is a bit complicated because it involves multiple countries. Let's see how you apply the Transfer Limitation Obligation, as well as the rules on extraterritoriality.

  So here you have Plasco, a company that provides certain services to cosmetic surgery clinics.   In practice, Plasco transmits these facial images to its partner in Italy, which is the one actually providing the AI service that does the modification. Furthermore, if a facial image is not appropriately modified by the AI, the Italian partner then transfers the facial images to a US service provider for someone to do the modification manually.

  You also know that Plasco recently expanded to Malaysia and Thailand, and opened branch offices in those countries. The clinics there send their facial images to the local branches. But the HR functions are centralised in the Singapore office, and the local branches in Malaysia and Thailand send their employee records to the Singapore office for storage.

  So it's a bit complicated, but the first question is: how should Plasco comply with the Transfer Limitation Obligation in relation to the facial images? Just as a bit of a hint, you should note that Plasco here is a data intermediary of the clinics. Because Plasco is a data intermediary, Plasco itself doesn't need to comply with the Transfer Limitation Obligation, but of course the clinics, as data controllers, do need to comply. So the clinics will basically ask Plasco: how are you going to make sure we are compliant with the Transfer Limitation Obligation? That's the first question. The second question is: to what extent must Plasco comply with the PDPA in relation to the employee records?
